# @bitburner-agent/brain

The long-running brain process (architecture D1): the bridge to the game, the telemetry store, and from M4 phase B the planner. Since M2 ([v0.2.0](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/releases/tag/v0.2.0)) it runs the control channel and stores what the in-game Agent reports, and serves the Remote File API, over which it deploys the in-game code ([#91](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/91)) and backs up the game's save ([#92](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/92)). It also serves the local [dashboard](#the-dashboard) ([#93](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/93)), and, for Steam on Windows, [supervises](#supervision) the game itself ([#94](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/94)). Since M3 ([v0.3.0](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/releases/tag/v0.3.0)) it tells the Agent what to run, in one desired state: [the hack loop](#the-hack-loop) and its target ([#134](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/134)), its move to the next target without an income stall, with the `incomeStalled` alert ([#184](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/184)), and its hack threads per host, so it doesn't drain the target ([#187](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/187)); the purchases of [the hacking programs](#the-hacking-programs) and [home RAM](#home-ram) ([#136](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/136), [#138](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/138)); the owner's queue of [intents](#intents) ([#141](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/141)), the irreversible ones behind a gate with a save backup ([#147](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/147)), installs and destroys counted done across the reset ([#164](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/164), [#175](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/175)); [work](#work) ([#151](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/151), [#153](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/153), [#156](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/156), [#158](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/158)); and the state [safe mode](#safe-mode) keeps with the brain down ([#160](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/160)). The dashboard has their switches and forms. The simulator's suites test the `sim` and `singularity` backends (`test/effector-backends.test.ts`), and [the install](#the-install-suite) and [destroy](#the-destroy-suite) cycles; the `ui` backend is E2's ([`packages/e2`](../e2/README.md)). [The run report](#the-run-report), `npm run report` ([#185](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/185)), reads a live run back from the store. Since M4 phase A ([v1.0.0](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/releases/tag/v1.0.0), a playable BN1, [#191](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/191)), it plays BN1 on its own, its choices computed from the game's formulas ([owner decision 46](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md)): [the hacking ceiling](#the-hacking-ceiling), and what hacking earns against it ([#196](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/196)); [cloud servers](#cloud-servers) bought and upgraded by their payback ([#194](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/194)); [the batcher](#the-batcher), proven on the engine, run by the Agent and given its targets by the brain ([#204](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/204), [#205](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/205), [#206](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/206)); [the faction state](#the-faction-state) from each slow snapshot ([#197](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/197)); [the hacker factions' backdoors](#backdoors-for-the-hacker-factions) ([#195](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/195)); [joining factions and working for them](#factions-and-their-work) ([#203](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/203)), with [spare RAM shared](#sharing-spare-ram) for their reputation ([#224](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/224)); [buying augmentations and installing them](#augmentations-and-the-reset) ([#210](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/210)) on the legacy scripts' count rule ([#222](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/222)), with donations for reputation ([#225](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/225)); [the city factions' group, the travels for invitations and Daedalus's count](#invitations-and-daedaluss-count) ([#228](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/228)), with an install held for the count's last one ([#243](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/243)); and [Daedalus, The Red Pill and the World Daemon](#the-world-daemon), the destroy into BN1 again ([#215](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/215)). Each shows on the dashboard, with its alerts. The run report gained what a run of them needs: hacking against the ceilings (section 5, [#196](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/196)) and the batcher's health (section 6, [#206](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/206)), with its batches short of RAM, its moves and its orders dropped ([#232](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/232), [#236](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/236), [#246](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/246), [#253](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/253)); progress in each BitNode, and the augmentation cycles with what held each back (sections 2 and 2b, [#244](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/244)); a summary a line a cycle by default ([#245](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/245)); the reloads a destroy, an install or a deploy causes, not counted as gaps ([#240](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/240)); and a `WARN` for the batcher out of step after a page stall, which it recovered from by itself ([#241](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/241), owner decision 55). Run #3 destroyed BN1.1 and BN1.2 with it, and 1.0.0's exit run BN1.3, its report a PASS ([#276](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/276)).

## Running it

```
npm run brain
```

From the repo, in a terminal, until stopped (Ctrl+C). Nothing is installed (owner decision 30). `npm run brain` runs `src/start.ts`, which runs the brain (`src/main.ts`) as a child process and starts it again after a crash: after 5 s, then 10, 30 and 60 s in a crash loop, back to 5 s after a run of 5 minutes (`src/restart-on-crash.ts`). Ctrl+C reaches both, the brain shuts down cleanly, and nothing is restarted. After a reboot, run it again. [docs/setup.md](../../docs/setup.md) has the owner's steps, from a clone (`npm run bootstrap`) to a running skeleton. One brain per game.

- **On Windows, an E0 brain (the default) runs the game itself.** Within 30 s it launches Bitburner through Steam. If no Agent reports for about 5 minutes, it kills and relaunches the game ([Supervision](#supervision)). Pause supervision on the dashboard while you set the game up.
- **The data directory is the repo's `.data/`** (gitignored), so everything stays in the repo ([#122](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/122)). `--data-dir <dir>` or `BITBURNER_AGENT_DATA_DIR` picks another; a relative path is taken from the directory the command was run in. Tests always pass their own (with an E2 config): a brain started in `.data/` creates an E0 config, which on Windows launches Steam's game.
- **Logs** go to stdout, one line per event, each cut at 500 characters; fatal errors go to stderr, and the brain exits non-zero. The startup lines name the release, the deployment it deploys, the control channel's and the Remote File API's ports, where the save backups and the data backups go, the dashboard's address, whether it supervises the game, and whether the hack loop, the program buying, the home RAM upgrades, the cloud servers, the brain's faction work, and buying and installing augmentations are on. Each switch flipped on the dashboard is logged too ("the brain's faction work turned off from the dashboard", #203), and so is an income stall as it starts and as it ends (`incomeStalled`, #184), and the faction state unread, when its problem changes, and read again ("the faction state couldn't be read (ui): …", "the faction state is read again (ui)", #197), and the backdoors, joins and faction work ([backdoors](#backdoors-for-the-hacker-factions), [factions](#factions-and-their-work)).
- **At start it thins the store's old data only if `config.json` opts in** ([thinning](#thinning), #283): with `telemetryKeptWholeDays` set, fast and medium snapshots older than that many days before the newest snapshot are cut to one whole a minute, before its `brainStarted`, and logged ("store thinned before …"), once a [data backup](#the-data-backup) holds them (#284: one is taken first if none does). Without it (the default) it logs "store thinning at start: off …". A thinning that fails is logged and is the dashboard's `storeThinningFailed` alert, and the brain starts all the same.
- **It backs its data directory up** ([the data backup](#the-data-backup), #284): the store, `config.json` and the newest save backup of each environment, into `<data dir>/backups/<time>/`, at its start when the newest is a day old or there's none, then each day; the newest 3 kept. The store's copy is made in a worker thread, off the event loop. Each is logged ("data backup backups/… (at the brain's start): the store 412.3 MB, 1 save backup, in 1.7 s"); one that fails is logged, recorded and the dashboard's `dataBackupFailed` alert, and play goes on.
- **It holds a lock file while it runs,** `brain.lock` in its data directory, naming its process and, under `npm run brain`, the runner's (which lives on through a crash and the restart after it). It's claimed before the store is opened, written whole (a file of its own, renamed into place), and removed only on a clean stop: after a crash it stays, and the runner's process keeps it counting until the restart claims it again (#297's re-review). It's never taken from another brain whose process is alive (that one keeps it, and the log says so), and one the brain can't write is logged, never fatal. `npm run thin` refuses while either process is alive (#283's review).
- **At start it builds the in-game code** from its own checkout (`packages/ingame`, with esbuild from `npm ci`) into `<data dir>/ingame-build/`, rebuilt from scratch every start, and checks each file against the build's manifest. So it always deploys the code it was started from, with no separate build step. It also reads the pinned game's definition file, for the version check: the checkout needs the `vendor/bitburner-src` submodule (`npm run bootstrap` fetches it), or the brain stops at start, saying which file is missing.
- **Deploying:** whenever the game connects to the Remote File API (on load, and after a drop, by its own reconnection setting), the brain checks the game's version and deploys if `agent/current.txt` names another deployment ([the steps](../../docs/bridge/protocol.md#deploying)). The very first start of the Agent on a save is by hand: `run agent/boot.js` in the game's terminal; autoexec does it on every load after that (#95).

## The data directory

| File | What |
|---|---|
| `config.json` | Created with defaults on first run, then read |
| `telemetry.sqlite` | The store (below), with its WAL files |
| `ingame-build/` | The in-game code built at start, which the brain deploys |
| `saves/<environment>/` | The game's save backups ([below](#save-backups)) |
| `brain.lock` | The brain's process and its runner's (#283's review): left after a crash, removed on a clean stop |
| `telemetry.pre-thinning-<time>.sqlite` | A copy of the store `npm run thin` made before thinning it ([below](#thinning)); delete it when you no longer want the data |
| `backups/<time>/` | The data backups ([below](#the-data-backup), #284): each a copy of the store, `config.json` and the newest save backup of each environment, the newest 3 kept; elsewhere if `dataBackupDirectory` says |

`config.json`:

| Field | Default | What |
|---|---|---|
| `environment` | `E0` | Which environment this brain serves: `E0` Steam, `E2` a source build under test ([architecture §9.4](../../docs/architecture.md#94-environments)). A simulator's brain (`E1`) runs from code, with no config |
| `controlPort` | 12526 | The control channel ([docs/bridge/protocol.md](../../docs/bridge/protocol.md)) |
| `remoteFileApiPort` | 12525 | The Remote File API server: the game's RFA settings must name it (#91, #95) |
| `dashboardPort` | 12527 | The dashboard (#93) |
| `allowedOrigins` | `["file://"]` | `Origin` headers allowed to connect; `file://` is Steam's page |
| `token` | 32 random bytes, hex | The control channel's shared secret, generated once |
| `saveBackupIntervalMinutes` | 30 | How often the game's save is backed up, besides once on each RFA connection |
| `saveBackupsKept` | 96 | How many save backups to keep per environment, the newest: two days at 30 minutes |
| `steamAppId` | 1812820 | Bitburner's Steam app ID, which the [supervisor](#supervision) launches (E0 on Windows only) |
| `gameExecutable` | `bitburner.exe` | The game's executable, as tasklist shows it: a file name, no folder |
| `telemetryKeptWholeDays` | absent (not written) | Thinning at each start, opted into: the days before the newest snapshot the store keeps whole; older fast and medium snapshots are thinned, which can't be undone ([below](#thinning)). Absent or null: no thinning at the start (`npm run thin` by hand) |
| `dataBackupsKept` | absent (not written): 3 | How many [data backups](#the-data-backup) to keep, the newest; 0 for none (and then no thinning at the start). Owner decision 62 |
| `dataBackupIntervalHours` | absent (not written): 24 | Hours from the newest data backup to the next: at least 1, at most 596 (the longest a timer can wait). Owner decision 62 |
| `dataBackupDirectory` | absent (not written): `backups` in the data directory | Where the data backups go: a folder, relative to the data directory unless absolute. Nothing goes outside the data directory unless you set it (another disk keeps a copy if this one fails) |

A config that isn't valid stops the brain, naming the file and the problem:
- invalid JSON, or not an object;
- another environment;
- ports out of range, or two the same;
- no allowed origins;
- a token that isn't 64 hex digits;
- a backup interval that isn't a number of minutes above 0 and at most 35791 (the longest a timer can wait, about 24.8 days), or a count kept that isn't a whole number of at least 1;
- a Steam app ID that isn't a whole number of at least 1, or a game executable that isn't a bare `.exe` file name;
- days kept whole, when given, that aren't a whole number of at least 1;
- data backups kept, when given, that aren't a whole number of at least 0; an interval that isn't a number of hours from 1 to 596; or a backup folder that's empty or isn't text.

A config from before the backup and supervision fields existed is refused too, naming one missing field and its default at a time. Add them (`"saveBackupIntervalMinutes": 30, "saveBackupsKept": 96, "steamAppId": 1812820, "gameExecutable": "bitburner.exe"`), or start from a fresh data directory.

A port of 0 picks any free port, which is for tests only: the game's RFA settings can't name it, and a control port of 0 changes at every start, so every start would be a new deployment.

## The telemetry store

SQLite through Node's built-in `node:sqlite`, in WAL mode so a reader (the gap report's and the run report's commands) never blocks the brain's writes (architecture §7, §11).

| Table | Rows |
|---|---|
| `snapshots` | Every snapshot the control channel passes on: `environment`, `release`, `agent_session_id`, `tier`, `sequence_number`, `game_time_ms`, `received_at_ms`, `missed_before` (numbers skipped before it; after a brain restart, counted from the highest stored for its session and tier, #119), `data` (JSON) |
| `events` | Everything else the brain's parts report: the brain's own `brainStarted` (at each start), its thinning's `storeThinned` and `storeThinningFailed` (#283, [below](#thinning)), and its data backups' `dataBackup` and `dataBackupFailed` (#284, [below](#the-data-backup)); the channel's `agentConnected`, `agentDisconnected`, `connectionRejected`, `versionDrift`; the deployer's `deployed`, `deploymentCurrent`, `deployFailed`, `versionDrift`; the save backups' `saveBackup`, `saveBackupRotationFailed`; and the supervisor's `relaunch`, `supervisionGaveUp`, `supervisionFailed`. The game's dialogs the Agent handled, as `gameDialog` (#143), with their kind and text. A brain message the Agent refused, as `messageRefused` (#194's review), with its reason and count, stored once. A promotion the Agent took, as `workPromotion` (#155), with the company, the titles before and after, and the game's time, stored once (and logged). A slow snapshot's faction state unread, as `factionStateUnread` (#197), with the backend and the problem, each time the problem changes, and `factionStateRead`, with the backend, once it's read again (both logged). The brain's faction work (#203): each choice as `factionWorkChosen` (the faction, work type, target, reputation, requirement, rate and ETA) and its end as `factionWorkStopped` (`{ reason }`). Augmentations (#210): where the count rule stands each time it changes, as `augmentationProgress` (#222: the counts, the bars, the time online this cycle, when each bar falls next, the countdown and a hold); each install chosen, as `augmentationInstallChosen` (the set and its money, what's queued, the time online this cycle, the counts and bars, and why); each purchase, as `augmentationBought` (the augmentation, faction, level, price and the queue's length); a purchase never shown queued, as `augmentationNotQueued`; a donation shown in its faction's reputation, as `augmentationDonated` (#225: the faction, the amount, the reputation before and after), and one never shown, as `augmentationDonationFailed`; a cycle called off, as `augmentationCycleCalledOff` (`{ reason }`); an install going out, as `augmentationInstallSent` (the brain's or the owner's, and what's queued); and an install reported done with no reset in the next slow snapshot, as `augmentationInstallNotReset` (what's queued, #213 re-review). The World Daemon (#215): a step stalled, as `worldDaemonStalled` (the step, since when, and what's missing), and a destroy going out, as `bitNodeDestroySent` (the brain's or the owner's, the BitNode, and why it's a replay or null, #311); an install while short of Daedalus's count that added none to it with nothing that counts passed over, as `daedalusCountNeuroFluxOnly` (the count, A6's commit's facts and the line, #238). The levelling choice (#336, [below](#levelling-the-hacking-level-all-thats-left)): each change, as `levellingChosen` (the mode and the one before, each candidate's estimate, the target, batching's measured rate, the fitted factor, NeuroFlux Governor's levels on offer, and the line). The stop at the BitNode's end (#303, [below](#stopping-at-the-bitnodes-end)): the stop, as `brainStoppedAtBitNodeEnd` (the reason, `destroyed`, the BitNode and the next, whether the game closes first, and the line); the game's close going out, as `gameCloseSent`; and, from main.ts, `gameClosed` (when the close went out, and how long after it the Agent's connection dropped) or `gameCloseNotConfirmed` (why). A restart after a reset through the page that couldn't type `run /agent/boot.js`, as `restartFellBack` (#212), with the outcome (the fallback's reload, or given up), the reason and the page's time, from the next Agent's first fast snapshot (and logged). Columns: `environment`, `release`, `at_ms`, `kind`, `detail` (JSON) |
| `settings` | Switches kept across restarts: `supervision.paused` (the dashboard's switch), `supervision.gaveUpAtMs` (the supervisor's give-up, #94), `hackLoop.enabled` (the hack loop's switch, on when unset, #134), `work.desired` (the work the owner set on the dashboard, #151, #153, #156, #158), `programs.enabled` (buying the hacking programs, likewise, #136), `homeRam.enabled` (upgrading home's RAM, likewise, #138), `cloudServers.enabled` (buying cloud servers, likewise, #194), `factionWork.enabled` (the brain's faction work, likewise, #203), `augmentations.enabled` (buying and installing augmentations, likewise, #210), `augmentations.online` (the count rule's clock, the game's time seen online this cycle, keyed by its `lastAugReset`, #222), `augmentations.countHold` (the window of a hold for Daedalus's count: its cycle's `lastAugReset` and the time online at its start, #243), `augmentations.cycle` (the install chosen: the `lastAugReset` it was chosen in, and whether it's asked for, #210), `worldDaemon.enabled` (destroying the BitNode, on when unset, #215), `share.enabled` (sharing spare RAM, on when unset, #224), `stopAtBitNodeEnd` (what the brain does at the BitNode's end: `off` when unset, `brain` or `brainAndGame`, #303), `intents.manual` (the owner's manual intents queue, as `[{ action, args }]`, #141), and `intents.resetSent` (the note of a sent install or destroy, `{ id, field, resetAt }`, #175) |

- **Every snapshot and event is tagged** with its environment and its release, so releases and environments can be compared directly (§7). The release is:
  - the brain's `git describe` ("unknown" without git, which the startup log shows);
  - plus the deployment of the Agent connected when the row arrived, or `none` while no Agent is. A deployer or save-backup event's is the deployment the brain deploys.

  For example, `v0.2.0+3f2a1b…`.
- **Writes wait up to 5 s** for a reader's lock, so a reader never fails a write.
- **Timestamps** (`received_at_ms`, `at_ms`) come from the brain's clock: the system clock live, virtual time under the simulator.
- **Thinned only when asked** ([below](#thinning)): a day of snapshots is about 50,000 rows, and a 12-hour run's about 200 MB.

### Thinning

The campaign's months of data, kept to what its readers need of old runs ([#283](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/283), `src/store-thinning.ts`; [the spec](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/283#issuecomment-6711790410), and its review's changes). **Thinning can't be undone:** what a stub drops is gone, and keeping more days later doesn't bring it back. Only a backup has it (`npm run thin` makes one first; at the start, a [data backup](#the-data-backup) holds it, #284).

- **Off unless the owner opts in** (owner decision 60, #283): by default nothing is ever thinned, and the store keeps everything. `npm run thin` thins by hand. Set `"telemetryKeptWholeDays"` in `config.json` to thin at each brain start too, once a [data backup](#the-data-backup) holds what it cuts (#284). To keep everything, do nothing.
- **The rule:** fast and medium snapshots received more than `telemetryKeptWholeDays` (`npm run thin`: 14 unless said) before the **newest snapshot** are thinned; nothing else. The cutoff is on a minute. A brain started after a long pause thins nothing it hasn't already, and the dashboard's newest readings are never thinned. With the newest snapshot after now, a clock is wrong: nothing is thinned, and the line says why, so a clock set wrong can't thin real data. Before the cutoff:
  - every event, setting, static and slow snapshot, and the first fast and the first medium snapshot stored in each minute (the brain's clock), stay whole;
  - each other fast and medium snapshot keeps its row, its times (the brain's and the game's), session and sequence number, and its data becomes a stub (`TelemetryStore.thinSnapshots`): `{"thinned":true,"degradedTiers":[…],"pageHeapUsedBytes":…,"sentAtMs":…,"resetIntents":[…]}` for a fast one (`sentAtMs`: the Agent's send stamp, which tells whose a delivery lag was, #309; `resetIntents`: the ids of the install and destroy intents it held, which a gap's reload reads, #240), `{"thinned":true}` for a medium one (`isThinnedReading`). That's 29 in 30 fast and 5 in 6 medium snapshots, most of the store's bytes; a stub's row is about 250 bytes.
- **When:** from `npm run thin` (below), and, opted in, at the brain's start, before its `brainStarted` (never in a run's hot path).
  - **At the start, only once a data backup holds what it cuts** (#284, `backupBeforeThinning`): the newest backup counts if it's within `dataBackupIntervalHours` and was taken at or after the thinning's cutoff (everything it cuts was stored before it, and none of it thinned yet). Otherwise one is taken first, "(before the start's thinning)". With data backups off (`dataBackupsKept` 0), or the backup failing, nothing is thinned: logged as a failed thinning ("no data backup holds what it would cut (#284): …"), recorded and the `storeThinningFailed` alert. A thinning with nothing to cut asks for no backup. The copy that holds the cut data whole is rotated out with the rest, and it can be up to an interval old when the thinning trusts it, so a start's thinning can be undone from it for between `dataBackupsKept` less one and `dataBackupsKept` intervals: 2 to 3 days by default, and less than a day with 1 kept. `npm run thin`'s own copy is kept until you delete it. Each thinning starts where the last stopped (the newest `storeThinned` event's cutoff), so it reads only what has aged past it since; a first one over months of data reads them all once. It's one write transaction, all or nothing, and the write-ahead log is emptied after one that cut something.
- **What it says:** a line, "store thinned before … (the last 14 days before the newest snapshot kept whole; before, one fast and one medium snapshot a minute): 12345 fast and 2345 medium snapshots cut to their times, their data 180.2 MB → 3.4 MB, in 4.1 s", or "store thinning: nothing to thin before …" (a write-ahead log it couldn't empty after the commit is added to the line, "its write-ahead log not emptied: …": the thinning stands); and, if it cut anything, a `storeThinned` event (the cutoff, where it started, the days kept, the counts by tier, the bytes before and after, the time it took). A thinning at the start that fails is logged ("store thinning FAILED, the store left as it was: …"), recorded (`storeThinningFailed`) and the dashboard's `storeThinningFailed` alert, and the brain starts all the same: losing the store never breaks play. The run report's section 1 lists both events: in its window, and from just before it, since the start's come just before its `brainStarted` ("(before the window)").
- **Never touched:** the save backups (files beside the store), the settings, the events, another environment's snapshots.
- **Its readers:**
  - the run report's time checks (the heartbeat's gaps, clock steps, page stalls and delivery lags, the page heap, the run's end, a gap's reload) read every row, stubs included, so they're exact over a thinned window; its content checks read the whole snapshots, one a minute there ([the run report](#the-run-report)). A thinning's events are never the run's last record;
  - `npm run gaps` reads the times and the page heaps, which the stubs keep: exact;
  - the dashboard and the brain read only recent data and the settings;
  - the fast model's calibration (the medium and slow snapshots at 5, 10, 15 … min after each install, the events, the fast snapshots' `batcher.sharing`): a reading at a mark is within 60 s of the unthinned one (10 s before), `batcher.sharing` is read a minute at a time, and the slow snapshots and the events are exact. A read-only extract skips stubs with `substr(data, 1, 11) <> '{"thinned":'`; a test runs that extract over a thinned store.
- **The file:** SQLite reuses the pages thinning frees, so the file stops growing past its high-water mark, but doesn't shrink. The brain never compacts it; `npm run thin -- --vacuum` does, on demand.

```
npm run thin -- [--data-dir <dir>] [--environment E0] [--kept-whole-days <days>] [--dry-run] [--no-backup] [--vacuum]
```

- **Refused while the brain runs,** every time: its lock file names a live process (the brain, or its runner between a crash and the restart), or something answers on `config.json`'s dashboard port. A thinning holds the store's write lock for its whole run, longer than the brain's writes wait (5 s). A lock left by a killed brain with no runner names dead processes, so it doesn't count; one naming a process id the system has since given to another program is taken as running, and the message names the file to delete. `--vacuum` checks again just before its swap, and if the brain has started meanwhile it removes its copy and leaves the store as it is.
- **The days:** `--kept-whole-days`, else `config.json`'s `telemetryKeptWholeDays`, else 14; the environment `config.json`'s unless `--environment`. It reads `config.json` without creating one.
- **`--dry-run`** prints what it would cut, by tier, and the bytes before and after, and changes nothing.
- **A backup first:** before a thinning that cuts anything, it writes a compacted copy of the store, `telemetry.pre-thinning-<time>.sqlite` in the data directory (SQLite's `VACUUM INTO`), and prints its path and size; `--no-backup` skips it. A copy that fails part-way (the disk full, say) is removed, never left looking good. It takes seconds a GB.
- **`--vacuum`** then compacts the store: a compacted copy written beside it (`telemetry.sqlite.compacted`; a stale one from a stopped run is removed first, and a partial one on failure), then swapped in, so nothing goes to the system's temp folder. It takes seconds to minutes at GBs, and prints the size before and after.
- **Free space:** the backup is about the store's size less its free pages, and kept; `--vacuum`'s copy is the thinned store's size, while it runs. So the peak is about twice the store's size (the store and its backup), a little more with `--vacuum`.
- **Exits** 0 when done (thinned, nothing to thin, or a dry run), 2 when it couldn't run: no store, a bad flag, a `config.json` that isn't valid, or the brain running.

**The owner's calls** (#283; owner decision 60): off unless opted in, and at the start only once a data backup holds what it cuts (#284); 14 days kept whole by `npm run thin` (two weeks of 24/7 runs is several GB whole); one whole snapshot a minute, the other rows kept as stubs; no automatic VACUUM.

## Save backups

The brain backs up the game's save over the Remote File API (`getSaveFile`; architecture §6). The backups are insurance against a bug or a corrupted save (game issue #2809 is open on 3.0.1), and real starting states for the simulator.

- **When:** once on each RFA connection (the game connects on load and after a drop), and every `saveBackupIntervalMinutes` from the brain's start, over the newest open connection.
  - Before an irreversible intent goes out ([intents](#intents)), on the desired state's asking (`backupNow`).
  - One at a time: a backup asked for while one is under way follows it (several such become one).
  - With no open connection (none yet, or the last one closed), the interval's backup fails and is recorded, so a stretch with no backups shows.
  - The backups share the RFA connection with the deployer, each on its own client (request ids are unique per connection).
- **What:** the game's save at that moment, in the format Steam writes to `bitburnerSave_*.json.gz` (gzip bytes). It's a fresh save, not a copy of Steam's file. The brain checks it is what the game's own Import Game accepts before keeping it:
  - the game sent it as bytes (`binary: true`). A page without CompressionStream sends text instead, which isn't Steam's;
  - every character is a byte;
  - it gunzips into valid UTF-8, then JSON, then a `BitburnerSaveObject` with a player (`data.PlayerSave`).
- **Where:** `<data dir>/saves/<environment>/<UTC time>.json.gz`, for example `saves/E0/2026-10-27T03-30-00.000Z.json.gz` (`-` for `:`, which Windows doesn't allow).
  - Each environment has its own folder, so a simulator's or source build's backup (`E1`, `E2`) never sits beside Steam's (`E0`).
  - Each is written under a temporary name, then renamed. A half-written backup is only ever a `<name>.partial` file: it's removed when the write fails, and by the next rotation after a crash.
  - Two backups in the same millisecond: the second replaces the first.
- **Rotation:** after each backup the brain keeps the newest `saveBackupsKept` in that environment's folder and deletes the rest.
  - Only files named as backups count; anything else in the folder is left alone.
  - The backup just written is never deleted, even if the clock went back and its name sorts first.
  - An old backup that can't be deleted (in use, say) is reported as a `saveBackupRotationFailed` event, and the rest are still rotated.
- **Recorded:** every backup is a `saveBackup` event in the store, with the file (relative to the data directory) and its size, or with the failure. A failed or damaged backup is never kept. Failures include:
  - no open connection;
  - no reply in 30 s;
  - a save that isn't valid;
  - a disk error.

  Anything else is a bug, and stops the brain (fail fast).
- **Size:** the RFA server takes frames up to 128 MiB. A save reply is about twice the gzip save's size, so saves up to about 60 MB gzipped come through. A larger one would close the connection.
- **Restoring one:**
  1. **First, stop the brain, or copy the backup you want out of `saves/`.** A running brain keeps backing up whatever the game holds, on every reconnect and every interval, and rotation deletes the oldest. After a bad bug, or a corruption that still loads, that would push the good backups out.
  2. In the game: Options → Import Game, pick the `.json.gz`, then confirm. The game replaces the current save and reloads. It accepts these gzip files as they are (`src/SaveObject.ts:338-355`).
  3. When the brain runs again, the reload's RFA connection backs up the restored save.
  - **Only `E0` backups may ever go into Steam.** Saves flow one way: nothing from the simulator or a source build goes back (architecture §9.4).
  - Backups are personal data. They live in the data directory, by default the repo's gitignored `.data/`; never commit one, and keep `.data/` ignored.
- **In the simulator:** a backup loads as a save fixture as it is (`{ kind: "save", path }`), to the same state as the game's own save at that moment (`test/save-backup-round-trip.test.ts`).
- **A backup before an irreversible intent** (R8, #147): `backupNow()` takes one at once and resolves with its own event, saved or failed. Asked while one is under way, it waits for the next, so the event is never from a backup that began before the asking. The desired state's gate asks for it ([intents](#intents)).

## The data backup

What the brain can't rebuild from the game, copied so a bug, a bad thinning or a damaged disk can't lose it: the store (the campaign's history and the fast model's calibration data), `config.json`, and the newest save backup of each environment ([#284](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/284), `src/data-backup.ts`). Losing the store never breaks play (architecture §7), so a backup never does either.

- **What one holds:** a folder, `<backup directory>/<UTC time>/` (`backups/` in the data directory unless `dataBackupDirectory` says), with `telemetry.sqlite`, `config.json` (with the control channel's token: keep backups as private as `.data/`), `saves/<environment>/<newest>.json.gz`, and `backup.json`, the marker naming the store it's of: its id (kept in the store's settings, `dataBackups.storeId`) and its path. A backup is this store's only if both match. A restored store keeps both; a copied data directory's store has the same id but another path, so the two are told apart (#298's re-review). The full rotation of save backups stays in `saves/`: a data backup carries the newest of each, the one you'd restore.
- **The store's copy** is SQLite's `VACUUM INTO` on a read-only connection of its own, in a worker thread (`src/data-backup-worker.ts`), never a file copy: one read transaction, so the copy is the store at one moment however the brain writes meanwhile (WAL: its writes don't wait on a reader), and compacted (no free pages). Then the copy is opened on its own and `PRAGMA quick_check`ed, the restore check: a copy that fails it isn't kept.
- **Off the event loop** (#284's measurement, `DATA_BACKUP_LARGE_STORE_MB=450` on `test/data-backup.test.ts`): a 486 MB store, written every millisecond meanwhile, was copied (454 MB) and checked in 1.7 s, with the event loop's longest delay 65 ms and 1,288 writes made during it. The same copy on the brain's own thread blocks the event loop for its whole length: 0.9 s for a 426 MB store on this PC's SSD, more on a slower disk, and longer with the check. On the brain's own connection (SQLite's online backup, `node:sqlite`'s `backup`) the brain's writes waited up to 77 ms for each step's lock.
- **When:**
  - at the brain's start, after its `brainStarted`, if one is due: none yet, or the newest older than `dataBackupIntervalHours` (24). So a crash loop's restarts don't take one each, rotating the good ones out. A backup named after now (a clock was wrong) isn't the newest: it would put the next off for as long;
  - then each time one is due, from the newest;
  - before a thinning at the start that no backup holds yet ([thinning](#thinning));
  - after a failure, again in an hour (or at the interval, if that's sooner).
  One at a time: one asked for while another is under way follows it. The due time is a timer's: a PC asleep puts a backup off (on Linux the timer's clock stops while suspended), never drops it. A store's copy that takes longer than 30 minutes (`COPY_TIMEOUT_MS`; a 486 MB store's takes 1.7 s) is a failure, so a stuck one never holds up the next, or a thinning at the start. Its worker is told to stop, but a `VACUUM INTO` already running in SQLite runs on until it ends or the brain stops, its read snapshot open and the store's write-ahead log growing meanwhile.
- **Rotation:** after each backup, this store's newest `dataBackupsKept` (3) are kept and older ones removed. Only folders named as backups with this store's marker count: anything else in the folder is left alone, so two data directories (a copy of one included) can share a backup folder without touching each other's backups. One named after now counts as the oldest: after a clock set forward and then corrected, the backups it named in the future are the first rotated out, deliberately, as the real ones are newer.
- **Never removed, so yours to delete by hand:** a folder with no marker, or one that can't be read; a partial one whose marker was never written (a crash in the moment after its folder was made); and another store's backups, a replaced store's or a moved data directory's included. The backup just written is never removed, even if the clock went back. A backup you copy back into the folder under its own name is rotated like the rest: to keep one, copy it elsewhere or rename it. Removing or renaming is tried again a few times if Windows says the file is busy (antivirus, the indexer).
- **Partial ones:** each is written as `<time>.<store id>.partial/` and renamed when it's whole and checked. A partial one is removed when it fails, and one of this store's left by a crash or a stop is removed before the next backup measures the free space. That removal is best effort: one that can't be removed (Windows, or a worker still copying after a timeout) is said in the event (`rotationFailure`, the `dataBackupFailed` alert) or, if the free space then falls short, in the failure, and the free space it takes is counted as it stands.
- **Free space:** before each, the backup's disk must keep at least 1 GB free (`MIN_FREE_BYTES_AFTER_BACKUP`) after the store, its log, `config.json` and the saves, or it fails, saying how much it needs and how much is free.
- **Disk cost:** each backup is about the store's size once compacted (a 12-hour run adds about 200 MB), so the backups take about three times the store, four times while a new one is written before the oldest goes. A 2 GB store: about 6 GB of backups, 8 GB at the peak. A thinned store keeps them smaller.
- **Recorded and logged:** each backup is a `dataBackup` event (its reason: `start`, `interval` or `thinning`; its folder, relative to the data directory when inside it; the store's copy's bytes; the saves copied; the time it took; the backups removed; and a rotation failure, if any) and a log line; a failure is a `dataBackupFailed` event (the reason and why) and a line, "data backup FAILED (at the interval): …". The dashboard's `dataBackupFailed` alert shows the newest backup's problem, a failure or older ones not removed, until one is taken cleanly or the brain restarts. The run report's section 1 lists both events and counts them, and the one a start's thinning waited for (or its failure) "(before the window)", never an earlier run's; a failed backup isn't the run's failure.
- **Stopping:** a backup under way when the brain stops is cut short with the process and records nothing; the next backup removes its partial folder.
- **Restoring the store** (or `config.json`, or a save) from a backup:
  1. Stop the brain (Ctrl+C), so nothing writes the store, and check it's stopped: `.data/brain.lock` is gone (or names no running process), and the dashboard doesn't answer.
  2. Keep the current one, its log with it: rename `.data/telemetry.sqlite`, `.data/telemetry.sqlite-wal` and `.data/telemetry.sqlite-shm` (those two if they're there) together, to `telemetry.before-restore.sqlite`, `telemetry.before-restore.sqlite-wal` and `telemetry.before-restore.sqlite-shm`. Never delete the log: after a crash it holds data not yet in the main file, and renamed with it, the kept store still opens whole. With none left, no old log can be read into the restored file.
  3. Copy `backups/<time>/telemetry.sqlite` (or your `dataBackupDirectory`'s) to `.data/telemetry.sqlite`.
  4. To restore `config.json` too, copy the backup's over `.data/config.json`.
  5. Move the backups newer than the one you restored out of the backup folder (keep them elsewhere, or delete them): the restored store is the same store to them, so the next start would take none while one is newer than the interval, and a thinning would trust a copy from before the restore.
  6. Start the brain. What was stored after the backup's time is in the renamed file, not the restored one.
  A save in a backup restores like any [save backup](#save-backups). To look at a backup first, the reports read its store as it is: `npm run report -- --data-dir .data/backups/<time>`.
- **Before a thinning at the start** (`backupBeforeThinning`): see [thinning](#thinning).

**The owner's calls** (#284; owner decision 62): 3 kept, one a day from the newest (at least an hour apart), at the start only when due, in `.data/backups/` unless configured; at least 1 GB left free; the newest save of each environment, not every save. `"dataBackupsKept": 0` turns them off, which turns thinning at the start off too. 3 kept a day apart give 2 to 3 days to notice a problem `quick_check` can't see (bad rows), on the store's own disk by default (#298's review; 2 were first proposed, which gave 1 to 2 days).

## The dashboard

A local page showing whether the skeleton is healthy, its alerts, and its switches: supervision, the hack loop, the batcher (#205; on by default since #206, owner decision 49), buying the hacking programs, upgrading home's RAM, the brain's faction work, buying and installing augmentations (#210), and destroying the BitNode (#215; [#93](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/93); architecture §7, §10.2). Open **http://127.0.0.1:12527/** (`dashboardPort`) while the brain runs.

- **What it shows,** refreshed every 2 s from the store:
  - **Alerts:** the ones holding now (below).
  - **Agent:** connected or not, and since when, as of this run of the brain; its deployment and session; the game's version, and whether it's the pinned one.
  - **Last snapshot per tier:** when each tier last arrived, and how long ago.
  - **Game:** from the latest fast and medium snapshots: money, hacking level and intelligence beside it (0 before BN5 or SF5; #349), home RAM used of its total, scripts on home, the page's heap, and the tiers the Agent is skipping (`degradedTiers`); and the hacking ceiling and hacking's income over the last 10 minutes (#196, [the hacking ceiling](#the-hacking-ceiling)).
  - **Gaps in the last 24 h:** the gap report over the fast snapshots, from 24 h before now: how many gaps and their total time, with the newest 20 listed. Any time without snapshots in the window counts: from the window's start to the first snapshot with a new store, or while the brain or the Agent was down.
  - **Supervision:** off, running, paused, or gave up; the supervisor's recent relaunches; and the switch (hidden when off).
  - **Hack loop:** on or off; on, whether the brain is waiting for the Agent's snapshots, has no target yet (while servers are being rooted), or runs it against a target on so many servers, and the next target it prepares on so many more ("on, against n00dles, on 21 servers, hacking with at most 92 threads; preparing zer0 on 7 servers, hacking with at most 48 threads", #184, #187: the hosts' hack threads added up, "at most" as a host's loop may have fewer threads than its RAM holds); and its switch ([the hack loop](#the-hack-loop)).
  - **Batcher** (#205): on or off; on, what was sent (where it runs, the target, the hosts, the share and the spacing) or why nothing, and its health; red with a desync or a problem ([the batcher](#the-batcher)). Its share threads, their RAM and bonus, while it shares (#224).
  - **Sharing spare RAM** (#224): on or off, whether the batcher is asked to share or why not, its last report, and the button ("Stop sharing spare RAM", `POST /api/share`) ([sharing spare RAM](#sharing-spare-ram)).
  - **The BitNode's end** (#303): what the brain does there, in words, and a button for each of the other two settings ("Play on", "Stop the brain", "Stop the brain and close the game", `POST /api/stop-at-bitnode-end` with `{"setting": "off" | "brain" | "brainAndGame"}`) ([stopping at the BitNode's end](#stopping-at-the-bitnodes-end)).
  - **Hacking programs:** on or off; on, the item being bought, or what it waits for (the Agent's snapshots, money for the next item, travel, or a cheaper home RAM upgrade first); what's owned; and its switch ([the hacking programs](#the-hacking-programs)).
  - **Home RAM:** on or off; on, the size the upgrade being bought brings home to, or what it waits for (the Agent's snapshots, money for the next size, travel, a cheaper program first, or the BitNode's price), or that home is at its limit; home's RAM; and its switch ([home RAM](#home-ram)).
  - **Cloud servers** (#194): on or off; on, the server being bought or upgraded, its RAM, price and payback ("buying pserv-2 with 256 GB for $14,080,000, paid back in 26 min"), or what it waits for (the Agent's snapshots, the BitNode's prices, the hack loop's income over 10 minutes, an income that pays back in time, money beyond what's held for a program or home RAM, a program or home RAM first, the hack loop switched off, or you, after a purchase that went wrong), or that all are at the most RAM; the servers owned with their RAM; and its switch ([cloud servers](#cloud-servers)).
  - **World Daemon** (#215, [the World Daemon](#the-world-daemon)): destroying the BitNode on or off; the brain's destroy asked for, or yours queued instead; the path in the brain's words (Daedalus's requirements, The Red Pill's reputation and ETA, w0r1d_d43m0n's root and hacking requirement with the ETA); a stall in red; and its switch.
  - **Augmentations** (#210): on or off; what the brain asks for once an install is chosen ("install chosen: buying BitWire from CyberSec for $10,000,000"); or what it waits for: the Agent's snapshots, a reset (after an install reported done with no reset), The Red Pill's way, the install's countdown (its time left), an install held for Daedalus's count (#243: the last augmentation and its ETA), or the count to reach a bar; the set the money covers and its money; where the count rule stands (#222): the counts affordable, new and with NeuroFlux, the time online this cycle, each bar and when it falls next, the countdown (quick or not, its time left, its extensions) and a hold; each purchase not queued, in red; and its switch ([augmentations and the reset](#augmentations-and-the-reset)).
  - **Intents:** each intent sent, in order, with its source (the manual queue, a purchase, a backdoor, #195, a join, #203, a travel for an invitation, `travel`, #228, an augmentation's purchase or install, #210, or the World Daemon's destroy, `worldDaemon`, #215, or the close of the game at the BitNode's end, `bitNodeEnd`, #303) and how it went in the game: not reported yet, done, or its tries and last problem. An irreversible one the gate holds, or one queued after an install, says why instead ("not sent: waiting for a save backup", #147, #164). A manual one has a Cancel button, and a form queues a new one: an action and its arguments as JSON ([intents](#intents)).
  - **Work** (#151, #153, #156, #158): the work the brain manages, whose it is ("Yours: …", or "The brain's: …" for [its faction work](#factions-and-their-work), #203), focused or not, and how the Agent last reported it (with the latest promotion it took, #155), or "Not managed"; a form sets faction work (a faction and a work type), another company work (a company and a job field, Noodle Bar and Waiter to start with), a third a crime, a fourth a class (a university or gym, Rothman University to start with, and a course or stat), and **Stop managing work** clears it; while the brain's own faction work is showing, that button reads **Stop the brain's faction work** and turns that off instead (#203 review), since clearing your work would only hand it back to the brain.
  - **Factions** (#197): from the newest slow snapshot, each joined faction's reputation and favour, the invitations and the queued augmentations (NeuroFlux Governor's levels counted, "NeuroFlux Governor ×2"), when they were read and through which backend; or why they couldn't be read, in red ([the faction state](#the-faction-state)). Above them, the brain's own (#203): its faction work's choice and why ("hacking for CyberSec, for Neurotrainer I (reputation 2 of 1,000, 0.32/s: about 52 min)"), or "not sent (your work on the dashboard); it would be …", or what it waits for, or "off"; the faction being joined; the city group the brain chose and why (#228, owner decision 54: "City factions: group B (Chongqing, New Tokyo, Ishima): 5 augmentations that count, against A 4, C 3"), and each other city faction's invitation it passes over, with what joining it would ban; and the faction work's switch ([factions and their work](#factions-and-their-work)).
  - **BitNodes and installs** (#285): a timeline of the campaign, every BitNode the store shows, the newest first, each one a line you can open (the newest opens at the first drawing that lists any, and one you open stays open, a "Reading…" drawing or a brain restart between included).
    - **Each BitNode's line:** its name (number and level, "BN1.4 #2" when played again), its start ("before the store", or "a save loaded"), how it ended (destroyed, at the cap, a save loaded, a new game, b1t_flum3, a destroy sent, or "not ended yet": [the campaign report's endings](#the-campaign-report)), its time online and by the clock ("so far" if not destroyed; "≥" where its start wasn't seen), and its cycles and installs (by hand among them). Opened: the time online left out, the kinds of augmentation installed at its last look and NeuroFlux Governor's level ("4 kinds, NeuroFlux Governor at level 2"), the saves loaded in it, and its releases.
    - **Then its cycles, one row each:** its number ("cycle 2"; "cycle ≥ 2" where an install came before the BitNode's first look), or **"save"** for a cycle a save loaded from a cycle never seen (not an install); when it began; its time online ("≥" for the first where the BitNode's start or an install before its first look went unseen); and how it ended, any of: each install that ended it (when, by hand or not, and the augmentations it put in, NeuroFlux Governor once a level: "installed …: 3 augmentations (NeuroFlux Governor ×2, Synaptic Enhancement Implant)"), **"left for a save loaded …"** where a save took the game to another cycle, in time order, and then for the BitNode's current cycle (at its last look, which after a save loaded isn't always the last listed) **"going on"** ("going on, the destroy sent" after a destroy sent with the next BitNode not seen, whose times run "so far"), or **"ended with the BitNode"**. A row with none of these says **"no install seen"**. A cycle's time online is the steps to its slow snapshots, the step across an install split at the install's `lastAugReset`, so a BitNode's cycles add up to its time online. An install's augmentations are those `ownedAugs` gained from the last look before it to the first after.
    - **It's the campaign report's model** (`src/campaign-model.ts`, shared with `npm run campaign`: the same BitNodes, starts, endings, times and counts, worked out once on its `BitNodeView`, which a test checks against the report's rows, a BitNode entered by a save loaded among them), **kept up to date** by `CampaignTimeline` (`src/dashboard/timeline.ts`) rather than read again: the page asks every 2 s, and the store holds about 525,000 slow snapshots a year. Each slow snapshot's `resetInfo` alone (`json_extract` in the query: the rest of its payload is never parsed) is folded once, and the events of the campaign's three kinds appended once, so a poll reads only what was stored since the last: about 0.1 ms with nothing new, 0.4 ms with a slow snapshot, on a year's synthetic store. The view is built again only when something was folded or appended (a dirty flag, so a read failing part way still shows what it folded).
    - **The first read** (after each brain start, when the page first asks) takes the store as it was then, in the report's order (by arrival), 20,000 slow snapshots each time the page asks (`TIMELINE_BATCH`), so it never holds the brain up for long: a year's store in 27 polls, the longest about 140 ms, under a minute. Until it's done the panel says how far it's got ("Reading the store: 40,000 slow snapshots so far."), and shows no BitNode. After it, the slow snapshots stored since are folded in the order stored, at most 20,000 a poll: by the row id's range while 10,000 rows of any tier or fewer were stored since, by the slow tier's index past that (the page closed for days: a week's 10,080 slow snapshots in one poll of about 110 ms). One edge: the brain's clock set back while it runs, where the report's order by arrival differs from the order stored; the two agree again from the brain's next start.
    - **It works on a thinned store** (#283): it reads no medium or fast snapshot, as the report doesn't; a test thins a campaign with #283's own `thinStore` and gets the same timeline.
    - **A failure** to read it is shown in the panel in red ("Couldn't read the timeline: …"), with what was read before (and never "no BitNode in the store", which isn't known then), and logged ("dashboard timeline: …") once each time its message changes, never failing the rest of the status. A slow snapshot the model can't fold is skipped and counted, never read again, in red too ("1 slow snapshot couldn't be folded, left out or partly applied, so the timeline may be off from there (the first: row …: …; npm run campaign fails on it)"), the first logged: a fold failing part way may keep part of the row. After a read short of a full batch, the next goes on from the newest row as it began, not the last slow snapshot, so with slow snapshots stopped (or another environment writing) the rows since stay few. Two failures aren't recovered from, neither seen in practice: a slow snapshot SQLite can't parse as JSON fails every read at that row (and `npm run campaign`), and the tier index's absence fails `INDEXED BY` (`TelemetryStore.open` creates it on every start).
    - **The cost measured:** `TIMELINE_SLOW_SNAPSHOTS=525600 npx vitest run packages/brain/test/campaign-timeline.test.ts -t "a large store" --reporter=verbose` builds a year's store of slow snapshots with a real one's payload and logs the first read, an idle poll, a poll with one slow snapshot, `npm run campaign`'s report over it, and the poll after a week away (CI runs it on 3,000).
    - **Tests:** `test/campaign-timeline.test.ts` builds a campaign (BN1.1 begun before the store with an install before its first look, BN1.2 with an install by hand and an offline gap, BN1.3 with a save loaded within it and installed from again, BN1.4 going on) and checks each BitNode and cycle (which is current, the saves that left each), the report's own rows against it (with a BitNode entered by a save loaded), a BitNode going on or destroyed in a cycle a save went back to, the brain's install event stored after the install's slow snapshot, the reads after the first (a slow snapshot read once; a piecemeal store the same as one read at once; a batch a poll), the batched first read, a read failing part way, a slow snapshot the fold fails on, a thinned store, an unread slow snapshot, and a failure. `test/dashboard-page.test.ts` renders a fixed timeline (each line and cycle row as text, the newest open, one opened kept open, the newest opened after a "Reading…" drawing and kept across a restart, reading, none, a problem with and without BitNodes, slow snapshots that couldn't be folded, none given) and one from the real builder over a store; `test/telemetry-store.test.ts` covers the reads it uses.
  - **Recent events:** the last 50, newest first, including rejected connections and their origin.
- **When it can't show the status,** it says why in place of the data, rather than showing stale numbers:
  - "Brain unreachable": the brain stopped, or crashed;
  - "The brain couldn't answer": the brain failed to build the status (it logs why);
  - "The dashboard couldn't show the brain's status": the page itself failed.
- **The supervision switch:** while supervision runs, the button pauses it; otherwise (paused, or given up) it resumes it, and resuming also clears a give-up. Paused, the supervisor won't relaunch the game, so it can be closed on purpose. The switch is kept in the store's `settings`, so it survives restarts of the brain. A switch the brain refuses says so under the button.
  - **A brain that doesn't supervise** (anything but Steam's game on Windows) shows supervision "off": nothing reads the switch there.
  - **The contract with the supervisor** (`src/supervision.ts`): it reads the switch with `readSupervisionSwitch` at every check, and again before any kill or launch; it records a give-up with `recordSupervisionGaveUp` (the `supervision.gaveUpAtMs` setting); and it records each launch as a `relaunch` event (`RELAUNCH_EVENT_KIND`), with its cause as `{ cause }`. The dashboard lists the newest 10.
- **Alerts** are rules over the store, evaluated each time the page asks, so there's no alert state to get out of step. `incomeStalled`'s one fact the store can't give cheaply, the money's last rise, comes from the brain's `IncomeWatch` (`src/income-watch.ts`), which notes it as each medium snapshot arrives: reading back every medium snapshot of an hours-long stall, each with the whole network, at every status read would cost too much.
  - **"Since the brain started"** means after the brain's own `brainStarted` event, which it records once its in-game build is ready. That's this run of the brain, whatever the clock does.

  | Alert | When | Clears |
  |---|---|---|
  | `agentSilent` | No fast snapshot for over 60 s, counted from the brain's start if none came since | When the next one arrives |
  | `versionDrift` | The brain went on hold since it started: the game isn't the pinned version (#91) | When the brain restarts (a hold lasts the brain's run) |
  | `deployFailed` | The last deploy since the brain started failed | When a deploy succeeds, or a connection finds the deployment already in place (`deploymentCurrent`), or the brain restarts |
  | `supervisionGaveUp` | The supervisor stopped (#94): after 3 launches in an hour, or after 10 failed checks in a row with the last failure (#127) | When supervision is resumed |
  | `dialogBlocking` | A dialog the Agent leaves for the owner (a script's prompt, one the owner opened) is open, in the newest fast snapshot (#143), quoting its text: nothing is done on the page while it's open | When it's closed |
  | `incomeStalled` | The hack loop is on with a target, and the player's money in the medium snapshots hasn't risen for over 10 minutes (`INCOME_STALLED_AFTER_MS`, #184), with how long, the money and the target. A purchase lowers money, which isn't a rise. The 10 minutes count from the last rise, or from when the watch began: the Agent's connection, a medium snapshot that finds the loop off or without a target, or the switch flipped on the dashboard. The brain logs a line as it starts and as it ends | When money rises, the loop goes off or loses its target, or the Agent disconnects or connects again |
  | `cloudServerProblem` | A cloud purchase the Agent reported done with a problem (#194): bought under another name, say. The brain buys no more cloud servers meanwhile | When cloud servers are switched off (and on again), or the brain restarts |
  | `messageRefused` | The Agent refused the brain's last message as not valid (#194's review), in the newest fast snapshot, with its reason and how many it has refused since it connected: it acts on the last one it took, so the hack loop, the work and the intents are as they were | When the Agent takes a desired state again, or another Agent connects |
  | `factionStateUnread` | The faction state couldn't be read in the newest 3 slow snapshots in a row or more (#197, `FACTION_STATE_UNREAD_AFTER`: about 3 minutes; a single miss is an event and a log line; a skipped one neither ends nor adds to the run), with the backend and the newest unread's problem, since the run's first (followed back as far as a day's slow snapshots). A skipped reading (no sidebar on the page), one read with the page not put back, and an older Agent's slow snapshot (no reading) aren't unread | When a slow snapshot's is read, skipped or from an older Agent |
  | `intentStuck` | An intent tried 5 times (2 minutes) and still not done, in the newest fast snapshot (#141); one alert per intent, with its last problem | When it's done, or leaves the list |
  | `backdoorOverdue` | One of the hacker factions' servers rooted, at its hacking level and not backdoored for 5 minutes or more (`BACKDOOR_OVERDUE_AFTER_MS`, #195), by the connected Agent's medium snapshots: one alert per server, from the controller's `backdoorsDue()`, which judges each medium snapshot as the rule does. The brain asks for it at once and it takes seconds, so this is the rule or the backdoor failing; a backdoor with no intent can't be `intentStuck` | When it's backdoored, or the Agent disconnects |
  | `factionWorkStalled` | The brain's faction work is sent, and its faction's reputation has risen under half the work's rate over the last 10 minutes (`FACTION_WORK_STALLED_AFTER_MS`, #203), by the connected Agent's slow snapshots: from the controller's `factionWorkStall()`. A rise alone isn't enough: a joined faction not being worked gains a little passively. With no reading of its reputation in the window at all (slow snapshots stopped, or each read skipped), the alert says that instead of blaming the rate. The work isn't converged (the Work row says why), or isn't counted. The brain logs "faction work STALLED" too | When the reputation rises at half the rate again, the brain's work stops (your work, the switch, another faction), or the Agent disconnects |
  | `batcherNotRunning` | The batcher is sent and the last 3 fast snapshots or more say it isn't running, with the deploy worker's reason (#211, `BATCHER_NOT_RUNNING_AFTER`): the hack loop has left its hosts to it, so nothing hacks them. The Agent tries again 2 rounds after a failed start, so a start that works on the retry never raises it. The brain logs it when it starts and ends too |
  | `batcherOutOfStep` | The batcher's desyncs, late or lost operations rose within the newest 100 fast snapshots (about 3 minutes), with the target, the counts and the first snapshot that showed it (#206, `batcherOutOfStep`): its batches no longer land as planned, or someone else touched its target. It holds its hacks and batches on until the target is back, and past a weaken's time and 10 batches prepares it again (#315); the run report fails the run, unless a page stall, a delivery lag the page was behind (#309), or (for a batcher before #251) a clock step explains it and the batcher recovered by itself (a warning, #241) | When the rise is older than the newest 100 fast snapshots, or another batcher run starts |
  | `augmentationDonationFailed` | A donation the Agent reported done that 2 faction state reads since don't show in its faction's reputation (#225): one alert per donation, from the augmentation rule's `donationsFailed()`. The install cycle it was for is called off: check the faction's page | When the game resets (an install) |
  | `augmentationNotQueued` | A purchase the Agent reported done that 2 faction state reads since don't show queued (#210): one alert per purchase, from the augmentation rule's `notQueued()`. The game's own state says it wasn't bought, or the reader missed it: check the Augmentations page | When the game resets (an install), as the rule forgets that game's purchases |
  | `installNotRestarted` | An install out when the Agent disconnected, with no slow snapshot showing the reset 5 minutes later (`INSTALL_NOT_RESTARTED_AFTER_MS`, #210), from the controller's `installAwaitingRestart()`. The install's restart (boot.js, #164, #167) takes seconds. Or, at once, an install reported done that the next slow snapshot doesn't show reset ("install reported done but the game didn't reset", #213 re-review) | When a slow snapshot shows the reset (or, for one reported done, the brain restarts) |
  | `worldDaemonStalled` | A step on the path to the World Daemon the brain finishes in minutes, not done 5 minutes after it could be (`WORLD_DAEMON_STALLED_AFTER_MS`, #215): Daedalus's requirements met and not joined; The Red Pill reached or queued and not installed; or w0r1d_d43m0n at its level and the BitNode not destroyed (not with the destroy switched off), with what's missing. Or at once (#228, step `daedalusCount`), "Daedalus count stuck": an install while short of Daedalus's count that added none to it, passing over one that counts (#238) | When the step is done, or no longer could be (for `daedalusCount`, the next install) |
  | `gangStalled` | One of the gang's silent stalls (#354, `src/gang-stalls.ts`), from the gang rule's own watch on each medium snapshot, with its own plan: one of the worker's problems in every reading, a member of the rule's off its task, or the respect flat while the plan has members working (under a process's worth of the plan's tasks a reading), each for a minute. One alert per stall, with what and since when; the run report fails the same | When a reading no longer shows it, or a reading has no gang (none formed, unread, or outside BN2) |
  | `storeThinningFailed` | The store's thinning at this start (opted into with `telemetryKeptWholeDays`, #283's review) failed, with why: the store is as it was, and the brain runs on | When the brain restarts (the next start tries again) |
  | `dataBackupFailed` | The newest [data backup](#the-data-backup) failed (#284), with why ("the data directory's backup failed: not enough free space in …"), or was taken but older ones couldn't be removed, so they use more disk. The brain plays on; a failed one is tried again within the hour | When a backup is taken cleanly, or the brain restarts (the problem is held in memory) |

  The route-stall alert (no progress on the route's critical path) needs a route, so it comes with the planner (M4).
- **Security** (owner decision 14, localhost only):
  - it listens on `127.0.0.1` only;
  - it refuses (403) a request whose `Host` isn't `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, so a web page can't reach it by DNS rebinding;
  - it refuses (403) any request another site's page makes (`Sec-Fetch-Site`, or an `Origin` that isn't its own), so no page can even make the brain build its status;
  - it refuses (403) a `POST` without the dashboard's own `Origin`, so another page can't flip a switch;
  - the page can't be framed by another page, and everything from the brain is shown as text, never as HTML.
- **The API** (the page's; a script must follow the same rules):
  - `GET /api/status` gives the status as JSON (`src/dashboard/status.ts`, `DashboardStatus`). Built on the brain's event loop, it holds every snapshot arriving meanwhile, so its reads must stay quick (#309: 15 ms on a copy of the 2026-11-08 run's 512 MB store); one slower than `SLOW_DASHBOARD_STATUS_MS` (0.2 s) is counted, and logged with the minute's holds (`src/brain-holds.ts`, below).
  - **The brain's event loop held** (#309, `src/brain-holds.ts`): whatever holds it holds every snapshot arriving meanwhile. Two cheap readings, a line a minute only when something was held, and what's left at the brain's stop: the slow statuses, counted with the slowest; and Node's own event-loop delay (`perf_hooks.monitorEventLoopDelay`, sampled every 20 ms), its longest over `BRAIN_HELD_MS` (0.2 s), with its 99th percentile: "held in the last 1 min: 2 dashboard statuses built slower than 200 ms, the slowest in 723 ms; the event loop held up to 650 ms (99% of its waits under 30 ms); every snapshot arriving meanwhile waited (#309)". A delivery lag the run report finds is then in the brain's log at the same minute, with its cause if it was the status. The timer doesn't keep the process alive. Tests: `test/brain-holds.test.ts` (the line, its parts, nothing held, counted afresh, and Node's histogram for real: a 300 ms hold seen), and `test/brain-process.test.ts` main.ts's wiring: a real brain over a store whose status is slow to build (1,440 slow snapshots of 5,000 small objects each), stopped cleanly at the BitNode's end, logs the slow status and the loop held, as it stops (Node's monitor samples every 20 ms from its start, so a hold before its first sample isn't seen).
  - `POST /api/supervision` with `{"paused": true}` or `{"paused": false}` sets the switch, and answers with the same. A script must send `Origin: http://127.0.0.1:12527` (with the dashboard's port) and `Content-Type: application/json`.
  - `POST /api/hack-loop` with `{"enabled": true}` or `{"enabled": false}` sets the hack loop's switch, the same way, `POST /api/programs` the hacking programs' (#136), `POST /api/home-ram` home RAM's (#138), `POST /api/cloud-servers` cloud servers' (#194), `POST /api/faction-work` the brain's faction work's (#203), `POST /api/augmentations` buying and installing augmentations' (#210), and `POST /api/world-daemon` destroying the BitNode's (#215).
  - `POST /api/intents` with `{"action": …, "args": {…}}` queues an intent (#141), answered with `{"id": …}`, or 400 with why it isn't valid. `POST /api/intents/cancel` with `{"id": …}` takes one off the queue, or 404 if it isn't there. Both the same way as the switches.
  - `POST /api/work` with `{"work": {"faction": …, "workType": …}}` sets the work (#151), or `{"work": {"kind": "company", "company": …, "field": …}}` company work (#153), or `{"work": {"kind": "crime", "crime": …}}` a crime (#156), or `{"work": {"kind": "class", "location": …, "classType": …}}` a class (#158), or with `{"work": null}` stops managing it; 400 with why for one that isn't valid.
  - Refusals:
    - 403: not the dashboard's Host or Origin;
    - 400: a body that isn't JSON, an on/off switch's body that isn't exactly `{"enabled": true}` or `{"enabled": false}` (or `{"paused": …}`), a body for `POST /api/stop-at-bitnode-end` that isn't exactly `{"setting": "off"}`, `{"setting": "brain"}` or `{"setting": "brainAndGame"}` (#303), `closeGame` queued (only the switch closes the game, #303), an intent that isn't valid (or a full queue), work that isn't valid or a body without `work`, or a path that isn't valid;
    - 413: a body over 1 KiB;
    - 404: another path, or cancelling an intent that isn't in the queue;
    - 405: another method;
    - 500: the brain failed, and logs why. The server carries on.

## The hack loop

M3's first slice ([#134](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/134), owner decision 34): the brain tells the Agent to run a hack loop on the game's servers (and, since #194, on its own cloud servers), and the Agent does it ([`packages/ingame`](../ingame/README.md#the-hack-loop)). It's the first desired state (architecture D2), and the code is `src/hack-loop.ts`.

- **The switch:** on unless the owner turns it off on the dashboard. It's the store's setting `hackLoop.enabled`, so it survives restarts of the brain. Off is sent at once, and the Agent stops every loop at its next round: within two rounds (4 s).
- **The target** (`chooseHackTarget`): a starter rule, until the planner (M4) decides.
  - Among the game's rooted servers with money (never home, a Hacknet server or one the player bought) that need at most half the player's hacking level (at least 1), it takes the one with the highest maximum money times growth.
  - Growth counts because the loop hacks only once a server's money is back at 75% of its maximum, and servers start at 4%. Over an 8 GB fresh start's first virtual hour, n00dles (growth 3000) earned $15m. foodnstuff, joesguns and sigma-cosmetics, with more money but growth 5–20, earned nothing.
- **The hosts** (`hackLoopHosts`, [#194](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/194)): the rooted servers with RAM in the medium snapshot that are the game's own (the static snapshot's, not purchased: never home, which is the Agent's, nor a Hacknet server, whose RAM makes hashes), and the brain's own [cloud servers](#cloud-servers) (`pserv-<n>`, core's `isBrainCloudServerName`). Those are hosts from the first medium snapshot after they're bought (10 s), at their RAM after an upgrade. The owner's own cloud servers, under any other name, are never hosts: the owner uses them (#134). It's the one place that says where the loop runs: the plan, the cloud server rule and the run report read it, and so does A1's hacking ceiling (#196).
- **The move to the next target** (`planHackLoop`, [#184](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/184)): on Steam, `7990834`'s loop moved every host from n00dles to zer0 when hacking reached 150, with zer0 at 4% of its money. The loop hacks only at 75% of the maximum and within 5 of the minimum security, so every thread weakened and grew it, and money stayed flat for 24 minutes until the owner stopped it. Now:
  - `best` is the starter rule's target. `current` is the target last set while it's still a candidate, across the Agent's reconnects while the game hasn't reset (the slow snapshot's `lastAugReset`). With none (a brain restart, the switch turned on, or a reset), it's the best candidate that's **ready**; else the one with the most of its money, of its maximum, if more than a fresh start's 4% (Server.ts:74-75): that's the one being worked, caught between a hack and its weaken; else `best`. Judged afresh on a reconnect, a target between a hack and its weaken isn't ready, and every host would have moved to the next target, the Steam stall again (#184 review).
  - **Ready** is the loop's own thresholds, in core (`isHackLoopReady`, `HACK_LOOP_MONEY_FRACTION`, `HACK_LOOP_SECURITY_MARGIN`), which `hack-loop.js` uses too: the medium snapshot's money at least 75% of the maximum, and security at most the minimum + 5.
  - `current` **earns** once a medium snapshot has shown it ready since it became the target (the loop has hacked it), or when it was taken as the one being worked.
  - `best` is `current`, or ready, or `current` doesn't earn yet: every host goes on `best`. Nothing earning is given up.
  - Otherwise `current` keeps earning on most hosts, and the largest, in RAM order (ties by name), **prepare** `best` until they hold at least half the hosts' RAM. Preparing is the same loop: it weakens and grows `best`, and hacks once it's ready. With two hosts or more, some are always left to earn. The hosts that prepared last time stay while they still hold at least half the RAM and leave some to earn, so a larger host rooted since doesn't stop a weaken or grow under way. With no host at all, nothing is prepared. The next medium snapshot that shows `best` ready moves every host to it.
  - The rule may move on while a target is prepared. In the simulator, zer0 was still being prepared when max-hardware became `best` at about hacking 160, and the loop moved from n00dles to max-hardware.
- **Each host's hack threads** (`hackThreads`, [#187](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/187)): the loop hacks once money is at 75% of the maximum, so every host's loop sees that at about the same time and hacks together. With all their threads, they took max-hardware from $124m to $111 in the simulator, and its growth of 30 took 25 minutes to bring it back. So the brain gives each host, earning or preparing, the threads its loop hacks with:
  - **The rule:** a round of hacks by every host takes no more than `HACK_REGROW_ROUNDS` (2) rounds of their grows bring back. A round of grows multiplies the target's money by e^g: g is the hosts' threads (their RAM over the loop's 2.4 GB budget, core's `HACK_LOOP_RAM_BUDGET_GB`) times one thread's log of growth at the target's minimum security plus 5, the slowest the loop lets it grow (core's `growthLogPerThread`, the game's `calculateServerGrowthLog`, grow.ts:8-28). A host's hack with t threads keeps 1 − t × p of what it finds, p being one thread's share at the target's minimum security, the most it ever is (core's `percentMoneyHackedPerThread`, the game's `calculatePercentMoneyHacked`, Hacking.ts:44-57). Each host's part of 2g is its part of the RAM, G, and its threads are the most whose hack keeps at least e^−G: (1 − e^−G) / p. Together the hosts' hacks keep at least e^−2g.
  - **n00dles** (growth 3000) grows back from anything: every host's count is over its threads, so all of them hack, as before. **zer0 or max-hardware** (growth 30 to 40): a fraction.
  - **Each count** is at least 1, then rounded down to a power of two, so the share's slow rise with the hacking level changes it, and restarts that host's loop, only now and then. Then it's at most the threads the host's RAM holds (n00dles's count is all of them), still at least 1. A count over the loop's threads (RAM used by other scripts), the deploy worker caps.
  - **BitNode multipliers:** `ScriptHackMoney` is folded into the player's `hacking_money` (#321, core's `bitNodeHackingMultipliers`, from the slow snapshot's BitNode: 0.2 in BN4, so five times the threads for the same share, 0.15 in BN5), and `ServerGrowthRate` into `hacking_grow` (#334: 0.8 in BN2), so exact in BN1, BN2, BN4 and BN5.
