One with none (combat, The Red Pill) isn't worked for; NeuroFlux Governor is left to A6.
  - **The candidates:** each augmentation a joined faction that offers work sells, with a hacking value, neither installed nor queued, each missing prerequisite sold by a joined faction, and its reputation (core's `augmentationCost`, the BitNode's multiplier in) not yet reached at any joined faction that sells it: one reached is A6's to buy.
  - **The rate:** each faction's best work type by the game's formula (core's `factionReputationPerSecond`): the player's skills and `faction_rep`, the faction's favour, and the focus the work goes with (decision 37).
  - **The choice:** the most hacking value per second of work to reach the reputation; ties to the sooner, then the faction first in the game's order. It's stable: working for a faction only brings its candidates nearer, and once one's reached the next is chosen, usually at the same faction, which changes nothing sent.
  - **Short of Daedalus's count** (#228, core's `daedalusCountOf`): every augmentation but NeuroFlux Governor and The Red Pill is a candidate (each adds one to the count). Those with a hacking value still go first, by value a second (they count too, and the early game stays as above: the brain's reading of #228); with none left to work for, the soonest reached of the rest, the work saying it's for the count ("…, for Daedalus's count: 23 of 30 augmentations installed").
  - **The Red Pill first** (#215, [the World Daemon](#the-world-daemon), `redPillWork`, core's since #260): once a joined faction sells it (Daedalus), it's neither installed nor queued, and no joined seller's reputation reaches it, the work is the seller's best, for The Red Pill, ahead of any hacking value: it ends the BitNode.
  - **Sent** as the work (`{ faction, workType }`, focused per decision 37) when the owner has set none and the switch for **the brain's faction work** (`factionWork.enabled`, on unless turned off) is on. The owner's work always wins. The Work view says whose the work is (`source`: `owner` or `brain`).
  - **None** when the snapshots don't say yet (none, the faction state unread, a figure missing): no work is sent, so the Agent leaves the game's work as it is. Or definitely: no faction joined, none that offers work, nothing left worth it, or an augmentation core doesn't know (another game version).
- **The brain's log:** "factions: joining CyberSec (its invitation)", "factions: CyberSec joined"; "faction work: hacking for CyberSec, for Neurotrainer I (reputation 2 of 1,000, 0.32/s: about 52 min)" at each new choice; "faction work: stopped (your work on the dashboard)" when a definite reason ends it.
- **Events, for the run report:** each choice as a `factionWorkChosen` event (the faction, work type, target, reputation, requirement, rate and ETA) when its faction, work type or target changes, and its end as `factionWorkStopped` (`{ reason }`). Snapshots missing (a reconnect) store nothing. The controller's clock for them is the latest channel event's time.
- **Fail loudly:** a join that fails is an intent not done: after 5 tries, `intentStuck`. While the brain's work is sent, its faction's reputation is watched in the connected Agent's slow snapshots: risen under half the work's rate (`FACTION_WORK_ON_TRACK_SHARE`) across the last 10 minutes (`underRateSince`), it's the `factionWorkStalled` alert and a "faction work STALLED" log line ("… rising at the work's rate again" when it recovers). Not any rise: a joined faction not being worked gains passively (`Faction/FactionHelpers.tsx:132-170`), at most a tenth of the best work's rate, or 1/120 a cycle. Judged over the whole window, not snapshot to snapshot: the page's reader (#197) reads the worked faction's reputation fresh each time from the work's own display, but any other faction's only when it opens the Factions page, at least every 10 minutes; work that isn't going on leaves a reading that stays flat, then jumps by up to 10 minutes of passive gain, which one interval alone could take for work. The run report fails on the same stretches ([the run report](#the-run-report)).
- **The BitNode's `FactionWorkRepGain`** (0.2 in BN14, 0.5 in BN2) is in the rate, from core's table: the stall compares against it, so it must be the game's.
- **Working toward donation favour** (#225, [owner decision 53](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md), legacy `work-for-factions.js:897-918`, `:929-930`; `workTarget`, core's since #260): at a faction that takes donations (core's `acceptsDonations`), the work goes to the lesser of the augmentation's reputation and the reputation this cycle that unlocks the faction's donations at the next install (core's `reputationToUnlockDonations`: 462,491 from no favour in BN1), unless the augmentation's is within 10% of the larger of that and the reputation now (`WORK_TO_AUGMENTATION_WITHIN`): then all the way to it. Past the target, the augmentation is bought with a donation after the install. The ETA and the value a second use the target.
  - **Unlocked:** a faction whose donations are unlocked isn't worked, nor an augmentation one sells: A6 buys its reputation.
  - **The Augmentations switch off:** no install or donation follows, so none of this applies: each augmentation is worked to its own reputation, at any faction.
  - **The Red Pill** stays first, worked to Daedalus's target the same way, and not at all once Daedalus's reputation reaches it (A6 installs for "Daedalus donations") or its donations are unlocked (A8's way donates).
  - **The choice in words** says the target and why: "hacking for BitRunners, for BitRunners Neurolink (reputation 400,000 of 462,491: donations at the next install, BitRunners Neurolink at 875,000; favour 0.0, 150.0 after the next install at it, …)". Its event carries `target`, `augmentationReputation` and `favor`, and a change of target is a new choice.
- **The share bonus** (#224): the rate counts the bonus of the share threads the batcher reports running (`shareBonusOf`, [sharing spare RAM](#sharing-spare-ram)), so the choice, its ETA and the stall's rate include it; 1 with none. A choice made before the share starts (it runs only while faction work goes on) carries the rate without it, and is stored so: BN1.2's and BN1.3's Red Pill holds logged 49 and 42/s against 76 and 64 with the share the fast snapshots showed at the hold's start (×1.555 and ×1.527; #345).
- **Not modelled:** a gang's faction (none in BN1: the shops are asked with no gang, so a gang's would be its faction's own list).

### The faction suite

`test/factions-simulation.test.ts`: an 8 GB fresh start in BN1, the brain as on Steam. The hack loop roots CSEC, the brain backdoors it (#195), and CyberSec invites the player; the next slow snapshot's faction state shows the invitation and the brain joins (`sim`'s `acceptFactionInvitation`), then chooses CyberSec's hacking work for Neurotrainer I and the Agent works it, focused. Over the next 15 virtual minutes CyberSec's reputation rises in every slow snapshot, the work's view says it's the brain's, no stall is logged, and the run report shows the join, the choices counted in the cycle (each with `--verbose`) and the reputation gained, and passes. CI runs seed 1 (about 6 s); `FACTION_SEEDS=1,2,3,4,5` runs others: on those, CyberSec was joined by 14.7 to 15.7 virtual minutes and held 403 to 417 reputation 15 minutes on.

## Augmentations and the reset

M4 phase A's sixth slice ([#210](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/210), epic #191): the brain buys the augmentations worth installing and installs them, the run's reset cycle. Its choices are computed from the game's formulas (owner decision 46), in core's `augmentation-plan.ts` ([`packages/core`](../core/README.md#augmentations-and-the-reset)). The rule, as the controller runs it, is `src/augmentations.ts`; what the run report reads of it is `src/augmentation-report.ts`. **It's on by default, so a live run resets** (owner decision 48). The dashboard's Augmentations switch (`augmentations.enabled`, `POST /api/augmentations`) turns it off. Since #260 the decision before the commit is core's (`augmentationShops`, `augmentationEndgame`, `installJudgement`, [core](../core/README.md#the-brains-choices-shared-with-the-fast-model)), shared with the fast model; the rule here keeps the countdown, the commit, the purchases and the words. What of these the fast model copies is tested against the brain on the same inputs (#263, "brain against the fast model"): in `test/augmentations.test.ts`, the countdown's start, extensions, end and call-off, the countdown's and the lookahead's reserves, #243's hold with its window and `assumeMoney`, and the copied constants; in `test/desired-state.test.ts`, the purchase chosen (a program, home RAM or a cloud server) from the same purse, money and income, and what the countdown and the endgame holds hold back from it; since #294, in `test/cloud-servers.test.ts`, the cloud purchases' income measure, sample by sample, and in `test/backdoors.test.ts`, a hacker faction's join: `BackdoorRule` asking on the Agent's medium snapshots, one server at a time, and `chooseJoin` picking the joins' order at slow snapshots, on the Agent's and the game's clocks, within 0.3 s of the model's mean; with the constants the model copies read from their sources.

- **What it reads:**
  - from the slow snapshot: the faction state (#197: each joined faction's reputation and favour, and the queue), the reset info (the installed augmentations, the BitNode, the Source-Files, `lastAugReset`), its game time, and the money sources since the install (`moneySources`, for the income);
  - from the medium snapshot: the player (skills, money, `faction_rep`) and home's RAM.

  Without one of them it waits, saying which. Nothing is decided on a guess.
- **Not committed, each update:**
  - **Reached:** each augmentation a joined faction sells (not NeuroFlux Governor), neither installed nor queued, whose reputation a joined seller has; or (#225, [donations](#augmentations-and-the-reset)) one a joined faction with donations unlocked sells, counted when the money covers its price and its donation.
  - **The set:** core's `chooseAugmentationsToBuy`, the most hacking value (A5's `hackingValue` at the hacking level, #203) the money covers. It's chosen greedily, each augmentation with its prerequisites, in buy order: dear ones early, since each purchase makes the next 1.9 times dearer (`Constants.ts:41`), but a prerequisite before what needs it, and a prerequisite chain by its dearest member (so a cheap prerequisite of a dear augmentation goes first, its chain right after).
    - Only augmentations worth installing count: those with a hacking, speed, money, growth, chance, exp or faction reputation multiplier.
    - So no combat or charisma augmentations, and no The Red Pill (A8's); but short of Daedalus's count (#228, [below](#invitations-and-daedaluss-count)), any but NeuroFlux Governor and The Red Pill.
  - **When to install, short of Daedalus's count: the planner** ([#343](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/343), planner v0's second decision, owner decision 67, in place of the count rule, its quick install and #243's hold there, decisions 51 and 56; core's `install-timing.ts`). While the count isn't met (not 30 installed, NeuroFlux Governor once, and Daedalus neither inviting nor joined; never short with The Red Pill at the gang's faction), at every update:
    - **The candidates:** *install now*, the n an install would add to the count (what's queued, the set, and NeuroFlux Governor's levels only while none is installed: one entry, `Augmentation/AugmentationHelpers.ts:55-58`); or *wait for the next k*, the countables still to come soonest (core's `countablesToCome`: by work, by a donation, or by the money at the income; `nextCountables`), the k-th's τ the latest of the work for them, one faction at a time (each faction's longest, added up), the others' own times, and the money for the set with all k in buy order at the income.
    - **The estimate** of the time to the count, N still needed and T this cycle's time (online, and before the brain first saw the cycle the game's time since its install, `augmentations.cycleSeen`: a save first seen mid-cycle, an install made while the brain was down; offline counted whole, cautious for installing now): install now 0 once n ≥ N, else ⌈(N − n) / n⌉ · T; wait for k τ_k once n + k ≥ N, else τ_k + ⌈(N − n − k) / (n + k)⌉ · (T + τ_k). The next cycles are taken to be this one's shape (cautious for installing now: the new multipliers make them faster); every left-out term (later countables dearer, factions not joined yet, a money-bound τ's income spent meanwhile, restarts, each install's commit, a τ by work at the level now though it rises) is labelled in core's file. A τ by work counts the share bonus the batcher reports running (#345, `shareBonusOf`), at its mean over the income's window (`shareBonusMean`: sampled at each slow snapshot, 1 only once no share ran in the whole window, so a dip for an update can't flip a wait to an install): leaving it out made a wait look 1.6 times as long, pushing the planner toward installing now, the irreversible choice.
    - **No income measured** (the income's 10-minute window, after a restart or a gap): every money-bound τ is never, so installing now is held unless the set meets the count ("install now held: no income measured yet"); the least wait, or nothing, meanwhile. Held so two of the income's windows (`INCOME_HELD_TOO_LONG_MS`, 20 minutes online) it's `augmentationIncomeHeld` (an event, an "INCOME UNMEASURED" log line), once, and a run report failure.
    - **An invitation's money held** (#228's hold, `invitationHold` from the invitations rule's last update: a city group's money, or Tian Di Hui's in its city; it ends with the invitation, or 5 minutes in the city without one): installing now, which would spend it, is held likewise unless the set meets the count.
    - **The choice:** the least, a tie to install. Installing now is chosen at once, no countdown ("Daedalus's count"; then committed as any install, through the gate and a backup). With n = 0 (NeuroFlux Governor's levels alone) there's no install candidate: never a NeuroFlux-only install. While it waits, A5 works for the first of the k by work (`countHoldTarget`), and the money for the set and the k is held back from cloud servers and home RAM only when τ_k is within the lookahead's 15 minutes.
    - **Seen:** the progress carries `timing` (N, n, T, each candidate's τ and estimate, the next countables, the choice, and the words), as do the dashboard ("The planner: …" in place of the bars), the log ("augmentations: the planner's install timing: …" as the choice, or n, changes) and the `augmentationInstallChosen` event (`timing`, and `short`: installed of required).
    - **The wait, and failing loudly:** the wait is kept in the store (`augmentations.installWait`: its cycle, start, first τ, the n + k it waits for, and whether it's overdue), so a restart carries it on. Still short at twice its first τ and `INSTALL_WAIT_OVERDUE_SLACK_MS` (the income's 10-minute window) online from its start, it's `augmentationWaitOverdue` (an event, a "WAIT OVERDUE" log line), once; from then on every fresh τ is scaled by its measured error, the wait so far over its first τ (a rate fitted online), so a stalled wait gives way where installing now is shorter, except at the last countable, where the scaled wait never exceeds T; the overdue failure reports it. The run report fails the overdue wait, an install chosen short of the count without the planner's estimates, a NeuroFlux-only install short of it (the planner's n 0, or without its estimates a set and queue of NeuroFlux Governor alone), and installing held for the income too long.
    - **The A/B's baseline:** the controller's `installTiming` "count rule" (a test's option, never main.ts's) times installs short of the count as before #343, the count rule with #243's hold. `test/augmentations.test.ts`'s count rule tests and `test/desired-state.test.ts`'s augmentation tests run on it; their fixtures are short of the count.
  - **BN2 before the gang: wait for the gang, or install now** ([#357](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/357)'s first slice, the planner's BN2 route, core's `gang-route.ts`). In BN2 with no gang read yet (the gang rule's stage "noFaction" or "forming") and avmnite-02h's required level in the static snapshot, the planned branch takes these two candidates in place of #343's, each scored by its time to the gang formed: once it forms, Daedalus's count never counts (The Red Pill is the gang's faction's), so the rest of the route is the same either way.
    - **Wait for the gang**, τ_g: avmnite-02h's level's experience (`Server/data/servers.ts:1468-1486`, its roll) less the experience now, at the level multiplier read (`mults.hacking` × BN2's `HackingLevelMultiplier`, `PersonObjects/formulas/skill.ts:7-15`) and the hacking experience rate A6 measures (the World Daemon's `HackingExpRate`: a 10-minute window, a minute at least), plus 70 s for the backdoor, the invitation, the join and the forming (`GANG_FORMING_STEPS_MS`, #363's seed 1; seeds 1, 3 and 5 since: 90, 60 and 60 s). Until a slow snapshot shows the gang's faction (a minute after the gang forms), still this route: #343's install now could otherwise go in that minute (seed 5 did).
    - **Install now:** the commit (`GANG_ROUTE_COMMIT_MS`, 2 minutes, #343's) and R, the level's experience from 0 (`Prestige.ts:74`) at the install's multipliers (core's `levellingInstallMultipliers` of what it installs: the level's, the experience's and the speed's) at the rate now. Only with something new to install (n above 0).
    - **Left out, each labelled:** the port openers' money where they're not bought (optimistic for waiting); the rate now after an install, though the money and servers rebuild (optimistic for installing); the re-roll taken as today's (either way); the install's multipliers a cycle later on waiting's side (optimistic for waiting); the 70 s on installing's side (optimistic for installing).
    - **The choice:** the least, a tie to waiting. With no rate measured (a restart or an install: a minute of medium snapshots), installing now is held; while an invitation's money is held (#228), likewise, as #343's. Waiting is a wait as #343's are: no countdown, no money held (none is needed for it), kept in `augmentations.installWait` (`route` "gang forming") across a restart, and overdue at 2τ_g + 10 minutes online (`augmentationWaitOverdue`, then τ_g scaled by the measured error).
    - **Seen:** the progress's `timing` carries `route: "gang forming"` and `gang` (τ_g, R, the level, avmnite-02h's roll, the rate and the multipliers); the dashboard reads "The planner: no gang yet: wait for the gang (avmnite-02h's 218 at 214, 12 min) against install now (34 min: the commit and 32 min from 0 at 64.8 experience a second); chosen: wait"; the log "augmentations: the planner's install timing: no gang yet: …" as the choice changes; an install's line "BN2 before the gang: installing now is the planner's shortest time to the gang formed (#357)". The run report fails an install chosen in BN2 before the gang, avmnite-02h's level read, without these estimates (section 7), and an overdue wait (section 4, "wait overdue: the planner's wait for the gang …").
    - **The A/B's baseline:** the controller's `bn2Route` "rules" (a test's option, never main.ts's): as before #357, #343's candidates there, which installed on #363's seeds 3 and 5.
  - **When to install once the count is met: the legacy scripts' count rule, kept** (#343 amended: without it nothing would install between the count met and Daedalus's invitation, where hacking 2,500 or $100b is slow; the planner's next slice, the Daedalus/Red Pill stretch, replaces it) (#222, [owner decision 51](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md), replacing #210's comparison of the next augmentation's times, which waited all through the first overnight run). It's core's `installRule`, from `alainbryden/bitburner-scripts`' autopilot.js (`maybeInstallAugmentations`); smarter timing is phase B's planner's (decision 46).
    - **The counts:** *affordable*, those worth installing that an install would really install (the faction state's queue and the purchases reported done it doesn't show yet, never one `augmentationNotQueued`, #222 review) plus the set; *with NeuroFlux*, that plus NeuroFlux Governor's queued copies and the levels the money left after the set covers at the joined sellers' most reputation (core's `affordableCounts`, each level priced after the last).
    - **The bars:** install at 8 affordable, or 12 with NeuroFlux. Each falls by `floor(0.5 × hours online this cycle)`, never below 1: 7 and 11 from 2 h, the bar 1 from 14 h, with NeuroFlux from 22 h.
    - **A quick install:** under 20 minutes into the cycle (online and in the game's time, so a save first seen mid-cycle isn't one), 4 with NeuroFlux.
    - **Nothing left:** no augmentation worth installing left unowned at the joined factions beyond the set, with something to install (#210's, kept).
    - **Never**, for any reason, with nothing really to install: nothing queued, in the set or among the levels to buy. So a purchase never queued can't keep choosing installs at a bar of 1 (#213's never-chosen-again guarantee). Left out of the legacy scripts', each the planner's: SF11's +1 a level (BN11), a gang's 6 for a quick install, and BN8's and BN9's overrides.
  - **The bars' clock: the game's time online this cycle** (`advanceOnlineClock`): the time between consecutive slow snapshots of the same `lastAugReset` no more than `AUGMENTATION_SLOW_GAP_MS` apart (3 slow intervals, 3 minutes: more is the game closed, or the Agent or the brain away). Never the game's time since the install, which counted the save's 95 h closed before the first overnight run. It's kept in the store (`augmentations.online`, keyed by `lastAugReset`), so a restarted brain carries it on; another `lastAugReset` starts it at 0, and so does a save first seen mid-cycle: the conservative side, the bars at 8 and 12. A gap, or the game's clock set back, is left out and logged. There's no NS call for the time online (`getTimeSinceLastAug` went in 3.0).
  - **The countdown:** once the rule says install, the brain doesn't commit at once. It starts a countdown in the game's time (the latest snapshot's): 90 s, 30 s for a quick install. Meanwhile the money the counted purchases cost (the set and the NeuroFlux levels) is held back from cloud servers and home RAM, as the legacy scripts' reserve.txt holds it, and no program is bought (lost at the install). Each rise in the count with NeuroFlux extends it to `max(10 s, countdown × (1 − extensions ÷ the bar with NeuroFlux))` from now, never shortening it. If the rule stops saying install, it's called off; so it is by The Red Pill's way, a hold for Daedalus's count (#243), the switch, a reset, or the game's clock set back (a save loaded: a fresh one starts from the new time). The Red Pill has none. Each start, extension, call-off and end is logged ("augmentations: install countdown started, 90 s, the count reached: 8 of 8 new augmentations affordable (8 of 12 with NeuroFlux); $2,927,417,203 held back from cloud servers and home RAM").
  - **The progress:** each change in a bar, the reason, the countdown's end or a hold is an `augmentationProgress` event at once (the counts, both bars, the time online, when each bar falls next, the countdown, the hold); a change in the counts alone at most every `AUGMENTATION_PROGRESS_COUNTS_EVERY_MS` (5 minutes: money see-sawing across a NeuroFlux price would record each crossing); a steady state records nothing. The dashboard shows the same.
  - **The income** is the rise in `moneySources.sinceInstall`'s earnings (hacking, work, crime, Hacknet, contracts, infiltration) over 10 minutes of slow snapshots with no gap, for the reserve's lookahead alone (below).
  - **Chosen** at the countdown's end: the commit is kept in the store (`augmentations.cycle`, the `lastAugReset` it was chosen in) until a slow snapshot shows another, so a restarted brain carries on. The choice is an `augmentationInstallChosen` event (the set and its money, what's queued, the time online, the counts, the bars, and why: `count reached`, `quick install`, `nothing left`, `The Red Pill` or, #225, `Daedalus donations`) and a log line: "augmentations: install chosen, 6.0 h online this cycle: buying …, the count reached: 5 of 5 new augmentations affordable (7 of 9 with NeuroFlux)". Short of Daedalus's count, the note also keeps the facts of what counts within reach then (`countFacts`, #238): the money left after the set, and what counts within reach beyond it, the World Daemon's for "Daedalus count stuck".
- **Committed, one intent at a time,** each with its source `augmentation` in the intents list:
  0. **The note's donations** (#225), one faction at a time, The Red Pill's first, each shown by the faction state before anything else is asked for (below).
  1. **The set's next purchase** (`purchaseAugmentation`, #149), from the joined seller with the most reputation. The set is recomputed from the queue each time.
  2. **Home's RAM,** an upgrade at a time while the money covers it and its switch is on, but only with the money above the NeuroFlux Governor levels counted when the install was chosen and not bought yet (the note's `neuroFluxTarget`, #222 review: an install counted on them installs them). An install keeps it (`Server/ServerHelpers.ts:224-237`).
  3. **NeuroFlux Governor,** a level at a time, while the money and a seller's reputation cover it.
  4. **The install** (`installAugmentations`, #164), once the faction state shows every purchase the Agent reported done.
     - Never an install of nothing (#213 re-review): what it would install is the faction state's queue and the purchases reported done it doesn't show yet, never one `augmentationNotQueued`. With none, the cycle is called off ("nothing queued to install"), a restarted brain's already asked-for install too.
     - It's queued after the owner's whole queue, through the gate (its guard: nothing before it undone; two medium snapshots; a save backup) and the reset note ([intents](#intents)).
     - Once it's asked for, nothing more is bought, so a purchase never restarts its gate's count.
     - An install the owner queued has the same id, so it goes once, as the owner's.

  Meanwhile:
  - the programs rule stands down, as an install loses the programs;
  - the controller's `augmentationReserve()` is Infinity;
  - a purchase reported done counts as queued until a faction state read shows it, so the next goes at once (the fast snapshot that reports it triggers the update);
  - if nothing is queued to install (the money went elsewhere, or every purchase was a false done), the cycle is called off: an `augmentationCycleCalledOff` event.
- **The reserve, for cloud servers** (A2, #194): `augmentationReserve()`.
  - Before the countdown: nothing, unless the rule would say install with `AUGMENTATION_RESERVE_LOOKAHEAD_MS` more income and both clocks that much later, A2's payback limit (15 minutes, decided in #213's review). Then the money the purchases it would count cost. With no income measured, nothing. A server bought now pays itself back within that, so holding money any sooner would cost the income the install waits for.
  - In the countdown: the counted purchases' money, held from home RAM too (`countdownReserve()`).
  - Committed: Infinity. With the switch off: 0.
  - `#reserveForOtherPurchase` holds back the larger of it and the program's or home RAM's reserve, so cloud purchases spend only what's above it.
  - While committed, no cloud server is bought or upgraded at all ("the install of augmentations chosen (cloud servers are lost at it)"), as no program is.
- **The money** is the medium snapshot's, less each purchase reported done in a fast snapshot taken after it, by the game's times (#213 review and re-review): the purchase after one goes at once, on the fast snapshot that reports it, before a newer medium snapshot shows the money spent. The Agent's round takes its snapshots before its intents, so a medium snapshot taken after the fast one shows the purchase. One reported done rounds after it was made (a page effector's) can be taken off a medium snapshot that already shows it, until the next: less money, the safe side.
- **The income's samples** are the game's own count since the install, so they're kept across the Agent's reconnects but for a gap (above); a reset starts them again.
- **The countdown** is the brain's alone, not stored: a restarted brain starts one again (the bars' clock it keeps).
- **A restarted brain** reads the commit, and whether the install was asked for (the note's `installing`): then it buys nothing more. An install out when it restarts is awaited from when it went out (the reset note's `sentAtMs`) until the Agent connects.
- **Purchases aren't irreversible intents** (core's `isIrreversibleIntent`), rightly: what's bought only takes effect at an install, which is the irreversible step, behind the gate. A purchase spends money, as a program's does.
- **Fail loudly:**
  - **Not queued:** a purchase the Agent reported done, which `NOT_QUEUED_AFTER_READS` (2) faction state reads since don't show queued. It still counts as queued for the plan, so it isn't asked for again (its done is already recorded, and it would be asked for forever). A skipped read doesn't count, and the first read may have been taken before the purchase. It's an `augmentationNotQueued` event, a log line ("augmentations: BitWire NOT QUEUED: …"), the dashboard's `augmentationNotQueued` alert, and a run report failure.
  - **No restart:** an install was out when the Agent disconnected, and no slow snapshot shows the reset 5 minutes later (`INSTALL_NOT_RESTARTED_AFTER_MS`). That's the dashboard's `installNotRestarted` alert. The run report fails an install sent with no reset seen within 5 minutes.
  - **Done, no reset** (#213 re-review): the Agent reported the install done (the game had nothing to install) and the next slow snapshot shows no reset. The faction state's queue isn't the game's: the cycle is called off, an `augmentationInstallNotReset` event and a log line ("INSTALL REPORTED DONE BUT THE GAME DIDN'T RESET"), the `installNotRestarted` alert at once ("install reported done but the game didn't reset"), and a run report failure. No other install is chosen until a reset or a brain restart.
  - A purchase or the install that fails is `intentStuck` after 5 tries, as for any intent.
  - **Install overdue** (#222): the run report fails a cycle 16 h online (`INSTALL_OVERDUE_ONLINE_MS`; the bar is 1 from 14 h) with no install chosen, naming its last counts. Nothing affordable in 16 h is reputation stuck, a failure too. Shown, not counted, with the switch off, or while The Red Pill's way held the rule.
- **Donations** (#225, [owner decision 53](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md), under the Augmentations switch; the legacy faction manager's `canAffordWithDonation`, `faction-manager.js:546-549`, `:822-860`):
  - **Counted:** an augmentation no joined seller's reputation reaches, sold by a joined faction that takes donations with them unlocked (`donationTermsOf`, core's since #260), is in the set when the money covers its price and the donation for its missing reputation (core's `chooseAugmentationsToBuy` with `DonationTerms`): from the seller whose donation it raises least, one donation a faction, for the most reputation bought there. NeuroFlux Governor's levels likewise (core's `affordableCounts`). So the counts, the bars, the countdown's hold and the lookahead's reserve include the donations; the progress carries `byDonation` ("…(7 of 12 with NeuroFlux; 2 of them by donation)").
  - **Committed:** the note records each faction's donation (`donations`), and the install chosen event too. They go first, one faction at a time, as `donateToFaction { faction, amount, reputation }` (the amount from the faction's reputation now, core's `donationToReach`), each reported done and then shown by the faction state before anything else is asked for ("the faction state to show CyberSec's reputation after the donation"): the set is then what's reached, in buy order, as before. One the money no longer covers is passed over for the cycle, said in the log. Home's RAM never spends their money: they're made, or passed over, first.
  - **Shown:** each confirmed as an `augmentationDonated` event (the faction, the amount, its reputation before and after) and a log line; one the faction state shows before its done is reported is recorded then, once.
  - **Fail loudly:** a donation reported done that `NOT_QUEUED_AFTER_READS` faction state reads since don't show is `augmentationDonationFailed`: an event, a log line ("DONATION FAILED"), the dashboard's alert of that name, a run report failure ("donation failed"), and the cycle called off. And the run report fails **donations unused**: an augmentation within reach by a donation for 30 minutes online (`DONATIONS_UNUSED_ONLINE_MS`) while the brain's progress counted none by donation and it bought, donated and chose to install nothing.
  - **Daedalus's favour** (legacy `autopilot.js:920-931`; core's `augmentationEndgame` since #260): with Daedalus joined, its donations not unlocked, its reputation this cycle at what unlocks them at the next install, and The Red Pill's not reached, an install is chosen through the normal countdown, reason "Daedalus donations" ("Daedalus donations: its reputation this cycle unlocks its donations at the next install, so The Red Pill's reputation is bought, not earned"), if anything is to install; otherwise it's held, saying so. Not once 90% of The Red Pill's reputation is earned (legacy `work-for-factions.js:829`, `daedalusSpecialCheck`): A5's target for it is then The Red Pill's own (`redPillTarget`, shared by A5 and A6), and it's earned to the end. The install goes through without The Red Pill queued: the note's `reason` exempts it from #215's guard. Not in a BitNode where donations are unlocked from the start (BN8): there the next rule applies at once.
  - **The Red Pill bought:** once a seller's donations are unlocked, The Red Pill's way donates its missing reputation and installs for it ("The Red Pill") when the money covers the donation; until then its money is held back from cloud servers, home RAM and programs (`redPillHold()`), saying how much. Its donation is the note's first (`redPill`), the counts are budgeted without its money, and it's never passed over: its money gone, the cycle is called off and the next held for it.
- **The Red Pill and Daedalus first** (#215, [the World Daemon](#the-world-daemon)), overriding the count rule: with Daedalus's count and skill reached and its invitation not in, no install and $100b held back (from cloud servers and home RAM); joined, no install while The Red Pill is short of its reputation, but "Daedalus donations" once Daedalus's reputation unlocks its donations at the next install (#225, below); reached or queued, the install is chosen for it at once, with no countdown (`reason` "The Red Pill"), The Red Pill bought last. A hold calls a countdown off. Reported bought and never queued (`augmentationNotQueued`), it counts as owned, as R2 has it: the cycle is called off, and none is chosen without it. Committed on Daedalus's way, no install goes without The Red Pill queued, but for "Daedalus donations", an install for the favour (#225 review). Once it's installed, with the destroy wanted, no ordinary install is chosen. Each hold is logged as it starts.
- **The gang's faction's shop** (#334, G5, BN2's win route; core's `augmentationShops` with `gang`): the controller hands the rule the gang's faction as the slow snapshot reports it (`gangShopOf`: its reputation the game's own, which the gang's respect feeds, `Gang.ts:144-155`, a 75th of the respect gained × (1 + favour/100) × `faction_rep`). It stays out of the factions joined, so no work or donation goes there, but its shop joins the others: nearly every augmentation (core's `augmentationsSoldBy`: all but the special ones and the violet Congruity Implant, so no NeuroFlux Governor; The Red Pill too in BN2), bought from whichever seller has the most reputation. **The Red Pill there:** reached, the install is chosen for it, as at Daedalus. Short, an install would lose the cycle's reputation there (`Faction/Faction.ts:77-85`; the gang stays, `Prestige.ts:133-146`), but holding every install from the gang's first minute would hold BN2's for good: so a hold only while the reputation reaches The Red Pill's within `GANG_RED_PILL_HOLD_MS` at the cycle's average rate (core's `gangRedPillEtaMs`: the reputation over the game's time since the install), and (#335's review) **at most 20 minutes online a cycle from the hold's first start, never restarted**, as the count hold's window (kept in the store, `augmentations.gangRedPillHold`, for a restarted brain). The estimate is wrong either way: long where the game was closed, the gang formed late or its rate is rising (no hold, and an install throws the reputation away, optimistic for the route); short where the rate is falling, as members train, ascend or go to Territory Warfare or money tasks (a hold that doesn't reach it, which the 20 minutes cap). The window was sized from BN1's 28-minute cycle; a cycle to 2.5m at a gang is hours, so a hold that reaches it saves far more than it costs. "The Red Pill: NiteSec's reputation 2,400,000 of 2,500,000, about 18 min more at the cycle's rate (the gang's faction; held 0 s of at most 20 min) (an install now would lose it)". **With the gang's faction selling The Red Pill, Daedalus isn't needed** (#335's review, core's `gangSellsRedPill`): Daedalus's count never counts (`daedalusCountOf`'s `redPillElsewhere`: so only what has a hacking value is worth buying, never any augmentation for the count, for A6, A5, the invitations' travel, the World Daemon's path and the reports alike), and no hold for Daedalus's reputation, its favour's install or its invitation's $100b. A commit for an ordinary install isn't held for The Red Pill there; a commit for it that no seller's reputation reaches now (a stale reading's: forming a gang zeroes its faction's, `PlayerObjectGangMethods.ts:67`) is called off, never left waiting; with the gang unread (a brain restarted in BN2, the gang rule's `gangUnread`) it waits for the reading instead. **A5** (#335's review): an augmentation the gang's faction's reputation reaches in its shop isn't worked for (A6's to buy), and The Red Pill isn't worked for at Daedalus.
- **Switched off:** nothing is bought or installed, the reserve is 0, and a cycle under way is called off. What's bought stays queued, for the owner.
  - The E2 tests' brains have it off (`effectors.e2.test.ts`).
  - So do the simulator suites that queue augmentations by hand: the install, destroy, backend and safe mode suites.

### The donation suite

`test/donations-simulation.test.ts` (#225): an 8 GB fresh start in BN1, the brain as on Steam, and a harness edit 2 virtual minutes in (`setFactionFavor`, the simulator's). CyberSec at 5,000 reputation with 150 favour and the money for its five and the donation: the five counted, two by donation, a quick install; committed, the donation first, seen in the faction state, then Gen I and Gen II bought far above the reputation earned; A5 never works for CyberSec; the run report shows the donation in its cycle's line, with its faction (its own line with `--verbose`), and no donation failure. BitRunners at 100 favour a little short of 306,374: A5 works to that, for donations at the next install, not to The BitRunners Neurolink's 875,000, and stops there. CI runs seed 1; `DONATION_SEEDS` (`1,2,3`) runs more. The formulas are checked against the engine's `ns.formulas.reputation` in `test/donation-parity.test.ts`.

### The augmentation suite

`test/augmentations-simulation.test.ts`:
- **The start:** an 8 GB fresh start in BN1, the brain as on Steam (the batcher, programs, home RAM, cloud servers, backdoors, faction work and augmentations on).
- **The edit:** 17 virtual minutes in, a harness edit stands in for the hours of work (A5's): CyberSec joined with 18,750 reputation, NiteSec joined with none (so something is left), and the money for the five, home's RAM upgrades and a NeuroFlux Governor level, $3m over. Programs, home RAM and cloud servers are paused from just before the edit until the install is chosen, so none takes that money before the next slow snapshot shows CyberSec's reputation.
- **The decision is a quick install** (#222): under 20 minutes into the cycle, 5 affordable (6 or more with NeuroFlux), so a 30 s countdown, then the install chosen (`quick install`); the run report lists the progress with its countdown (with `--verbose`).
- **The install:**
  - the brain buys Cranial Signal Processors Gen I and Gen II, BitWire, Synaptic Enhancement Implant and Neurotrainer I, in that order, through the `sim` effector;
  - then home's RAM (its next size), then NeuroFlux Governor's first level;
  - it installs through the gate and a save backup;
  - the game starts boot.js, and the next Agent connects.
- **After the reset:**
  - the five and NeuroFlux Governor level 1 installed, home at its new RAM;
  - the player's multipliers are theirs: hacking ×1.18 × 1.01, speed, exp and chance likewise;
  - the run report gives the cycle's set in one line by default, and with `--verbose` lists the progress, the decision, the purchases, the install sent and the install seen (the multipliers gained, the Agent away a few seconds), and passes; after the second install, its cycles (#244): the quick install's, the NeuroFlux Governor one's and the third under way, each with what it installed, Daedalus's count and what held it back.
  - The hacking level 20 minutes on is logged, not judged: home's RAM differs between the cycles.
- **The second install** (#238): about 35 virtual minutes on, NeuroFlux Governor's levels alone (13 queued), at 6 of 30. The factions joined that cycle (NiteSec, The Black Hand, Tian Di Hui, group B, CyberSec again) have too little reputation for anything they sell that isn't owned, so nothing that counts is within reach: section 3 and the brain's log say "installed NeuroFlux Governor only at 6 of 30 augmentations installed: nothing that counts within reach", and the report has no failure (#229's check failed it as "Daedalus count stuck").
- **Seeds:** CI runs seed 1 (about 50 s, 81 virtual minutes); `AUGMENTATION_SEEDS=1,2,3` runs others (seeds 1 to 5 pass).
- **On seeds 1 to 3** (#222): 182 s from the edit to the reset (the slow snapshot, the 30 s countdown, the purchases, the gate); home 32 GB then 64 GB; the hacking level 73 to 79 at the edit, 162 to 166 after 20 minutes of the second cycle.
- **Held for Daedalus's count** (#243), a second test: run #3's 17:49 in the engine, on to Daedalus's invitation. Its figures are the harness's more than the hold's: a live run's estimate is the issue's 10 to 25 minutes.
  - **Before the Agent,** harness edits stand in for the run so far: 25 installed (The Black Hand's all but its own augmentation, Tian Di Hui's eight, city group A's, Gen I and NeuroFlux Governor: the cheap ones every faction the brain could soon join sells), Sector-12 and Aevum joined (banning groups B and C), The Black Hand joined.
  - **Paused** from the start: sharing (so A5's rate is A6's), and programs, home RAM and cloud servers until the hold or the commit (so none takes the edit's money); augmentations until the edit (so no quick install of NeuroFlux Governor's levels resets the hacking level the edit gives).
  - **15 virtual minutes in,** hacking past 2500 (Daedalus's requirement, the hold's condition); a minute on, at A5's hacking rate at favour 0, The Black Hand's reputation is put 12 minutes short of its augmentation, CyberSec at 18,750 and NiteSec at 20,000 (four reached: the install would take the count to 29), and $50b.
  - **Held,** "install held for Daedalus's count: … The Black Hand in ~1x min (by work at The Black Hand, …)", A5 working for it, until its reputation comes; then "the count reached at this install", the countdown, and one install of the five: 30 installed. The run report's progress says "held: …", with no failure.
  - **On to Daedalus** (`AUGMENTATION_DAEDALUS=1`; CI stops at the install): 20 virtual minutes after each reset, a harness edit stands in for the hacking ramp (hacking past 2500, $200b), the same in both runs; then Daedalus invites. `AUGMENTATION_DAEDALUS_LOG` (a file) gets each install's time and count and the invitation's, as they come.
  - **The "before" column** comes from a copy of this test with the hold's checks taken out, run on main's code (7c741cc's `src`).
  - **The time from the edit to Daedalus's invitation,** seeds 1 to 5: before, 104.7, 61.2, 100.7, 61.2 and 98.7 minutes (the install at 29 at 3.7 minutes; the 30th took one more cycle on seeds 2 and 4, two on 1, 3 and 5, the first a NeuroFlux-only install at 29); after, 37.5 minutes on each (held 16.5 minutes, one install at 30). Seed 1 to the install runs in CI (about 20 s more); seeds 1 to 5 pass to Daedalus, and so does the fresh-start test. On main the long runs' reports also failed on "batcher out of step" and "income stalled" after the harness's $200b, not this change's.

## Invitations and Daedalus's count

[#228](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/228): the 2026-11-04 overnight run stalled at 23 of the 30 installed augmentations Daedalus needs (`DaedalusAugsRequirement`; NeuroFlux Governor counts once, `Faction/FactionJoinCondition.ts:130`). The code is `src/invitations.ts`; the count is core's `daedalusCountOf`.

- **Short of the count** (Daedalus neither joined nor invited, fewer installed than required): A6 counts any augmentation but NeuroFlux Governor and The Red Pill (core's `isWorthInstalling(name, true)`): in the set (hacking value first, then the cheapest), the counts and bars, the countdown, the lookahead's reserve, "nothing left" and the run report's "donations unused"; its progress says so ("…; short of Daedalus's count (23 of 30 augmentations installed): any augmentation counts"). A5 works for them once nothing with a hacking value is left to work for ([factions](#factions-and-their-work)).
- **Held one short** (#243, [owner decision 56](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md), a phase A exception to decision 51; core's `daedalusCountHold`): an install the count rule chose that would leave the count one short is held while the last can be bought within the hold's window. Run #3's BN1.2 installed at 29 of 30 with The Black Hand's augmentation 17 minutes off by work, and spent the next cycle (28 minutes) buying it.
  - **Only once this cycle has reached Daedalus's hacking (2500) or combat (1500) requirement** (core's `daedalusSkillsReached`): skills reset at an install, but the multipliers only rise, so the next cycle reaches it again, and the count is then what stands between the player and Daedalus. Before that, a hold would only delay the install's multipliers.
  - **The last:** each augmentation a joined faction sells that would add one (not NeuroFlux Governor or The Red Pill, not owned, queued or in the set, its prerequisites owned or queued), by its soonest route (`countablesToCome`, core's since #260), each the later of its reputation and its money:
    - its reputation reached at a seller: its money alone;
    - a donation (#225), at once: the seller whose donation is least; one the set already donates to is raised to the larger reputation, not both (as `planDonations` has it);
    - work, the sooner of that and any donation, only where someone works: the owner's faction work at the seller (at its work type), or, with no owner's work and A5's switch on, A5 under the hold (`bestWorkRate`, core's since #260; since #345 with the share bonus the batcher reports running, its mean over the income's window, 1 with none in it: cautious for waiting). A5 then works for that seller, for it (`countHoldTarget`; "…, the install held for it" in the faction work's line), ahead of hacking value.
    - its money: the set with it, in buy order (a dear one goes first), with any donation, at the measured income; none measured, only what the money covers now, unless the hold's window is already open (a restart or a gap in the slow snapshots loses the income for 10 minutes): then the money is taken as coming until the income is measured or the window ends.
  - **The window:** `DAEDALUS_COUNT_HOLD_MS`, 20 minutes of time online from the cycle's first hold: a cycle's cost (28 minutes in run #3) less a margin for the install's multipliers held back meanwhile. It's kept in the store (`augmentations.countHold`, keyed by `lastAugReset`), so a restarted brain doesn't start it again, and checked when the brain starts. It's never restarted in the cycle, so no hold outlasts it.
  - **Meanwhile:** no countdown; the money the countdown would hold (the counted purchases', so the rule goes on saying install), or the set's and the last's if more, is held back from cloud servers, home RAM and programs. The travel for invitations goes on (it isn't an install under way: the hold may end in none). The dashboard's Augmentations section shows "waiting for the install held for Daedalus's count: …" and "Held: …"; the brain's log "augmentations: install held for Daedalus's count: …"; the run report's progress line "…; held: …". Each is "Daedalus's count reachable this cycle, The Black Hand in ~17 min (by work at The Black Hand, $1,111,159,900; 29 of 30 after this install; held 20 min at most, 20 min left)".
  - **Over**, logged as "augmentations: the hold for Daedalus's count is over: …", with why:
    - "the count reached at this install" (the last reached and in the set: the countdown starts);
    - "this install would now leave it 2 short" (a purchase, or NeuroFlux Governor's first level, no longer covered);
    - "nobody works for The Black Hand" (a hold by work: the owner's work set elsewhere, or A5's switch turned off);
    - "The Black Hand isn't within what's left of its 20 min";
    - "its 20 min are up";
    - "the rule no longer says install";
    - "Daedalus's count met" (it invites);
    - "the Augmentations switch turned off", or "waiting for …" (the snapshots, The Red Pill's way, or what core doesn't know);
    - a restarted brain whose window is open logs the end at its first decision, unless the store says it was logged before the restart (the window is marked ended, and unmarked if held again).
  - Two or more short aren't held: the rest seldom fits a cycle's cost.
  - A held install isn't an install, so it's never "Daedalus count stuck"; the install after a hold keeps #238's facts. It doesn't excuse a cycle overdue its install, as The Red Pill's way does.
- **Tian Di Hui** has no enemies and sells eight; it invites only a player in Chongqing, New Tokyo or Ishima, with hacking 50 and $1m at the game's check (`Faction/FactionInfo.tsx:680-692`, every 2 s). While it sells one A6 would install, it isn't joined or invited, and the hacking level is 50, the brain visits Ishima (a Tian Di Hui city with a tech vendor, Omega Software, so home RAM and the TOR router are still bought there).
- **The city group** ([owner decision 54](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md)): joining a city faction bans its enemies (`Faction/FactionHelpers.tsx:45-47`) until the next install lifts every ban (`Faction/Faction.ts:77-85`), so each cycle the brain picks one group of core's `CITY_FACTION_GROUPS`: A (Sector-12, Aevum), B (Chongqing, New Tokyo, Ishima), C (Volhaven). A group's value (`cityGroupValues`, core's since #260) is what its factions sell that A6 would install, not installed or queued, nor sold by a joined faction or Tian Di Hui when it will join: short of the count, how many; otherwise their hacking values summed. The choice (`chooseCityGroup`): the most value of the groups whose invitations the money can earn (each faction's money, $15m to $50m, and the tickets), ties to the fewest travels, then A, B, C. Kept for the cycle once chosen, with its factions; a city faction joined fixes it.
- **The visits** (`planInvitations`): a stop a city, the one where the player is first; Tian Di Hui's goes with group B's Ishima, last, else first. The `travel` intent (#141) to the next stop once the money covers its invitations' and the ticket; there, their money is held from cloud servers, home RAM and programs (the most one of them needs, as #215's $100b for Daedalus) until they show. Group B ends in Ishima, its tech vendor. No travel with the owner's work set (a class or a job is in a city), the owner's own travel queued, or the Augmentations switch off. With an install under way (A6's countdown or commit: it puts the player back in Sector-12), nothing is travelled for or held, but from Chongqing or New Tokyo the player goes to Ishima, so A6 can still buy home's RAM before it. The intents list shows the source as `travel`.
- **The log and the events:** "invitations: city factions: group B (Chongqing, New Tokyo, Ishima): 5 augmentations that count, against A 4, C 3" (`cityGroupChosen`, once a cycle's choice); "invitations: travelling to Chongqing for Chongqing's invitation", "invitations: in Chongqing: $20,000,000 held for Chongqing's invitation", "invitations: Chongqing invited in Chongqing" (`invitationTravel`, phases `travel`, `hold`, `invited`).
- **Fail loudly:** held 5 minutes in a city with the money and no invitation is the `notInvited` phase: "invitations: NOT INVITED …", a run report failure; the visit is then dropped for the cycle (the game invites a faction once) and its money no longer held. A wait (the owner's work or travel queued, the switch, the money, the hacking level) is logged and stored as it starts, changes and ends, shown on the dashboard's Factions panel ("Invitations: …"), and summed in the run report while short ("waiting for a travel while short of Daedalus's count: …"). And the World Daemon's "Daedalus count stuck" ([below](#the-world-daemon)).
- **The reach** (`daedalusReach`): how many more the brain could still install, and where: the joined factions, those inviting without enemies, the hacker factions (whose invitations A3's backdoors bring as the hacking level grows), Tian Di Hui at hacking 50, and the city group: the one a city faction joined is in, else the one the brain chose (none before it chooses). Queued ones count; NeuroFlux Governor once; never The Red Pill.

### The invitations suite

`test/invitations-simulation.test.ts`: run #2's end against the real engine, from harness edits (the four hacker factions' set and NeuroFlux Governor installed, those four joined, donations unlocked at The Black Hand and BitRunners, hacking past 6,000, $2m, 20 minutes into the cycle; the hack loop, programs, home RAM and cloud servers off). The brain travels to Ishima and joins Tian Di Hui, works for it (its two with a hacking value, then, past them, the rest, for the count); with $100m it chooses group B (5 that count, against A 4, C 3), joins Ishima, Chongqing and New Tokyo, none of A's or C's, and ends in Ishima; with Tian Di Hui's reputation and $200b edited in, A6 buys its eight and the two no-value ones by donation and installs (33); Daedalus then invites. CI runs seed 1 (about 4 s); `DAEDALUS_COUNT_SEEDS=1,2,3` runs others.

## The World Daemon

M4 phase A's last slice ([#215](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/215), epic #191): from late game to the end of BN1.1. Daedalus's invitation and The Red Pill are A5's and A6's (above); `src/world-daemon.ts` reads the path from the snapshots, asks for the destroy once w0r1d_d43m0n is ready, and fails loudly when a step stalls; `src/world-daemon-report.ts` is what the run report reads of it. **The destroy is on by default** ([owner decision 50](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md): phase A's exit is a live run that destroys BN1.1 on its own), **into the next BitNode of the campaign's order** ([owner decision 66](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md), [#321](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/321): BN4 while Source-File 4 is short of 3, then BN1, then BN5, the BitNodes it can't play yet skipped, [#323](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/323)), behind the dashboard's "Stop destroying the BitNode" (`worldDaemon.enabled`, `POST /api/world-daemon`). **It can't be undone.**

- **Daedalus** invites when the BitNode's count of installed augmentations is reached (30 in BN1, core's `daedalusAugmentationsRequired`; installed only, NeuroFlux Governor once), $100b is held, and hacking is 2500 (or every combat skill 1500), `Faction/FactionInfo.tsx:138-149`. The game checks every 2 s (`engine.tsx:177-183`); Daedalus has no enemies, so [A5](#factions-and-their-work) joins it.
  - **While its count and skill are met and it hasn't invited,** [A6](#augmentations-and-the-reset) chooses no install (one would reset the money and the hacking level) and holds $100b back from cloud servers, saying what's missing ("Daedalus's invitation ($50,000,000,000 of $100,000,000,000): …"). With the skill short, ordinary installs go on: they raise the hacking multiplier.
- **The Red Pill** (2.5m reputation at Daedalus, $0, `Augmentation/Augmentations.ts:1946-1953`; not sold in BN15; in BN2 at the gang's faction too, #334: the path names the gang's faction, A6's route there, unless Daedalus has reached it (#335's re-review), at the cycle's rate, "The Red Pill: NiteSec's (the gang's faction) reputation 500,000 of 2,500,000 at 138.9/s (the cycle's): about 4.0 h", and its stall "reached, not installed" is the same 15 minutes; "reputation not rising" (10 minutes) there only while A6 holds installs for it (`redPillHeldAtGang`, #335's review: at most 20 minutes a cycle), as a gang's respect pauses while its members train, which holds nothing otherwise):
  - **A5 works for it first:** once Daedalus is joined and it's neither installed nor queued, the brain's faction work is Daedalus's best work, for The Red Pill, ahead of any hacking value (`redPillWork`, core's since #260).
  - **A6 holds ordinary installs while it's short:** an install resets every faction's reputation (`Faction/Faction.ts:77-85`), so it would lose the progress ("The Red Pill: Daedalus's reputation 1,000,000 of 2,500,000 (an install now would lose it)").
  - **Reached, or queued already, the install is chosen for it** (`reason` "The Red Pill"). It buys the set the money covers, home's RAM and NeuroFlux Governor, then The Red Pill last: every purchase makes the next dearer, and it costs $0. Then it installs.
- **w0r1d_d43m0n** is linked to The-Cave by an install with The Red Pill installed (`Prestige.ts:174-182`).
  - **Rooting:** it needs 5 ports (`Server/data/servers.ts:1532-1539`). The programs rule buys the port openers, and the hack loop's root worker roots it, as it roots every reachable server.
  - **Its hacking level** is 3000 × the BitNode's `WorldDaemonDifficulty` (`Server/ServerHelpers.ts:383-385`), 3000 in BN1, from the static snapshot.
  - **The ETA** is at the hacking experience rate measured over the medium snapshots (10 minutes, a gap or an install starting it again). It uses the multiplier the level and experience imply (`skill.ts:7-15`).
- **The destroy:** once The Red Pill is installed and w0r1d_d43m0n is rooted at its level, the rule asks for `destroyBitNode { nextBitNode }` (M3's, #175) into the next BitNode (below), fixed as it's asked for, logged ("world daemon: w0r1d_d43m0n rooted at hacking 3042: destroying the BitNode, into BitNode 4 …"). A slow snapshot whose BitNode or Source-Files can't be read asks for none, logged once ("… ready, but the next BitNode can't be read from the slow snapshot …"); the destroy step's stall follows.
  - **Through the gate:** the controller queues it after the owner's queue and before the brain's install (#215 review: an install resets the hacking level and drops the root), through [the gate](#intents): two medium snapshots of w0r1d_d43m0n's preconditions, then a save backup.
  - **No ordinary install once The Red Pill is installed** (#215 review), while the destroy is wanted (the switch on): A6 holds them, logged, as an install would reset the hacking level w0r1d_d43m0n needs, with `lastNodeReset` unmoved. The one exception is [levelling](#levelling-the-hacking-level-all-thats-left)'s (#336): NeuroFlux Governor's levels and an install, when the planner scores them the shortest way to the level.
  - **A restarted brain** with its destroy out restores it from the reset note (a `destroyBitNode` into a BitNode of the campaign's order the brain plays, `worldDaemonDestroyInto`), so the note is kept and the destroy held, into the same BitNode, until a slow snapshot shows the reset.
  - **Held until the reset:** once asked for, it's held until a slow snapshot shows another `lastNodeReset`, across the Agent's death, as the install's is.
  - **The owner's destroy wins:** one the owner queued, into any BitNode, is sent instead.
  - **As it goes out,** a `bitNodeDestroySent` event is stored (the brain's or the owner's, the BitNode, `replay`: why it's a replay, or null, and `skipped`: the BitNodes the order skipped on the way, in words, or null, and null on the owner's destroy, #323).
- **The next BitNode** follows the campaign's draft order ([owner decision 66](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md), #321, superseding 64 and amending 50; [`docs/synthesis.md`](../../docs/synthesis.md) §3), core's `nextBitNodeOf` over `CAMPAIGN_ORDER`, the whole draft list since #323: BN1.1, then BN4 ×3 (Singularity at ×1 RAM), then BN1 to Source-File 1's 3, then BN5 ×1, BN2 ×3, BN6/BN7 ×3, BN3 ×3, BN5 to 3, BN9, BN11, BN8, BN15, BN10, BN13, BN14 (each to 3) and BN12 to 50 (owner decision 1's levels where §3 gives no count). A destroy raises the destroyed BitNode's Source-File a level (to 3, but for BN12's: `giveSourceFile`, `RedPill.tsx:16-52`), so the next is the first step still short of its level *after* this destroy **whose BitNode the brain can play** (#323: core has its hacking multipliers and prices, `bitNodeUnplayableReason`; BN1, BN2, BN4 and BN5 so far, BN2 since #334): from BN1 (any level) into BN4 while SF4 is short of 3; from BN4.3 (SF4 reaching 3) into BN1 while SF1 is short of 3, else into BN5; from BN5.1 into BN2, three times (row 5); from BN2.3 into BN5 again (row 8's BN5.2-3), BN6, BN7 and BN3 skipped. The brain plays BN2, BN4 and BN5 with its BN1 rules (thin slices; BN2 with its gang): the first live run in each shows what breaks. For another BitNode, queue `destroyBitNode` with your own (M3's): yours goes instead.
  - **The skipped are said** (#323, core's `describeSkipped`, the rule's `skippedOf`): "BN6, BN7 and BN3 skipped: no hacking multipliers or prices yet", in the destroy's log line ("…, into BitNode 5 (BN6, … skipped: …)"), the start line, the view's `skipped` (the dashboard: "Destroying the BitNode (into BitNode 5; BN6, … skipped: …): …" while the destroy is the brain's), and `bitNodeDestroySent`'s `skipped` (the run report: "destroy sent (the brain's), into BitNode 5 (BN6, … skipped: …)"). A restored note's destroy is the brain's only into a BitNode it plays.
  - **With nothing playable left in the order to gain, a replay** (#311, kept by #321 for this case): Source-Files 1, 2, 4 and 5 at 3 (#323, #334), the destroy goes all the same, into BN1. BN1 destroyed into BN1 at the cap gives no Source-File (`giveSourceFile`, `RedPill.tsx:27-31`: "… is already at max level!", a dialog the Agent closes), and the portal enters BN1 regardless (`BitNode/ui/PortalModal.tsx:117-126`), so BN1.4 starts again: the run itself is the gain. `replayOf` says why ("the campaign's order has nothing playable left to gain (Source-File 1 at 3, Source-File 2 at 3, Source-File 4 at 3, Source-File 5 at 3), but the run"), after the BitNodes skipped (all the rest, for now).
  - **Named everywhere** (#321): the destroy's log line ("…, into BitNode 4", or "…, into BitNode 1 (BN6, … skipped: …), a replay (…)"), the view's `nextBitNode` and `replay` (the dashboard: "Destroying the BitNode (into BitNode 4): …", your queued destroy's BitNode while it's queued, and "…; it replays BitNode 1: …" while the destroy is the brain's to send), the start line ("destroying the BitNode on (into BitNode 4)", from the store's last slow snapshot), `bitNodeDestroySent`, and the run report's BitNodes line ("destroyed after …, into BN4.1"; "destroy sent at …, into BitNode 4, the next BitNode not seen"). The stop at the BitNode's end fires on it as on any destroy ([below](#stopping-at-the-bitnodes-end)).
  - **BN4's hacking and prices** (#321, and its review): core has BN4's hacking multipliers (`bitNodeHackingMultipliers`: `ScriptHackMoney` 0.2, `HackExpGain` 0.4, `BitNode.tsx:633-661`), which the snapshots don't carry and Singularity can't read there. The brain folds them into the player's `hacking_money` and `hacking_exp` where it reads the player (`hackingPlayerOf`, `batcherMultsOf`, the hack loop's threads, the ceilings), from the slow snapshot's BitNode, so the batcher plans and forecasts BN4's own hacks, and the Agent's batcher, given its config's multipliers, sizes them so. Before a slow snapshot says the BitNode, BN1's, logged once ("hacking: no slow snapshot yet says the BitNode, …"). Home RAM's and cloud servers' prices are BN4's own (core's `bitNodePriceMultipliers`: the same as BN1's but the cloud servers' softcap, 1.2).
  - **BN5's** (#323, `BitNode.tsx:663-693`): `ScriptHackMoney` 0.15, `HackExpGain` 0.5, folded in the same way, and the softcap 1.2. What BN5 sets on the servers (`ServerStartingSecurity` 2, `ServerStartingMoney` 0.5) the snapshots read; w0r1d_d43m0n's level (4500, `WorldDaemonDifficulty` 1.5) is the static snapshot's; `AugmentationMoneyCost` 2 is in core's augmentation table. Its other multipliers on hacking are 1. Intelligence, which BN5 opens, the formulas read from the snapshots already, so Formulas.exe isn't needed. The BN5 suite ([below](#the-bn5-suite)) plays an hour of it, an install at its prices, and its destroy into BN2.
  - **A BitNode core knows neither for** (#321, core's `bitNodeAssumptions`): none is bought, and its hacking takes BN1's, said once per BitNode the slow snapshots show ("in BitNode 3, taken as BN1's: …") and in the run report under the BitNode ("taken as BN1's in BitNode 3 (#321):"). None in BN1, BN2, BN4 or BN5.
- **Fail loudly:** a step the brain should finish, not done in its time after it could be, is `worldDaemonStalled`. It's an event, a log line ("world daemon: STALLED 5 min: …"), the dashboard's alert and a run report failure. The steps:
  - **Daedalus count stuck** (#228, step `daedalusCount`): an install while short of Daedalus's count that added none to it (NeuroFlux Governor's levels count once: the 2026-11-04 run's nine NeuroFlux-only installs at 23), at once, from the slow snapshots' `lastAugReset`, until the next install, if it passed over one that counts (#238). A6's commit while short records the facts in its note (`countFacts`): the money left after the set and its donations (what NeuroFlux Governor's levels and home's RAM then take), and each augmentation that would add to the count left within reach beyond the set (core's `countablesWithinReach`: a joined seller's reputation covers it, or a faction taking donations sells it; its prerequisites owned or queued; whatever its hacking value, so a rule that passes one over can't hide it), at its price after the set and any donation (from the seller's reputation now: a donation the set plans there isn't counted, so the money may be overstated). One whose money is no more than what was left after the set was passed over: A6's set, which takes every one that counts the money covers, should have had it. Its words: "Daedalus count stuck: an install at 23 of 30 augmentations installed added none to the count (NeuroFlux Governor's levels count once), passing over Neural Wit Amplifier (BitRunners, $10,000,000), Magnetism Amplifier (The Black Hand, $250,000,000), which the money left after the set ($…) would have bought". A6's own install (its install asked for, `installAskedFor`) whose commit kept no facts is stuck too, as it can't be judged clean: "…, A6's install, with no facts from its commit of what counts within reach". The check guards A6's choice of set, not its reach: a shop missing from core's data, or a wrong seller, would hide itself from both.
    Otherwise it isn't stuck: early in a BitNode nothing that counts may be within reach (the extended augmentations runs' second install, at 6 with CyberSec's five owned), and A6's NeuroFlux-only install raises the multipliers that reach the next factions. That's a `daedalusCountNeuroFluxOnly` event and log line, section 3's information: "installed NeuroFlux Governor only at 6 of 30 augmentations installed: nothing that counts within reach"; or "… nothing that counts within its money: the cheapest within reach, Neurotrainer II (NiteSec, $45,000,000), costs more than the $10,000,000 left after the set"; or, for an install with no facts from A6's commit and its install not asked for (yours, the switch off, or an earlier brain's note), "… no facts from A6's commit (yours, the switch off, or an earlier brain's), so not judged". The path's line says the reach (the joined factions, those inviting, the hacker factions, Tian Di Hui at hacking 50 and the city group chosen), the count once: "Daedalus: not joined; 23 of 30 augmentations installed, 15 more within reach (The Black Hand 1, BitRunners 1, Tian Di Hui 8, Chongqing 1, New Tokyo 1, Ishima 3)";
  - Daedalus's requirements met and not joined: 5 minutes;
  - The Red Pill reached (or queued) and not installed: 15 minutes from "reached" (`RED_PILL_INSTALL_STALLED_AFTER_MS`), A6's whole commit (the set, home's RAM and NeuroFlux Governor a purchase at a time, the faction state's next read, the gate and a backup);
  - The Red Pill short at a joined Daedalus and its reputation not risen for 10 minutes (`RED_PILL_REPUTATION_STALLED_AFTER_MS`, #215 review): A6 holds every install meanwhile, so nothing earning it (the owner's work, or the faction work's switch off) would stall the run unbounded;
  - w0r1d_d43m0n at its level and the BitNode not destroyed, with what's missing (root, say): 5 minutes. Not with the switch off: then the destroy is the owner's.

  Faction work for The Red Pill that stalls is A5's `factionWorkStalled`.
- **The dashboard's World Daemon section** shows the path in the brain's words:
  - "Daedalus: joined; 31 of 30 augmentations installed";
  - "The Red Pill: Daedalus's reputation 1,000,000 of 2,500,000 at 40.0/s: about 10.4 h";
  - "w0r1d_d43m0n: rooted; hacking 2,000 of 3,000: about 20.1 h at the measured experience rate";
  - the destroy's state, into which BitNode (#321; a replay said), a stall in red, and the switch.
- **Off in the tests** that queue their own destroys: the destroy, backend and safe mode simulator suites, and every brain in `effectors.e2.test.ts` (`worldDaemon.enabled` false, unless a test's `brainSettings` say otherwise).

### Levelling: the hacking level all that's left

Planner v0's first decision ([#336](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/336), [owner decision 67](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md): the planner chooses, computed rules propose and execute). `src/levelling.ts` is the brain's side; core's `levelling.ts` the formulas and the choice, which the run report shares.
- **When:** The Red Pill installed and w0r1d_d43m0n rooted, with only its hacking level missing (3,000 in BN1, 9,000 in BN4), and the World Daemon's switch on (`levelAllThatsLeft`, on A8's path).
- **The candidates,** each scored by its estimated time to the level (core's `levellingEstimates`), every rate scaled with the level as `level + 50` (an operation's time, `Hacking.ts:60-80`):
  - **batching for money,** as before, at its measured experience rate;
  - **levelling mode:** grow or weaken loops on the server, and with the operation, that earn the most experience a thread-second at its minimum security (core's `chooseLevellingTarget`, computed: joesguns' grow in BN1 and BN4, 1.25 times its weaken, 3.2 hacks' time against 4 at the same 0.15 GB), on the batcher's hosts and home's RAM beyond what it keeps for the Agent and its workers (`homeKeptGbOf`, the share threads' reservation; the owner confirmed home's use on 2026-11-10, and #340 makes home a batch host too). The grow goes where the medium snapshot shows the target at its minimum security, whatever its money (`levellingFiguresOf`'s `mayGrow`), the weaken elsewhere: below its maximum money a grow adds 0.004 security a thread used (`Server/ServerHelpers.ts:201-212`), but one round on its threads fills it, a weaken round takes the security back, and at its maximum money a grow moves no money, so adds no security, for good. At most a flip or two, each losing a round in flight (the simulator suite: weaken, grow, weaken, grow). (c) after an install takes the grow's rate after that preparation (core's `levellingPreparationMs`: a weaken from its starting security, a grow round from its starting money, `Server/Server.ts:74-76`, a weaken back, at the levels' level and speed; 2.15 minutes on the BN4.1-shaped fixture, its experience left out, cautious). The fit predicts for the threads running: the hosts' as sent, home's as the fast snapshot shows them. The formula's rate, `(3 + 0.3 × base difficulty) × hacking_exp` a thread over the operation's time (`Hacking.ts:30-38`, `:83-94`), times the factor fitted from its measured rate (1, optimistic, until measured);
  - **augmentations, then an install:** the NeuroFlux Governor levels the money buys at A6's prices and reputation, donations included (`neuroFluxOffer`, core's `affordableCounts`), and (#355) the set's augmentations, bought before the levels, from any shop, that raise the level's, the experience's or the speed's multiplier (`installAugmentationsOffer`, core's `addsToLevelling`). Under the destroy's hold A6's set is only what adds to levelling (#355's review): any other purchase would raise each later price 1.9 times for nothing, fewer NeuroFlux Governor levels after it. The gang's shop never sells NeuroFlux Governor (`FactionHelpers.tsx:172-178`), so in BN2 the set alone can be the offer, and the install then goes with the set alone. Their multipliers multiply in (core's `levellingInstallMultipliers`: the game merges each installed augmentation's by product, `AugmentationHelpers.ts:39-50`, `Multipliers.ts:71-79`). Then the install's own 15 minutes (`LEVELLING_INSTALL_MS`), w0r1d_d43m0n's root again (`LEVELLING_REROOT_MS`, 3.4 minutes: one live measurement, BN4.1's on 2026-11-10, the TOR router at 1.9 minutes after The Red Pill's install, the port openers at 3.2), then levelling mode from zero experience at the levels' and the set's multipliers, on home's RAM alone (cautious: the cloud servers come back later, and the experience while it's rooted again is left out). The dashboard lists the install's augmentations ("the install's augmentations: NeuroFlux Governor ×0; the set's …"), and the `levellingChosen` event carries them (`installAugmentations`), so the run report scores the install as the brain did.
- **The choice:** the shortest, once it's 1.25 times shorter than the one it's on (`LEVELLING_SWITCH_GAIN`, as the batcher's move); money first, until its rate is measured (a minute). Each rate is measured once its mode has settled, the brain's first update too: a minute and the longest weaken still landing (`settleMsOf`). Levelling's only while its loop runs (the state sent levels with the hack loop on, and the fast snapshot shows its threads on home), and it settles again as it starts; an install resets every rate and fit.
- **What it changes:** levelling, the hack loop's `levelling` (the protocol's `hackLoop.levelling { homeKeptGb, operation }`) in the batcher's place: the level or grow loop on every host and on home (ingame's `level-loop.js`, `level-grow.js`); the dashboard's hack loop says `levelling`, and no income is expected of it (`incomeStalled` neither alerts nor fails then). The install: A6 commits it at once, `reason` "the hacking level", buys the set and the levels, and installs through the gate. After the install, the choice starts again from money, as w0r1d_d43m0n needs rooting again: the next install must pay by the same score.
- **Said:** each choice is a `levellingChosen` event and a log line ("levelling: batching for money to levelling mode: batching for money about 2307.7 h, levelling mode about 62 min, …"); the dashboard's World Daemon section adds the candidates, the choice and the fitted factor.
- **The run report** (section 3, the path) shows the candidates from the window's last snapshots, the brain's choice from its events, and the factor, its measured rate against the formula's. **It fails** (section 4) a brain off the shortest past the 1.25 times for longer than 10 minutes (`LEVELLING_OFF_SHORTEST_FAILS_AFTER_MS`, the rates' window), or whose choice's estimate is past the time the BitNode has taken so far: a hopeless one, once the level has been all that's left 10 minutes in the BitNode, added up across its levelling installs (#355's review: installs every few minutes can't keep it passing). Estimates past a million hours are given to 3 figures ("about 1.28e+193 h"). With the World Daemon's switch off, both are warnings: the destroy isn't wanted. A fixture shaped like BN4.1's last snapshot (2026-11-10) fails both: batching for money about 2,308 h, levelling mode about 62 min, 27 h into the BitNode.

### The levelling suite

`test/levelling-simulation.test.ts` (#336) runs the brain against the real engine, its rules as on Steam, home at 4 TB, the hacking multiplier about 4 (augmentations given installed), The Red Pill queued: A6 installs it, then the level at 2,000 of 3,000 and $1t (harness edits). With A6 off, the brain buys the port openers, roots w0r1d_d43m0n, measures batching's rate and chooses levelling mode on joesguns; the level loop weakens joesguns from its starting security, then the grow loop takes over at its minimum (weaken, grow, weaken, grow), on the hosts and on home beyond its 32 GB, and the fitted factor, the grow's for the threads running, is 0.953, 0.953, 0.953 and 1.008 over 10 minutes on seeds 1 to 4 (the 5% under on three seeds has no known cause; it's well inside the choice's 1.25 times, so it changes no decision). With A6 on, CyberSec's reputation and the money buy NeuroFlux Governor's levels: the install is chosen for the hacking level, and after it the choice is money. BN2's case (#355): the gang formed with NiteSec, The Red Pill queued and installed, then NiteSec at 1m reputation and $1t (harness edits), so its shop's hacking augmentations are within reach and no NeuroFlux Governor is. Once w0r1d_d43m0n is rooted the planner chooses the install with the set alone (7 min 10 s in; its estimate about 8.31e+80 h against levelling mode's 1.28e+193 h, both hopeless at BN2's ×0.8 from a fresh level), A6 buys 13 at NiteSec, only what adds to levelling, and installs at 8 min 32 s, the gang kept; the run report scores the install with the set, and passes. Then $1t again for the port openers: rooted again at 9 min 55 s, levelling mode, and once the level-only time adds up past 10 minutes across the install (by 14 min 55 s), the report fails the hopeless estimate (about 4.43e+80 h, 14 min 34 s into the BitNode), as it must.
