Seed 1 (about 4 s) and BN2's case (about 8 s) run in CI; `LEVELLING_SEEDS` runs other seeds. `test/levelling-parity.test.ts` checks the formulas against `ns.formulas`.

### The time to the destroy

Planner v0's one score ([#360](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/360), [owner decision 67](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md)'s points 2 and 5: the estimated time to the BitNode's destroy, and the measure is the estimate against the outcome). Core's `destroy-estimate.ts` is the estimate, pure, so the brain, the run report and the campaign report call the same function; `src/destroy-estimate.ts` is the brain's rule, `src/destroy-estimate-report.ts` the reports'.
- **The estimate:** remaining = A + BC + D, the total the time online so far plus it, each term with its time, its source and how it bears on the total (done, optimistic, cautious, or either way). Daedalus's route alone: BN1, BN4 and BN5 (`DESTROY_ESTIMATE_BITNODES`); elsewhere none, said once in the log ("its route isn't Daedalus's": BN2's gang is #357, Black Ops #359).
  - **A, Daedalus's count** (N, `DaedalusAugsRequirement`): 0 once met; else t·(N − c)/c, the BitNode's mean (c the count installed, `daedalusCountOf`'s, NeuroFlux Governor once; t the BitNode's time online), fitted online; at the start (c = 0), the prior's. Either way: optimistic after a big early cycle (BN1.4's c3-c5, 0.30-0.53 of what was left), cautious where later factions bring more (BN4.1's c3-c7, 1.7-2.7); within ×2 of what was left at 27 of the store's 31 installs.
  - **BC, the invitation and The Red Pill:** before Daedalus is joined, the prior's B + C less the time since the count was met, never under the prior's Red Pill cycle (either way); once joined, A5's ETA for the hold (its first choice there, to the favour target: with the share if it runs at the choice; cautious, the store's 1.2-2.6× the hold they timed: BN1.2's and BN1.3's 157 and 184 min against 62 and 72, BN1.4's 35 against 30) plus the prior's Red Pill cycle (cautious: the level's rise is left out); after the donations install (the install after the join, or Daedalus's favour at the donations' threshold), the prior's Red Pill cycle; 0 once installed.
  - **D, w0r1d_d43m0n's level** (3000 × the BitNode's `WorldDaemonDifficulty`, core's `worldDaemonRequiredLevel`: 3,000 in BN1, 9,000 in BN4, 4,500 in BN5): once The Red Pill is installed, levelling's chosen candidate's estimate (#336); before, the prior's D if its level is this BitNode's ("levelling not yet chosen" after The Red Pill), else none (optimistic: the total is "at least"). A prior whose first levelling choice came later than its Red Pill's install plus the re-root, the settle and a slow gap (`LEVELLING_PROMPT_MS`, 7.4 min: `LEVELLING_REROOT_MS`, `LEVELLING_SETTLE_MS`, `AUGMENTATION_SLOW_GAP_MS`; the store doesn't record the root itself), or not at all, has its D from before #336, the money pace's (BN4.1's 419 min: #336's brain chose 4 min before the destroy, after 409 at the money pace): cautious, said so.
  - **The prior:** the last destroyed BitNode in the store, the same number first (BN4.1's is BN1.4: no BN4 before it), its stretches measured by the campaign fold (`destroyStretchesOf`: each cycle's time online, the count at each cycle's first look, The Red Pill's first install and the destroy; a save's cycle left out). With none, its terms are none and the total "at least".
  - **Left out, each labelled in #360:** B's hacking 2,500 and $100b by the formulas, and C with the level's rise (the prior's until the next slice); install timing's per-cycle shape for A (right for its choice, wrong for the total: 50 h at BN4.1's c3, when 12.7 h were left); D before The Red Pill by the formulas (BN2's 15,000 and BN5's 4,500 need it); restarts and offline time (the campaign's online clock); a prior scaled by the multipliers.
- **When:** at the BitNode's start (its first slow snapshot), each install (a new `lastAugReset`), Daedalus first joined with A5's choice for The Red Pill there (the moment the hindcast keys on: its first `factionWorkChosen`), and levelling's first choice with an estimate (none apart when the cycle's first estimate already has it). A brain with no note for the BitNode (a first deploy mid-BitNode) records its first look as "first seen" unless it's the store's first slow snapshot of the BitNode, since the hindcast has that start or install already: listed, left out of the summaries. A `lastAugReset` earlier than the noted (a save loaded) isn't estimated again. Each a `destroyEstimated` event (every term, the BitNode's time online, the count, N, the level, the prior) and a log line ("destroy estimate: the destroy (#360), at install with 1 h 28 min online: 9 h 1 min to go, 10 h 29 min in all; A 7 h 20 min (either way: the BitNode's mean: 5 of 30 …), …"); a term that's never (a rate of 0) is stored as `never`, with no time.
- **The BitNode's time online** is the campaign's clock (`advanceOnlineClock` keyed by its `lastNodeReset`), seeded from the store when the rule first sees a BitNode (`readDestroySeed`: the campaign fold over every slow snapshot's `resetInfo`, as `npm run campaign` reads them, once a BitNode), which also gives the prior.
- **Restarts:** the setting `destroyEstimate.last` (the BitNode's and the last estimated cycle's resets, the moments made, the clock, the prior and the last estimate) keeps a restarted brain from repeating one. An install made while the brain was down is estimated at the first slow snapshot after it, and marked late (that snapshot more than a slow gap, 3 minutes, after the install).
- **The dashboard:** the World Daemon section shows the latest estimate after the levelling lines ("time to the destroy (#360), at …"), once this run has seen a slow snapshot of its BitNode.
- **The run report** (section 3, the path) lists each estimate in the window. **It fails** (section 4) on an estimate with a term that's never (a rate of 0: hopeless), at once, or an event it can't read; with the World Daemon's switch off, warnings. Every other estimate is reported, not judged (#360 amended): a short history (harness-made, a save loaded) or the prior's B + C (4.21× at BN1.4's count met) would break a bar on the whole estimate against the time online so far, so its ratio in the campaign report is the measure; #336's bar stays on levelling's own estimate. It also fails where a static snapshot's w0r1d_d43m0n level differs from the BitNode's by its multipliers (`worldDaemonRequiredLevel`).
- **The campaign report's section** (`npm run campaign`, last): a row per estimate, the brain's own and, before the brain's first, hindcast from the store with the same function: at each start and install (a BitNode whose start the store saw), and at Daedalus's join (A5's first choice for The Red Pill there, its ETA as it was then), reading nothing after its moment (the campaign fold as it stood, the events before it). Each row: the BitNode, when, the moment, the time online at it, the estimated remaining and total, the actual (its time online at the destroy; "open" before it), the ratio (estimated over actual remaining) and the terms. Then each BitNode's median and worst ratio (furthest from 1 by its factor), the ratios by moment, and how many are within ×1.25, ×1.5 and ×2 (Q9, [#39](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/39)); the summaries take only the measured rows: an estimate that's "at least" is a bound, a row with under 10 min actually left is too coarse (`SHORT_REMAINING_MS`: a slow snapshot a minute makes every time ±1 min, over ±10% below 10 min), and a first look is the hindcast's install again; each is listed, flagged, and left out.
- **Tests:** core's `test/destroy-estimate.test.ts` (w0r1d_d43m0n's level for BitNodes 1-15 and BN12's by its level; each term's cases: met, c = 0, no prior, D's prior at the same level only, joined, the donations install, never; and the golden cases from #360's tables: A at every install of BN1.2-BN4.1, ratios asserted, and the whole estimate at BN1.3's, BN1.4's and BN4.1's starts, count met and joins); the brain's `test/destroy-estimate.test.ts` (the fold's stretches over the four BitNodes rebuilt cycle by cycle from the findings' tables, NeuroFlux Governor counted once, a save loaded, an open BitNode; the prior; the hindcast reading nothing after its moment, the store cut there or changed after it; the brain's own rows from its first on; the campaign report's section; the rule at each moment, once across a restart, a missed install marked late, a term never, outside the route; the store's seed; the run report: an estimate reported, not judged, a term never failing, the switch off, and the level against the multipliers); and `test/bn5-path-simulation.test.ts` (#350's BN5 path: the estimates at the start and the install, and at The Red Pill's cycle, at 4,500).

### The World Daemon suite

`test/world-daemon-simulation.test.ts` runs the brain against the real engine, its rules as on Steam, from a harness-edited late game to the destroy:
- **Before the Agent:** 30 augmentations installed, hacking past 2500, and $200b. The game invites Daedalus, and A5 joins it, with no install chosen meanwhile.
- **The Red Pill:** a harness edit puts Daedalus just short of it. A5 works for it, and A6 installs it, chosen for The Red Pill and bought last.
- **The destroy:** a harness edit stands in for the hours back to hacking 3000, with $1t. The brain buys the port openers, the hack loop roots w0r1d_d43m0n, and the World Daemon rule destroys BN1 into BN4, the campaign order's next (#321), through the gate and a backup.
- **After:** the next Agent connects in BN4 with SF1.1, and the run report passes. Its section 3 has the install and the destroy done by the reset that followed each (#286): neither worker lives to report.

Seed 1 runs in CI (7.8 virtual minutes); `WORLD_DAEMON_SEEDS` (`1,2,3`) runs others.

### The BN4 suite

`test/bn4-start-simulation.test.ts` (#321) runs the brain against the real engine from a BN4.1 start with SF1.3, as the campaign's order enters BN4 from BN1.4, for one virtual hour (about 8 s of real time), its rules as on Steam and the simulator's own effectors taken away, so the Agent picks its backends as in a browser. It roots 19 servers, the batcher batches n00dles, money rises with no income stall, and every action (the TOR router, BruteSSH.exe and FTPCrack.exe, CSEC's backdoor, CyberSec's and Tian Di Hui's joins, the travel to Ishima) goes through Singularity, at ×1 RAM in BN4 beside the Agent on SF1's 32 GB home. Its run report passes, with nothing taken as BN1's in BN4. With BN4's `ScriptHackMoney` and `HackExpGain` folded in (#321's review), what the batcher's hacks landed ($3.72m) is what the money sources say hacking earned ($993/s over 59 minutes), and hacking is 6.5% of BN4's own batcher ceiling ($15,225/s), against 19.4% for BN1 from the same start: both batch n00dles all hour, BN4's n00dles has a ninth of BN1's most money (`ServerMaxMoney` 0.1125), and the BN4 run's hosts held 524 GB, five times the BN1 run's. Before the fold the hour earned $671/s (the hacks planned for five times their take). One seed, bounded: a thin slice, and the live run shows the rest.

### The BN5 suite

`test/bn5-start-simulation.test.ts` (#323) runs the brain the same way from a BN5.1 start with SF1.3 and SF4.3, as the campaign's order enters BN5 from BN4.3, for one virtual hour (about 14 s of real time). Singularity is at ×1 RAM with SF4.3. It roots 69 servers, the batcher batches n00dles then joesguns, hacking reaches 303, and every action with a Singularity backend goes through it (the TOR router, the programs, home RAM to 256 GB, the backdoors, six faction joins, the travels); the 25 cloud servers, bought and upgraded at BN5's prices (128 GB at the softcap's 1.2: $8,448,000), go through their own worker. Its run report passes, with nothing taken as BN1's. What the batcher's hacks landed ($1.55b) is 1.22 times what the money sources say hacking earned over the window ($351,414/s over 59 minutes): the batcher's count runs a minute past the last slow snapshot while the income climbs with the cloud servers bought from minute 47. Hacking is 18.6% of BN5's batcher ceiling. The World Daemon's destroy would go into BN2 (since #334). One seed, bounded.

`test/bn5-path-simulation.test.ts` (#348, from #346's check) covers the two stretches of BN5.1 that the hour doesn't reach, from the same start and the same way. Each is one seed, about 4 s of real time. Coverage only: neither found a defect.
- **An install at BN5's prices** (`AugmentationMoneyCost` 2, the first BitNode the brain plays with one other than 1). The ×2 is checked three ways: core's table, core's price against BN1's, and the game's own `getAugCost` (what Singularity's `getAugmentationPrice` returns). A minute in, harness edits stand in for the work: CyberSec joined with 18,750 reputation, and the money at BN5's prices for its five, home's RAM and a NeuroFlux Governor level, $3m over. The planner holds the install until 10 minutes of slow snapshots measure the income, then installs now for Daedalus's count. The brain buys the five in buy order through Singularity for $894,341,800 (twice BN1's $447,170,900; Gen I $140,000,000), each at core's price, which is the game's unqueued price × 1.9 per queued one. Then it buys home's RAM, 32 to 128 GB, and NeuroFlux Governor's first level ($37,141,485), and installs through the gate and a save backup, 665 s after the edit. The next Agent connects, home keeps its RAM, and the run report passes with nothing through the page.
- **The destroy at BN5's World Daemon** (4,500: BN1's 3,000 × `WorldDaemonDifficulty` 1.5, read from the static snapshot). The Red Pill is installed and w0r1d_d43m0n rooted before the Agent (destroy-restart's `toTheRedPill`), with home at 64 GB so the 33.9 GB destroy worker fits. At hacking 3,988, past BN1's bar, the rule holds: "hacking level 3988: w0r1d_d43m0n needs 4500". Past 4,500 (a harness edit), the brain's own destroy goes through the gate and a backup into BN2.1, seen in a slow snapshot 35 s after the edit (owner decision 66, core's `nextBitNodeOf`). It goes through Singularity's `destroyW0r1dD43m0n`, which enters the next BitNode without the BitVerse. SF5.1 is gained beside SF1.3 and SF4.3, the next Agent connects, and the run report passes: "BN5.1 … destroyed after 2 min 19 s, into BN2.1".

## Stopping at the BitNode's end

The owner's requests of 2026-11-07 ([#303](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/303)): leave a run going and have the brain stop by itself at the BitNode's end, rather than play on, and close the game too if asked. `src/bitnode-end.ts` is the rule and the shutdown's timing; the controller runs the rule, and main.ts shuts down.
- **The switch:** the dashboard's "The BitNode's end" section (`stopAtBitNodeEnd`, `POST /api/stop-at-bitnode-end` with `{"setting": …}`): `off` (the default, "Play on"), `brain` ("Stop the brain"), or `brainAndGame` ("Stop the brain and close the game"). It takes effect at once, survives restarts, and the start line lists it in the buttons' words ("at the BitNode's end: play on").
- **Once** (#303 review): as the brain shuts down at the BitNode's end, the switch is set back to "play on" ("the stop at the BitNode's end set back to play on: set it again for the next BitNode's end"; the event's `settingAfter`), so it stops this BitNode's end only, and a brain started again to look around plays on. Set it again for the next. While a close is awaited it stays set, so "Play on" calls the close off (`gameCloseCalledOff`): the brain then stops alone. Ctrl+C mid-stop sets it back too (`BitNodeEndShutdown.interrupted`); a crash can't, so a brain started again at the same end stops (and closes) again.
- **When:**
  - **Destroyed:** the next BitNode's first slow snapshot, which main.ts's recorder has stored by then, so `npm run campaign` has its start and says the last one was destroyed. The next BitNode is another `lastNodeReset` (a destroy's prestige sets it, `PersonObjects/Player/PlayerObjectGeneralMethods.ts:173`; an install or a soft reset only `lastAugReset`, `:126`), not before the last slow snapshot's game time (else a save loaded, as the campaign model tells one), with a destroy's reset note out (the brain's or the owner's queued `destroyBitNode`, #175, across a brain restart too), or else the old BitNode's Source-File a level up (a destroy by hand in the game below the cap, `RedPill.tsx:16-52`). The note is what tells the brain's replay with nothing playable left in the campaign's order to gain (#311, #321), which gains no level: "BN1.4 destroyed, BN1.4 seen again (a replay: no Source-File level gained)". b1t_flum3 gives none (`RedPill.tsx:62-63`), and at the cap a destroy by hand gives none either, so with no note neither stops the brain.
  - **Never on** an install, a soft reset, a save loaded (an older BitNode's, or this one's), b1t_flum3, a new game, or a restart mid-BitNode. A destroy seen while the switch is off is forgotten.
- **Stopping:** one line, "stopping at the BitNode's end (the switch is on): BN1.3 destroyed, BN1.4 seen" (or, after a replay, "…: BN1.4 destroyed, BN1.4 seen again (a replay: no Source-File level gained)"), and `brainStoppedAtBitNodeEnd`. Then, for `brain`, main.ts's `shutDown` a second on (`BitNodeEndShutdown`; the dashboard's reply to the switch that asked goes first), as on Ctrl+C: the data backup stopped, the lock removed, exit 0, and `npm run brain`'s runner ends with it (`restart-on-crash.ts` never restarts an exit 0). The game goes on: the in-game scripts keep running, and the Agent stays in [safe mode](#safe-mode) (none after a destroy, which drops the kept state), retrying the brain.
- **Closing the game too** (`brainAndGame`): the controller queues `closeGame` (core's intent, the `ui` backend's `close-game.js`, [packages/ingame](../ingame/README.md#closing-the-game)) after the owner's queue and before the brain's resets, through the irreversible gate (once everything queued before it is done, as for a reset, two medium snapshots, then a save backup), source `bitNodeEnd`. The worker opens Options, clicks the game's own "Save Game", waits for its "Game Saved!" toast, then calls `window.close()`. As it goes out, the controller logs it and stores `gameCloseSent`, and supervision stops (`BitNodeEndShutdown`), so the game isn't relaunched. Each try the Agent reports (it tries again every 30 s, or 2 s on after a worker that couldn't start, so all 3 tries can go in seconds) is logged and stored as `gameCloseTried`. The brain shuts down once the Agent's connection drops after the close and no Agent connects within 15 s (`gameClosed`, logged "the game closed: its Agent's connection dropped 0.4 s after the close went out, and no Agent came back within 15 s"). An Agent back within 15 s means the window stayed open (a reload, or the Agent restarted). That, or no drop within 60 s of the close going out or of its last try reported, or the close not out within 5 minutes of the stop: it logs "game close NOT CONFIRMED: …", stores `gameCloseNotConfirmed` and shuts down anyway. The close is withdrawn after 3 tries, once the window stayed open (`CLOSE_GAME_WINDOW_STAYED_OPEN`), or once the Agent went after it went out, so it's never a loop of saves and closes, nor sent to the game's next start. **The manual queue refuses `closeGame`** (400, naming the switch): queued by hand, the Agent went before reporting it done, so it stayed queued, and with supervision on the game was relaunched and closed again. One in a store written before is dropped the same way.
  - **Steam (run live on BN1.4, 2026-11-08, #308):** `window.close()` from the page goes through Chromium's own close of the window, not BrowserWindow's `close`, so the game's `closingWindowHandler` (`vendor/bitburner-src/electron/main.js:43-78`) didn't run: Electron's `main.log`, at debug for this run, had no "Destroying the window" (`:77`) and no save-folder write at the close (the relaunch's "Current save data is the freshest", also debug, shows the level). The last window closed still quit the app (`window-all-closed`, `:16-20`); the beforeunload prompt is off on `file://` (`index.tsx:53-57`). The page has no other way to quit: the preload exposes only the save and export channels. **Fresh:** the game's own save in its IndexedDB, written before the toast shows (`SaveObject.ts:246`, `db.ts:122-125`): the relaunch read its `lastSave` and kept it ("Current save data is the freshest", `electron/storage.js:481-496`); and the brain's save backup from the gate. **Stale whatever the brain does:** the save-folder copy. Its debounce (`main.js:151-163`: 5 minutes, leading and trailing, no `maxWait`) writes the session's first save at once, then only once saves pause, which they never do while the game autosaves every 60 s (`Settings/Settings.ts:17`, `engine.tsx:233`): the BN1.4 session wrote it once. That matters only on another PC, or if the game's storage is wiped. **Steam Cloud** refused every write on the owner's PC ("Cannot write Steam Cloud save file", since at least 2026-11-06), closing or not: the game's or Steam's doing. Working, its own debounce (`:135-149`, 15 minutes, leading) would leave it stale the same way. **To see the handler run (optional):** "Destroying the window" and "Saving to Disk" (`:153`) are `log.debug`, and the file log is at info by default (`electron/utils.js:136-138`), where only "Quitting the app..." shows, either way; the game's **Debug → File Log Level → debug** (`electron/menu.js:325-334`) shows them, the log under **File → Open Directory → Open Logs Directory** (`:200-201`).
  - **A browser (E2's Chromium)** refuses `window.close()` on a window no script opened ("Scripts may close only the windows that were opened by them"): the game saves, the window stays, and the worker says so. `effectors.e2.test.ts` asserts exactly that.
- **The run report:** section 1 lists the stop, the close, each try, a call-off and its end, and ends "the run's end: the brain stopped itself at …, at the BitNode's end (the switch on): …. Expected, not a failure: …"; a silence or disconnect that follows any of those events is expected, not a gap, so a window across the stop and a later start passes. A replay's destroy in section 3: "destroy sent (the brain's), into BitNode 1 (BN6, BN7, … skipped: …), a replay (the campaign's order has nothing playable left to gain (Source-File 1 at 3, Source-File 2 at 3, Source-File 4 at 3, Source-File 5 at 3), but the run)" (#321, #323; a store from before it has #311's "Source-File 1 is at 3, nothing to gain but the run"). In a store from before #311 only (the brain emits neither now), the `nextBitNode` stall a stop for nothing more to gain followed is expected, not a failure; with none, the owner's prompt (#308, [section 4](#the-run-report)). The window ends at the run's last record (#308). At each confirmed close (`gameClosed`, #308), `closeGame` counts as done ("done: the game closed"): its worker dies with the window, so it's never seen done. Each other intent still in the Agent's list at its last look before that close's drop is left by the close: counted, not listed as not done. `gameCloseNotConfirmed` is a warning (WARN).
- **Tests:** `test/bitnode-end.test.ts` (the switch, `destroyedBetween`, the controller's stop on a destroy, the replay at the cap among them, and not on an install, a soft reset, a save loaded, b1t_flum3 or a restart, the close's gate, withdrawal and the owner's close, and `BitNodeEndShutdown`'s timing), `test/brain-process.test.ts` (the brain under `npm run brain`'s runner, set mid-run, a destroy seen: exit 0, no restart, the lock gone; and the start line at the cap), `test/destroy-restart.test.ts` (the replay in the simulator: the brain's own destroy at SF1.3 into BN1, SF1 left at 3, the stop on the next BN1.4, and the campaign report's "destroyed (at the cap)"), `test/run-report.test.ts` and `test/dashboard-*.test.ts`.

## The gang (BN2)

Slices G1 ([#325](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/325)), G2, ascension ([#328](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/328)), G3, equipment ([#330](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/330)), and G4, territory warfare ([#332](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/332)), of the gangs epic [#324](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/324), built as BN1 was (owner decision 46): rules computed from the game's formulas, no fast model. The mechanics are in [`docs/mechanics/gang-bladeburner-sleeves-stanek.md`](../../docs/mechanics/gang-bladeburner-sleeves-stanek.md) §1. `src/gang.ts` is the rule's brain side, core's `planGang` (`packages/core/src/gang-rule.ts`) the rule itself, and `src/gang-report.ts` the run report's section.

- **Only in BN2, for now.** The campaign plays gangs there (BN2 ×3, [`docs/synthesis.md`](../../docs/synthesis.md) §3), where a gang needs no karma (`PersonObjects/Player/PlayerObjectGangMethods.ts:16-18`). Elsewhere, with SF2, it needs −54,000 karma, which the brain doesn't seek; outside BN2 the desired state carries no gang, and nothing is read. No switch: in BN2 the gang is the brain's to play.
- **The faction** (core's `gangFactionToForm`): the first gang faction joined. The brain reaches NiteSec first, by A3's backdoor on avmnite-02h (hacking 202-220, `Server/data/servers.ts:1468-1485`); The Black Hand's server needs 340-365, and the five combat factions need combat stats and karma the brain doesn't train. With several joined, a combat one: its top respect task, Terrorism, scales with territory to the power 2 against Cyberterrorism's 1, ×14 at the starting 1/7 territory (`Gang/formulas/formulas.ts:26`, `AllGangs.ts:11-40`).
- **Forming:** the `createGang` intent, irreversible (only one gang a BitNode, and the faction's reputation goes to 0, `PlayerObjectGangMethods.ts:56-68`), so it goes through the gate: its guard, the faction joined, on two medium snapshots, then a save backup. Its worker uses the NS API, which needs no Singularity.
- **Each member's task** (core's `planGang`, every medium snapshot, from the gang worker's reading):
  - **The objective:** while recruiting matters (under 12 members), respect less wanted level a cycle, the respect taken with no wanted penalty. The penalty, respect / (respect + wanted) (`Gang.ts:357-359`), settles in the long run at about 1 − W/R, R the gang's respect a cycle before the penalty and W its wanted (about: the penalty sits inside the respect's softcap exponent, 0.83 in BN2 at 1/7 territory, `formulas.ts:27`), so what the gang keeps of a member is about its respect less its wanted. Scored with the penalty at 1, a member's choice doesn't swing with the wanted level of the moment, which wanted control looks after. With 12: money, from the tasks whose respect covers their wanted. Each task's figures are the game's formulas (`formulas.ts:15-73`), at the gang's territory.
  - **The objective at 12, the planner's** ([#357](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/357)'s first slice, core's `gangObjectiveChoice`, `src/gang-route.ts`): respect or money, each scored by the time to The Red Pill's reputation at the gang's faction (`augmentationCost`'s, `Augmentation/Augmentations.ts:1946-1952`, less the faction's now, `gangRouteFactionOf`). Only respect earns it: respect gained × `faction_rep` × (1 + favour / 100) / 75 (`Gang/Gang.ts:144-155`). Each objective's rate is its own plan's (core's `planGang` given the objective, its training, ascension, justice and warfare with it): the members' respect a cycle at the gang's penalty now, × 5 cycles a second. The members' growth is left out (either way), bonus time's faster cycles (cautious for both) and what money buys toward it (equipment, a `faction_rep` set at an install: cautious for money). The least wins, a tie to money; once The Red Pill is bought (A6's queue), queued or installed it's money, as before; with the gang's faction unread, money (no estimate). A `gangObjectiveChosen` event at each change, with both estimates and the faction's figures ("gang: the objective at 12: respect, The Red Pill in 36 min (money's 4.3 h)" in the log, and on the dashboard's line). The A/B's baseline (`bn2Route` "rules", tests alone) keeps money. Under 12, the rule's respect.
  - **Training:** Train Hacking (or Train Combat in a combat gang) while a minute's training, then work until the member's next ascension, beats that time's work now, by the game's experience formula (`GangMember.ts:155-210`, `:70-72`). The horizon (G2) is the time to the next ascension (core's `gangTimeToAscensionMs`): working from now, how long until its ascension pays, at most a day (`GANG_ASCENSION_HORIZON_CAP_MS`). A recruit can't ascend (no experience over 1,000), so it trains as with a day's horizon; once its training passes 1,000, the next ascension comes into view and the horizon shortens.
  - **Ascension (G2):** a member ascends when it pays (core's `gangAscensionToMake`, before the tasks are set; the member then trains from its new level 1):
    - **What it gives:** each stat's points grow by its experience over 1,000, and its multiplier is √(points / 2,000), at least 1 (`formulas.ts:75-81`, `GangMember.ts:262-285`). The multiplier scales the level for the same experience and the experience a cycle (`GangMember.ts:70-79`, `:155-210`).
    - **What it costs:** the experience and levels go back to 0 and 1, the upgrades are lost and the equipment multipliers go back to the augmentations' (`GangMember.ts:298-320`; G3), and the gang loses the respect the member earned since its last ascension (`Gang.ts:390-393`, never below 1).
    - **The rule:** over a horizon, the ascended member's best output (training a share of it, then working, the best of sixteenths, `gangOutputOver`) less the member's as it is, against the cost (`gangAscensionCost`): its earned respect under the respect objective, plus the penalty's toll, the others' output lost to the lower penalty while they earn the respect back. The retraining's time is in the gain, as the ascended member starts at level 1. The horizon is the member's cycle so far, the next one taken as long (`gangCycleSoFarMs`): the training stats' experience over training's pace, plus its earned respect over its best work's respect a cycle. It's approximate: work's own experience counts as training time. The ascended member's own output is valued at the gang's respect after the ascension.
    - **When:** one a plan (every 10 s), the one with the most gain over cost. None that converts too little: the 1,000 experience it leaves out must be at most a fifth of what it converts (`GANG_ASCENSION_MOST_LEFT_OUT`, `gangAscensionConvertsEnough`), so a training stat's experience of 5,000 or more: ×√2 or more at the first ascension, √(1 + 4,000 / P) after it with P points. A floor under the rule's own measure, so a later change to the objective or the horizon can't let small ascensions through. None whose lost respect would take the share over the bound with the plan's recruits too, 1/(N + recruits), as the worker recruits before it ascends (so none while the share is over it already); none of a member on justice; none that would leave fewer recruits allowed than now (`gangRecruitsAllowed`); and none of a member whose owner-given name the protocol can't carry (over 64 characters), which the brain could never order. The game's own rule: a stat's experience over 1,000 (`GangMember.ts:245-248`).
    - **Done** by the gang worker (`ns.gang.ascendMember`, 4 GB) with the member's points as read (`pointsBefore`, core's `gangAscensionPointsOf`): the worker runs again 10 s later on the same state, and ascends only while the points are still those (`gangAscensionOrderStands`). Core computes the game's `getAscensionResult` from the points read (`gangAscensionResult`), checked against the engine's, so the worker doesn't call it (2 GB).
  - **Wanted control:** the wanted level never falls below 1 (`Gang.ts:164-166`), so the penalty's floor is respect / (respect + 1), and the share of output it takes above that floor is (wanted − 1) / (respect + wanted). A member on a justice task costs about its share, 1/N: the bound. Over it, members go to the justice task that lowers wanted most for each (Ethical Hacking or Vigilante Justice) until the wanted level, at that pace, is back at the bound within `GANG_WANTED_RECOVERY_MS` (23 min 6 s, one justice member's time to halve it), or every member is on justice. The pace is the game's: the members' gains and the justice members' decay, × (1 − 0.001 × J) each process (`Gang.ts:157-167`), so W' = g − 0.0001 J W a cycle (core's `gangWantedAfter`, `gangWantedRecovers`). The order: those the rule put on justice already stay (released as the wanted level fell, the course back would lengthen each round), then those training (their output is none either way), then the weakest working. **In bonus time** (the gang's stored cycles, `ns.gang.getBonusTime()`, 0 GB, in the reading as `bonusTimeMs`; over 2 s is bonus time, as the gang stores under 10 cycles between processes otherwise, `gangInBonusTime`) the game processes 25 cycles a call with the decay still once a call (`Gang.ts:100-104`, `:161`), 2.5 times slower a cycle, and the rule counts it so (`GANG_BONUS_CYCLES_PER_PROCESS`). The run report judges the same, and the outcome too (below). **Hysteresis:** members the rule put on justice stay there until the share is under half the bound (`GANG_JUSTICE_RELEASE`), so none flips at the bound every 10 s. One the owner put on justice by hand isn't the rule's, and goes back to work. The rule's own are kept in the store (`gang.placedOnJustice`), so a restarted brain leaves them where they are: with no slow snapshot (a brain's start, an Agent's disconnect or reconnect) the BitNode is unknown, and the rule waits with them kept (#365's review); only a BitNode read as another clears them.
- **Equipment (G3, [#330](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/330))** (core's `planGangEquipment`, once a medium snapshot, after the plan):
  - **What the game sells** (`Gang/data/upgrades.ts:34-227`, core's `GANG_EQUIPMENT`, checked against the engine's getters): 8 weapons, 4 armours, 4 vehicles and 5 rootkits, which an ascension loses, and 11 augmentations, which it keeps (`GangMember.ts:308-319`; an install keeps both, `Prestige.ts:133-147`). Each multiplies stat multipliers, so levels and training's experience (`GangMember.ts:141-150`, `:343-350`). A cost is the upgrade's over the discount, max(1, respect^0.01 + respect / 5e6 + power^0.01 + power / 1e6 − 1) (`Gang.ts:407-434`), `getGangInformation`'s `equipmentCostMult` its inverse.
  - **The rule** (`gangEquipmentAppraisal`): an item pays when, over a horizon, the money **the plan's task for the member** earns with it (the game's formula, the gang's wanted penalty in it) less without it beats its cost. **Dollars against dollars, the money that comes back** (#330's re-review): a member training, on justice or on a task that earns no money (most respect tasks: DDoS Attacks, Plant Virus, Cyberterrorism) gains nothing, so nothing is bought for it, augmentations included. Under 12 members, the respect objective, that's nearly every member; with 12, the money objective, members work the tasks that earn it (since #357 the objective at 12 is the planner's: under respect, as under 12, none). Respect's own worth is left out: the cautious side.
  - **Horizons:** a lost item only for a member the plan has working, whose ascension converts enough already (`gangAscensionConvertsEnough`), over G2's time to its next ascension (`nextAscensionMs`, at most a day): what it earns before the item is lost. One training earns nothing before the ascension that loses it, and G2's forecast for it, working from now, isn't when it ascends: valued over it in the BN2 hour's run, rootkits were bought ($42m) for members G2 gave 7 h, which ascended within the hour, and the gang ended with 1,309 respect against 1,348. An augmentation over a day (`GANG_AUGMENTATION_HORIZON_MS`, G2's cap): the longest the rule forecasts a member; the gain past it isn't counted. And only one paid back before the member's next ascension (#330's third review): after it the member trains from level 1, earning no money, and those on a money task are often the ones about to ascend. None for the member ascending now, or one whose ascension is due (held).
  - **Paid back within 15 minutes** (`GANG_EQUIPMENT_PAYBACK_LIMIT_MS`, #330's review), from that money, the cloud servers' rule (`CLOUD_SERVER_PAYBACK_LIMIT_MS`): gang equipment leaves alone what they leave alone (A6's set, held for an install within 15 minutes' more income; a program or home RAM, within a minute's), so it pays back as they must. Like a cloud server, a purchase can delay home RAM by about that long; one paying back slower could delay the install by more than A6's reserve allows (a $10b augmentation bought 25 minutes before an install the reserve doesn't hold yet).
  - **A lost item's lasting value is left out:** the faster training its multiplier gives raises what the next ascension converts (`GangMember.ts:141-150`): the cautious side.
  - **Which:** each member's best payback (gain over cost), one a member a plan, so the next plan values the next item on the member with this one; then, best first, those the money covers.
  - **The money** (the controller's `#gangEquipmentHold`, `gangMoneyHoldOf`): gang equipment is the brain's last purchase. It spends only money above the most of every hold the brain keeps for its other spending (what cloud servers leave alone, `#reserveForOtherPurchase`: the program or home RAM upgrade within a minute's income of its price, A6's augmentation reserve, Infinity once an install is chosen, and an invitation's money; and Daedalus's invitation's, the install countdown's and The Red Pill's donation's, as home RAM keeps them), plus, on top, the money spoken for (`gangPurchasesSpokenFor`): this round's purchase at its plan's price (a program's, home RAM's or a cloud server's), and the cloud server that pays back and waits for money (`CloudServersPlan.awaitingMoney`) while cloud servers would buy it (switched on, the hack loop on, no install chosen, no cloud problem holding them back). Sent as `keepMoney` (null for Infinity: none spent), worked out once a medium snapshot so the worker's runs don't change with each fast snapshot's holds, and again at once when an install is chosen, so none is spent from then on. **Its RAM:** `gang.js` (22.7 GB since G4) starts in home's free RAM. The batcher takes its own budget on home only with 8 GB beyond it (`HOME_KEPT_FOR_THE_AGENT_GB`), and its operations, on home since #340, and share threads leave `SHARE_HOME_KEPT_GB` (32 GB, with SF4 the largest Singularity worker's 33.9 too) free on home. A test keeps `gang.js` within that.
  - **Done** by the gang worker (`ns.gang.purchaseEquipment`, 4 GB, and `getServerMoneyAvailable`, 0.1 GB: `gang.js` 18.7 GB then, 22.7 since G4, with the Agent's 3.0 in BN2's 32 GB home) after the ascensions, each while the order stands: the member still one, its points still the reading's (`pointsBefore`: it hasn't ascended since), the item not owned, and the money less its cost (core's table × `equipmentCostMult`) at `keepMoney` or more (a cost not a number buys nothing). A stale order is skipped; one the game refuses is a problem. The worker calls none of the 2 GB getters.
  - **G2 counts the augmentations now:** `gangMemberAscended` keeps their multipliers, so the ascension appraisal and the member after an ascension include them.
- **Territory warfare (G4, [#332](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/332))** (core's `gangWarfareCrew` and `gangWarfareMeasure`, in `planGang`, every medium snapshot):
  - **What the game does:** at each territory update (every 100 cycles, 20 s, `Gang/data/Constants.ts:12`), the gang's power grows by 0.015 × max(0.002, territory) × the levels over 95 of its members on Territory Warfare (`Gang.ts:361-369`, `GangMember.ts:87-89`), and each other gang's at its own pace: half the time ×1.005 (at most +0.85), half the time 0.75 × a roll in [0.5, 1) × its territory × its multiplier, 1, 2 or 5 (`Gang.ts:191-204`, `Gang/data/power.ts`). While engaged (the clash chance 1, falling 0.01 an update once disengaged, `:210-216`), the gang clashes with each other gang with territory 2/(n − 1) times an update; it wins with power / (power + the other's) (`AllGangs.ts:73-77`), and the winner takes 0.0001 × max(1, 1 + ln(the power ratio) / ln 50) of territory on average (`Gang.ts:174-178`). In 35% of its clashes each member on Territory Warfare dies with chance 0.005 (won) or 0.01 (lost) over def^0.6, costing 5% of the respect and what it earned (`:281-303`, `:371-388`). A clash lost costs the player's gang 0.8% of its power (`:286`), and another gang 1% of its, to the player's or to another (`:243-260`); the other gangs clash with each other whatever the player's clash chance, and the territory each clash moves goes from the weak to the strong: from 1, the four weak gangs hold no territory about 9 hours on and drop out of the clashes (under a clash's least take, 0.00005, a gang's territory counts as none, as the game takes it in a clash), and the two strong (Speakers for the Dead, The Black Hand), clashing only each other, settle at about 207 power with 42.9% each, where without those losses the strongest would pass 2,500. Territory multiplies every task's respect and money and divides its wanted (`formulas.ts:26-31`, `:44-54`, `:68-73`): ×7 at full territory for most tasks, ×49 for Terrorism's respect.
  - **Territory's worth:** the rise in the members' output over a day (`GANG_WARFARE_HORIZON_MS`, G2's cap; G2's `gangOutputOver`, training then working, in the plan's objective) per unit of territory, of the members not on Territory Warfare (`gangWarfareBasis`). **A death's cost** (`gangDeathCost`): the respect lost, with its toll on the others through the penalty (G2's `gangAscensionCost`), and the member's output over the day less a fresh recruit's, or all of it when the respect left wouldn't recruit one. Each is the member's best over the day (`gangMemberBestOutputOver`, PR #333's second review): as it is, or ascended now where G2's ascension is due, so territory's worth and a death's cost (a death destroys the ascension due) are priced alike; later ascensions in the day are left out of both alike.
  - **Engaged** when the territory the clashes are expected to gain an update, at the powers read, is worth more than the deaths they're expected to cost (`gangWarfareMeasure`), and a disengage's tail too: the chance falls 0.01 an update once off, 49.5 updates of clashes in all (`gangClashTailUpdates`), the crew's deaths in it spread over the day's 4,320 updates, one engagement a day (counted in full, though the crew comes off while the tail runs unpaid: the cautious side). So never while a death is likely to cost more than the clashes gain: a death's cost against the territory's worth in the expected time to one, the same comparison. Not with the clashes expected to lose territory, whatever it's worth.
  - **The crew** (`gangWarfareCrew`): the members by power for the output they'd forgo, the prefix worth most over the day: the measure's net at each sixteenth, territory won late valued for the time left, less the output the crew forgoes. Projected update by update from the figures read: each crew member a slot (PR #333's third review): its member alive with chance e^(−λt), λ its chance to die an update, t the updates engaged, and once dead a recruit in its place (the plan puts it back on), at a recruit's power and death rate; so a slot dies λ × its time on the crew, each death costing the respect toll and the member's own loss once, and the output forgone is the member's × its time alive; the gang's power by the surviving crew's gain and, while engaged, its 0.8% at each clash expected lost (`gangOwnPowerLossPerUpdate`); the others' powers and territories by their pace and their clashes with each other (`gangOthersAfterUpdate`). The deaths expected on the crew in a day engaged are on the dashboard (`GangWarfarePlan.deaths`). (A per-member guard, tried in the second review's round, changed no crew and was taken out.) None when no prefix pays. **While clashes run** (engaged, or a disengage's tail) **and the measure doesn't pay with the crew, there's none**: only a member on Territory Warfare can die (`Gang.ts:294-295`). After G2's ascension (the member ascending is never on it) and before G1's wanted control, which moves the crew to justice last; the order is weighed with the crew wanted control leaves. **A crew member doesn't ascend while engaged** (`gangAscensionToMake`): an ascension would drop its levels to an ascended member's level 1 while it clashes, so its power holds as the rule weighed it; it ascends once off Territory Warfare or disengaged. Left out, each named: Territory Warfare's own experience (it trains defence too), the territory won raising the gang's power gain, and the 1% a gang loses at each clash it loses to the player's, each cautious; the territory the gang takes from the others, left out of their projection, optimistic (it would shrink the weak gangs it comes from sooner), bounded as the measure reads the real territories every plan.
  - **With the brain gone** the crew stays on Territory Warfare through the tail (49.5 updates of clashes): about 1.7 deaths expected for the boosted run's ten at defence 1 (10 × 0.0035 × 49.5; 1.9 for all eleven). The owner's to decide.
  - **Done** by the gang worker after the tasks (`ns.gang.setTerritoryWarfare` and `ns.gang.getAllGangInformation`, 2 GB each: `gang.js` 22.7 GB, with the Agent's 3.0 in BN2's 32 GB home, and within the 32 GB share threads leave free there): the order `{ engage, leastTerritoryPerUpdate }` engages only while the territory expected an update, from the powers the worker reads then, is above the brain's break-even (core's `gangWarfareOrderStands`), else disengages, so a stale order never keeps clashes on; with none (an older brain) the flag is left alone. **When the brain goes** (a disconnect, or a state with no gang, as while it holds), the Agent runs the worker once more with warfare off and nothing else, the tasks left as they are; a brain that gave no order leaves the flag as it was. `getChanceToWinClash` (4 GB) isn't called: core computes it from the powers, checked in the parity suite.
  - **Deaths:** a member on Territory Warfare at one reading gone at the next (`gangDeathsBetween`: only they can die; an owner's rename would count as one), logged and counted on the dashboard. The members read are forgotten when no gang is (none formed, or another BitNode), so a new gang's are never the old one's deaths.
  - **Whether it engages in BN2 (from the model): not in a hacking gang as G2 grows it.** None in the hour's run (the gang's power 1.0 against the others' 3.3 to 37.9). The clashes gain territory from a power of about 11 an hour after the gang forms and 22 six hours on; from about 9 hours on only the two strong gangs hold any, and the break-even is about 270, then 214 from twelve hours on. But a member is worth much (its day's output, its ascension due) and dies often at defence 1, which a hacking gang's training never raises: seven members at hacking 614 (×10 ascended) put none on Territory Warfare, the gang forming, an hour or three hours on. The first member goes on at defence about 2,000 when the gang forms, 3,000 an hour on and 7,000 three hours on (defence counts in power too). A combat gang is unmeasured: its power counts four stats, Terrorism's respect scales with territory squared, and Train Combat trains defence, so its floors would be lower; G1 forms with NiteSec in practice. (An earlier claim that it engages a few hours in came from a test fixture whose members had a ×55 ascension due counted in territory's worth but not in their deaths: retracted.)
  - **Expect none in BN2 for now, augmentations especially:** under 12 members the plan's members train or take respect tasks, which earn no money back, and G2 works them only minutes before each ascension. With 12, on Money Laundering at 1/7 territory, a rootkit (NUKE Rootkit) pays back within 15 minutes once the member earns about $120,000 a second there (hacking about 1,100), and an augmentation only at about $90m a second for Neuralstimulator (hacking about 3 million), $100m for DataJack, $135m for BitWire (hacking about 3.4 and 4.8 million): **member augmentations essentially never pay in BN2 for now.**
- **Recruits:** as many as the respect allows (core's `gangRecruitsAllowed`, the game's `getRecruitsAvailable`, `Gang.ts:325-334`: 3 free, then 5^(n−2) respect for member n+1), named `member-01` to `member-12`, each set to train at once.
- **The gang's faction is left out of the factions joined** that the faction, augmentation and World Daemon rules see (`withoutGangFaction`): the game offers no work or donations there (`Faction/ui/FactionRoot.tsx:97-99`, `:113`; `NetscriptFunctions/Singularity.ts:808-810`, `:934-935`), and its shop is A6's and A8's apart (G5, [The gang's faction's shop](#augmentations-and-the-reset), `gangShopOf`). In BN2 before the gang worker's first reading (a brain just started), every gang faction is left out, so no work goes where the gang may be. Outside BN2 nothing is left out. The reports' donors leave it out too: the slow snapshot carries `gangFaction` (core's `gangFactionOf`), carried forward within the BitNode past an Agent start's first slow snapshot (`GangFactionCarry`), so the gang's faction never shows as "donations unused" or a donations milestone.
- **A member the owner named** past what the protocol takes (over 64 characters) is left as it is: the Agent would refuse the whole state.
- **Fail loudly** (#354, from #352's findings): the run report's section 7 fails each way the gang can stall (below, [the run report](#the-run-report)), and three of them, the silent stalls, are also the dashboard's `gangStalled` alerts: one of the worker's problems in every reading, a member off the rule's task, and the respect flat while the plan has members working, each for a minute (`src/gang-stalls.ts`).
- **Seen:** a `gang:` log line on each change of stage, recruits or task mix, each ascension ("ascending member-02 (multipliers up to ×1.45, 60 respect lost)"), and each purchase ordered ("gang: buying NUKE Rootkit for member-02"), territory warfare engaged ("; territory warfare engaged" on the plan's line) and each death ("gang: member-02 died in a clash, on Territory Warfare"); the dashboard's "Gang" row (the members, the task mix, the respect, the penalty against its bound, the money a second, the ascension being made or the soonest next, "next ascension: member-02's, in about 12 min", and the equipment: "buying … ($…, $… more money by its next ascension)", the best that pays and the hold it waits above, or "no equipment pays back within 15 minutes"; and, G4, the territory and power, warfare on with the territory expected an hour, or off and why ("the clashes would lose territory", "would gain no territory", "its deaths would cost more than the … it'd gain is worth"), with the win chances against the others, how many are on Territory Warfare, and the deaths seen); and the run report's section 7 ([below](#the-run-report)).

### The gang suites

- `test/gang-simulation.test.ts` runs the brain from a BN2.1 start with SF1.3 and SF4.3, as the campaign enters BN2, for one virtual hour (about 13 s of real time), its rules as on Steam and the simulator's own effectors taken away; NiteSec is joined by a harness edit, as A3's backdoor would have it (the gang start suite below runs that path). The gang forms 1 min 25 s in, its worker the `ui` backend's; the first three train 5 minutes, work, and ascend at about 23 minutes (×1.48 each); 14 ascensions in the hour, ×1.39 to ×1.55, none under the floor. At the hour, 7 members (G1's too) and 1,348 respect (G1's 911), the penalty 13.9%. Money is lower than G1's: under 12 members the objective is respect, and the respect tasks the stronger members take (DDoS Attacks, Plant Virus) pay none: $1,092 a second over the hour against G1's $1,828. Money is the respect objective's price, not the ascension rule's. The gang section passes, its ascension check too, and since G5 (#334) the whole report, with no allowance: BN2's `ServerGrowthRate` 0.8 folded in, the batcher's 888 batches stay in step, none short and the money full at all 845 looks after a grow, and home RAM is bought at BN2's price. Its second test drives wanted control end to end: the owner's gang left on DDoS Attacks (wanted 183, respect 3.6: 97.8% above the floor against a bound of 33.3%), then the brain for 10 minutes: all three go to Ethical Hacking, as fewer couldn't bring it back to the bound (3.3) within 23 min; as it falls, two are enough, and the third goes back to training. 78 at the end, against 150 with one on justice, and the report's wanted check passes. **Equipment (G3):** none in the hour, so its respect is G2's, 1,348: the rule finds nothing that pays at the hour's last reading even with no limit on the money (G2 works its members minutes before each ascension, and trains them or keeps them on respect tasks the rest, which earn no money; even on its best money task, Fraud & Counterfeiting, the strongest, hacking 263, would pay a NUKE Rootkit back in 4 h 31 min). Its third test grows the owner's gang for 80 minutes (four rounds of 20 minutes' training and an ascension: ×9.4, hacking 172 after), gives it $10b, then runs the brain for 45 minutes: the members train 40 minutes, two ascend (×1.56), five are recruited, and still nothing pays (the strongest, hacking 1,819, is on Cyberterrorism, which earns no money; even on Money Laundering, Neuralstimulator would gain $2.1b a day against its $9.2b, 107 h to pay back). None is bought, the dashboard says why throughout, and the report's equipment check, on the same readings with $10b free, passes. Its fourth (#330's reviews) sets up a gang of 12 by harness edits (the simulator's `setGangRespect`, 2 million, so the owner recruits all 12 at once: the money objective, as the plan puts a member on a money task only then) and boosts one member (`setGangMemberFigures`: ascension points 4.5e13, 4.5e10 experience, about 88 million hacking; its ascension converts a thousandth more, and a minute's training adds too little, so the plan works it on Money Laundering). With $100b and A6 on (#335's review: Daedalus's count not counting, A6 buys 12 augmentations with a hacking value at NiteSec in the window, and the equipment still comes within 3 minutes), the rule orders for it the five rootkits and three augmentations that raise hacking ($8.6b in all), the worker buys them, and the report counts them, its checks passing: the rule's own purchase, end to end. The other eleven, training, get none. Under 12 the same member trains and gets none (a unit test). **Territory warfare (G4):** none on it in the hour and never engaged, so the hour is G3's (1,348 respect), the report's two warfare checks passing; why, at its last reading: the gang's power 1.0 against the others' 3.3 to 37.9, every member on Territory Warfare would gain 0.0301 an update, under a tenth of The Black Hand's 0.3255, and the clashes would lose 2.75e-4 territory an update. Its fifth sets up a gang of 12 (`setGangRespect`), member-01 boosted as the fourth's, and the other eleven made fighters by `setGangMemberFigures` (strength, dexterity and agility about 70,000, defence 1), then runs the brain for 20 minutes: all eleven go on Territory Warfare at once (they earn nothing, so their deaths cost little, defence 1 and all), the gang's power passes the others' at the first territory update and warfare is engaged from then on (19 min 30 s), territory 14.3% → 16.9%, power 3,059; two fighters die (member-06, and member-09 in the last minutes since #365's review, about 2.3 expected), each recruit trains (a slot's later deaths each cost the respect toll), the others stay, and the report counts the deaths, its warfare checks passing; the dashboard expects about 111 deaths on the ten slots in a day engaged. **BN2's win route (G5, #334):** its sixth forms the gang from a fresh start, then sets NiteSec's reputation to The Red Pill's 2.5m and gives $10b (harness edits: forming zeroes it, and an hour's gang earns its faction about 18): the install is chosen for The Red Pill, seven augmentations are bought from NiteSec in under 4 minutes (ECorp HVMind Implant first, ECorp's own), The Red Pill last, and installed, the gang still there after, the report passing with section 7's route "installed". The six take about 54 s (measured 2026-11-10, after #335's review). **The objective at 12 (#357's (2)), an A/B:** a gang of 12 by harness edits (as G3's, member-01 grown to about 7.7 million hacking, its ascension and training not paying, the eleven others training) with A6 off (an install would zero the faction's reputation), 20 virtual minutes an arm: the planner chooses respect (The Red Pill in 36 min against money's 4.3 h) and NiteSec gains 1,367,540, 1,139.6 a second against its estimate's 1,150.3; the rules' money gains 346,055 (288.4 a second, money's estimate 161.8). About 19 s an arm. **Since #357, the planner's objective at 12:** G3's end-to-end and G4's suites assumed money at 12 (member-01 on Money Laundering); with NiteSec short of The Red Pill the planner chooses respect there first, so they fail for now: how they take the money objective is the owner's call (#357).
- `test/gang-start-simulation.test.ts` (#353, from #352's findings) runs the path the other gang suites' harness edit stands in for, from the same BN2.1 start and the same way. Its edits stand in for the hours before: hacking 201, under avmnite-02h's lowest roll of 202 (202 to 220 at BN2's ×0.8, 2 ports), and the TOR router, BruteSSH.exe and FTPCrack.exe bought with money given for them. Then the brain's own rules: the hack loop roots avmnite-02h and levels to its roll; A3 backdoors it, NiteSec invites, A5 joins, and the gang forms through the gate (its save backup), the run report passing. On seed 1 (the roll 212) the level came 24 min 31 s after the edit and the gang 70 s later, 25 min 41 s in all, before #357; since it (the wait for the gang holds no money back for #343's countables), 21 min 11 s and 22 min 41 s: from the level the path is seconds a step, so the start's cost is the level. Seeds 2, 4 and 6 formed it in 18 min 41 s to 24 min 41 s before #357. Seeds 3 and 5 didn't: #343's install timing installed first, chasing Daedalus's count (no gang yet, so it counts), at hacking 214 of 218 and 216 of 217, and the level started again on re-rolled servers (seed 5's gang, given 90 minutes, formed 78 min in). Since #357 they're CI's A/B for the planner's (1): with the planner (CI runs seeds 1, 3 and 5, bounds 40, 45 and 40 min) seed 3's gang forms 34 min 41 s in and seed 5's 28 min 41 s, no install; with the rules (`bn2Route` "rules", as before) each installs as before, seed 3 at 30 min 26 s (no gang by 90 min), seed 5 at 28 min 26 s (its gang 78 min 6 s in). About 70 s of real time in all, 11 to 18 s an arm; `GANG_START_SEEDS` runs other seeds with the planner. The planner's runs fail on an install before the gang, loudly.
- `test/bn2-end-simulation.test.ts` (#356, from #352's findings) runs BN2's end, which the destroy suites (BN1) and the BN5 path (into BN2, no further) don't: from BN2.1 with SF1.3, SF4.3 and SF5.1, as the campaign's order enters it, the same way. The Red Pill is installed and w0r1d_d43m0n rooted before the Agent (the destroy suite's harness steps), NiteSec joined, hacking at 5,000. The brain forms the gang and holds the destroy short of 15,000 (BN1's 3,000 × BN2's `WorldDaemonDifficulty` 5, read from the static snapshot); past it (a harness edit), its own destroy enters BN2.2 through Singularity (SF2 1 of its 3, owner decision 66), SF2.1 gained, 35 s after the level. The destroy takes the gang and the factions (`PlayerObjectGeneralMethods.ts:157-158`). With the Agent gone and the next one's first moments the brain has no slow snapshot, so the gang rule waits, the BitNode unknown, keeping the first gang's faction and its justice members; the next gang worker reads none, NiteSec not joined, which drops them. The rule's stages are asserted from its own log. NiteSec joined again (a harness edit), the brain forms the second gang with the same `createGang(NiteSec)`, 40 s later, through the gate. The run report passes across both: each BitNode's Source-Files, both forms done, section 7's checks (#354's too), the time to the destroy none in BN2, said once a BitNode, and the report's gang faction carried per BitNode, by `lastNodeReset`: BN2.2's first slow snapshot, before its gang, shows none. About 4 s of real time, one seed. It found one defect, older than it (#365's review): with no slow snapshot, as at every Agent disconnect and reconnect and every brain start, the rule took the BitNode for another and cleared the stored justice members; it now waits, keeping them, and clears them only in a BitNode read as another (`gang.test.ts`, the rule's and the controller's tests). Section 7 names the first gang's formation only and its hours run across both gangs (cosmetic, #366).
- `test/gang-parity.test.ts` checks core's task table against `ns.gang.getTaskStats`, its respect, money, wanted and penalty against `ns.formulas.gang` over gangs and members that differ in every figure, training's experience against the members' `expGain`, the recruit threshold against `respectForNextRecruit`, every BitNode's `GangSoftcap` against the engine's, (G2) core's ascension result against `getAscensionResult` (a first and a second ascension) and the member after it against `ascendMember`'s, and (G3) every upgrade's type, multipliers and discounted cost against the equipment getters, three purchases' multipliers against `purchaseEquipment`'s, one owned refused, and an ascension that loses a rootkit and a weapon and keeps an augmentation; and (G4) an update's power gain with two on Territory Warfare against core's, and core's win chance against `getChanceToWinClash` for each other gang; and (G5, #334) the gang's faction's shop, before and after forming the gang, against Singularity's `getAugmentationsFromFaction` (97 and The Red Pill in BN2), and every multiplier in core's BitNode tables against the engine's, BN2's among them.
- `test/gang-effectors.test.ts` forms the gang through the simulator's effector and through its worker, each refusing what the game refuses, and drives the gang worker (and, G4, the Agent with the brain gone: warfare off on holding and on a disconnect, the tasks left; an older brain's flag left as it was): recruits, tasks, what the game refuses as problems, and its reading in the medium snapshot; its ascensions (G2): made while the order's points stand, not when they don't, and one the game can't make a problem; and its purchases (G3): made while the order stands, and skipped for an item owned (on the next runs), money under `keepMoney` (after another purchase in the same run), a member gone or one that ascended since; with `keepMoney` null, none; and territory warfare (G4): read with each other gang and the win chance against it; engaged while the order stands, disengaged when it's stale (its break-even above what the powers read give) or says off, the flag left alone with none; the clash chance falling once off, and the territory won.
- `test/gang.test.ts` is the rule on snapshots, the gang faction left out (in BN2 only, and held before the first reading), the gate's guard, the reports' donors, the warfare order, figures, deaths and line (G4), and each of the report's failures, the silent stalls (#354) on both sides of each bound, live and in the report. Core's `gang-territory.test.ts` has G4's facts and rule; its `gang.test.ts` has the rule's hysteresis over successive plans.

## Intents

M3's fourth slice ([#141](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/141)): one-shot actions the Agent does at most once, sent in the desired state as `intents` ([protocol](../../docs/bridge/protocol.md#brain--agent), [`packages/ingame`](../ingame/README.md#intents)). Each is an action and its arguments, with an id made from them ("travel(Aevum)"): `buyTor`, `buyProgram`, `upgradeHomeRam`, `travel`, `joinFaction` (#147), `purchaseAugmentation` (#149), `buyServer` (#159), `backdoor` (#162), `installAugmentations` (#164), `destroyBitNode` (#175) and `upgradeServer` (#194) so far. Core validates them (`parseIntentRequest`) and says which are irreversible (`isIrreversibleIntent`).

- **Where they come from,** in this order:
  1. **The owner's manual queue** (`src/manual-intents.ts`), in the order added. It's queued on the dashboard, or with `POST /api/intents`, and kept in the store (`intents.manual`), so it survives restarts of the brain. Any action may be queued, irreversible ones too, with no confirmation (owner decision 36). A queued intent leaves the queue once the connected Agent reports it done, or when it's cancelled. Not when a snapshot shows its result (but for an install or a destroy, below): that can change again (a travel there and back would lose its last leg). Queued twice, it's queued once; one that isn't valid is refused with why; and the queue holds 95 at most, leaving room in the protocol's 100 for the purchase, the backdoor, the join, the augmentation's (#210) and the destroy (#215). The dashboard lists the queue even with no Agent connected, so it can be cancelled.
  1a. **The close of the game at the BitNode's end** (`closeGame`, source `bitNodeEnd`, #303, [above](#stopping-at-the-bitnodes-end)): asked by the switch's "Stop the brain and close the game" alone (the manual queue refuses `closeGame`), after the owner's queue and before the brain's resets, through the gate, after what was queued before it.
  2. **The purchase rules:** [the hacking programs](#the-hacking-programs) and [home RAM](#home-ram), one purchase at a time, the cheaper first; then [cloud servers](#cloud-servers) (#194), while neither is being bought. If the owner queued the same purchase, it goes once, as the owner's.
  3. **The augmentations** (#210, [above](#augmentations-and-the-reset)): a purchase after the purchase rules', and the install right after the owner's queue, through the gate. While an install is chosen, the programs and cloud servers stand down. Then the World Daemon's destroy (#215, [above](#the-world-daemon)), after the install, through the gate, unless the owner queued a destroy.
  4. **The backdoor** for a hacker faction (#195, [above](#backdoors-for-the-hacker-factions)), likewise.
  5. **The join** of a faction that invited the player (#203, [above](#factions-and-their-work)), likewise.
  6. **A travel for an invitation** (`travel`, #228, [above](#invitations-and-daedaluss-count)): Tian Di Hui's, or the chosen city group's, likewise.
- **When they go:** with the hack loop, in the one desired state. With the hack loop off, the manual queue goes before any snapshot: each intent checks the game first, so a stale one does nothing. While the brain holds, none go.
- **Irreversible ones** (R8, #147; so far, joining one of the six city factions, which bans its enemies, installing augmentations, #164, destroying the BitNode, #175, and closing the game, #303), the owner's or a rule's (#269: the brain's join of a city faction, judged after the owner's queue and the brain's destroy and install), go only past a gate (`src/irreversible-gate.ts`):
  1. the guard holds on two consecutive medium snapshots from the connected Agent, received after it was queued: for a join, none of the faction's enemies joined; for an install or a destroy (core's `resetsTheGame`), or the close of the game (#303), nothing queued before it undone (it resets the game, so what was queued before it was for this game; the augmentations queued can't be an install's guard, as no NS call shows them without Singularity); for a destroy, then, w0r1d_d43m0n's preconditions (core's `worldDaemonProblem`): among the medium snapshot's servers (reachable only once The Red Pill is installed), rooted there, and the hacking level at its required level, from the static snapshot (3000 times the BitNode's `WorldDaemonDifficulty`). The game also lets every Black Op done destroy it (`NetscriptFunctions/Singularity.ts:1176-1186`); the guard doesn't count that route, the conservative direction;
  2. then a save backup is asked for ([`backupNow`](#save-backups)), and the intent goes out once that backup succeeds.

  Around that:
  - **One at a time,** in the queue's order: the next one's guard is judged only once the first is done or cancelled, so two that conflict (Sector-12 and Chongqing) never go out together. One no longer first (since #269's review: an intent the owner queued goes ahead of a rule's join, even one out) goes back to counting: it's withdrawn, and goes out again only once first, past its guard and a new backup, never on a stale release or beside the new first.
  - **The guard fails closed:** a medium snapshot without the factions joined, or without the hacking level, or a static one without w0r1d_d43m0n's required level, holds the intent.
  - **Only the backup asked for counts:** a backup asked for before the count started again releases nothing.
  - **Out, it's still watched:** its guard failing withdraws it, and so does staying out 60 medium snapshots (about 10 minutes) without being done; it goes out again past a new backup, so the backup is never older than that when the game acts.
  - **A reset's guard on every update** (#271): nothing queued before it undone reads only the brain's queue, so it's judged on every update, not just on medium snapshots. The brain's own install and destroy come after the owner's queue, so anything the owner queues goes ahead of them: the reset is withdrawn at once, whether it was out or not ("not sent: held: travel(Aevum), queued before it, isn't done"), and once that intent is done or cancelled it needs its guard and a new backup again. Before #271 it stayed out until the next medium snapshot, up to about 10 s, beside the owner's intent, which the reset could then cut short.
  - **A failed backup** starts the count again, needing 6 medium snapshots (a minute) before the next.
  - A disconnect, a new Agent or a restarted brain starts every gate again.
  - A held intent doesn't hold up those queued after it, but for an install (#164) or a destroy (#175): what's queued after it waits until it's done, as it was queued for the game after the reset.
  - Each intent's view says why it isn't sent (`waitingFor`), and the dashboard shows it ("not sent: held: Sector-12's enemy Chongqing is joined", "not sent: waiting for installAugmentations() first: queued after an install, it's for the game after the reset", "not sent: held: hacking level 2500: w0r1d_d43m0n needs 3000"). A brain without save backups never sends one.
  - **Tested** (#269, #271) by `test/irreversible-intents.property.test.ts` (fast-check), with every rule on, and in some runs the brain's own install (#210) or destroy (#215) chosen: whatever the owner queues or cancels, the factions joined and invited, the save backups' answers, the Agent's reports and reconnects, every irreversible intent sent, the owner's, a rule's or the brain's own reset, has its guard holding (a reset's, nothing ahead of it in the queue), leaves its gate only on its own save backup answered saved, and goes out alone. A second test runs it on a fixed seed (271, 300 runs) and checks that what it's for is reached, each count at least about half what that seed gives: the rules' joins out and the owner going ahead of them, the brain's install and destroy out, and a reversible intent of the owner's queued ahead of each of them (#271's path). Each run's store is in memory (#279): on disk, creating it and syncing each snapshot took two thirds of the time, and the floor test timed out at 30 s on a slow CI runner (run 42345801314), the file taking up to 27 s on others; it now takes about 4 s locally, its counts unchanged. The random properties shrink a failure, for at most 20 s (`interruptAfterTimeLimit`); the floor tests report one as found.
- **An install's or a destroy's done check** (#164, #175): it kills the Agent, so the one that sent it never reports it done, and a destroy can't be seen done in the game at all (another can follow). When the brain sends it, it notes the connected Agent's reset time (the slow snapshot's `lastAugReset` for an install, `lastNodeReset` for a destroy, which an install doesn't move); a slow snapshot with another one, from the next Agent, takes it off the queue (another, not only a later one: the game takes it from the clock, which can be set back, #167), so it isn't gated and backed up again for the new game. The note is kept in the store (`intents.resetSent`), so a brain restarted meanwhile still drops it on the reset: a destroy gated again in the next BitNode would wait for its requirements, then destroy that one too. Restarted before the reset, the same game's slow snapshot keeps it, and it's gated again. So that the note is always written, an install or a destroy past its gate goes out only once the connected Agent's slow snapshot has said the reset time ("not sent: waiting for the Agent's slow snapshot (the game's last reset), …").
- **Property tests** (#179, `test/desired-state.property.test.ts`, fast-check, 60 runs, under 1 s, each run on a store in memory, #279): a queue of every action (the joins with enemies and without, both resets), with the rules off or on, and any history after it: the owner queuing and cancelling, medium snapshots whose guards hold or fail, save backups that succeed or fail, the Agent's rounds reporting intents done or not, an install or a destroy done resetting the game (the brain up, or down across it so the next brain meets the reset game; the clock moved on, or set back, #167), slow snapshots with invitations, reconnects and a restarted brain. Every desired state sent is checked: nothing queued after an install or a destroy goes before its reset, nor a rule's irreversible intent, and the reset goes only once what was queued before it is done; never two irreversible intents together; each irreversible intent with its guard holding on the latest medium snapshot since the Agent connected, going out only as its own backup is answered saved; and nothing of the owner's the Agent reported done, or reset, goes again unless queued again. Then a stable game (guards holding, backups saved, every intent done): the owner's queue drains, each intent in it sent. The oracle is the owner's queue as the owner made it, never the brain's. With the rules on, some runs have the brain's own install (#210) or destroy (#215) chosen, and each backup asked for must follow two consecutive medium snapshots with the guard holding, by the model's count. A second test runs it on a fixed seed (179, 100 runs) and checks that what it's for is reached, each count at least about half what that seed gives: resets in the steps (destroys too), with the brain down across them and the clock set back, failed backups that were a gate's own, the rules' own joins out, and the brain's install and destroy out. The Agent runs only what's sent (the intent runner's properties, [`packages/ingame`](../ingame/README.md#intents)), so these hold of what runs in the game. Each rule's examples are `test/desired-state.test.ts`'s; `test/irreversible-intents.property.test.ts` (#269) is the gate's own, with the rules on.
- **How they went:** the Agent reports each intent it holds in the fast snapshot (done, tries, the last problem). The dashboard shows it beside each intent, and an intent tried 5 times without being done raises `intentStuck`: a worker that didn't run counts as a try. The outcome also names the backend the Agent used (`sim`, `singularity` or `ui`, #145), which the dashboard shows ("2 tries via ui: …"): the brain never chooses it.

## Work

M3's eighth slice ([#151](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/151), owner decision 37): the brain's second activity for the Agent, the work the player does (`src/work.ts`, `src/desired-state.ts`).
- **What:** faction work, `{ faction, workType }` (hacking, field or security), or company work ([#153](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/153)), `{ kind: "company", company, field }` (one of the game's companies, and one of its job fields: "Software", "Waiter", …), or a crime ([#156](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/156)), `{ kind: "crime", crime }` (one of the game's 12), or a class ([#158](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/158)), `{ kind: "class", location, classType }` (a university and a course, or a gym and a stat: "str", "def", "dex" or "agi"; `packages/core/src/classes.ts`), set on the dashboard (or `POST /api/work`) and kept in the store (`work.desired`), so it survives restarts of the brain. None set: [the brain's faction work](#factions-and-their-work) goes (#203), unless its switch is off; with neither, no `work` goes, and the Agent leaves the game's work alone.
- **Focus:** worked out by the brain, not set by the owner: focused for full gains, unless they come unfocused too: the Neuroreceptor Management Implant installed, as the connected Agent's slow snapshot says, or a part-time job (#153: Part-time Waiter, Part-time Employee; `Work/CompanyWork.tsx:36`). A class (#158) always goes unfocused: focus changes nothing for it (`Work/Formulas.ts:108-121`). Before that snapshot, focused, unless it's part-time or a class.
- **When it goes:** in the one desired state, once the connected Agent's slow snapshot has said whether the implant is in (so the focus never flips on a connect), and with the hack loop on, once its static and medium snapshots are in too. While the brain holds, none goes, and the Agent leaves the work as it is.
- **How it went:** the Agent's `work` outcome in the fast snapshot (converged, the problem, the backend), shown on the dashboard. Just after a change it may still be the last work's, until the next fast snapshot.
- **Promotions** ([#155](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/155), owner decision 44): the Agent takes any later title of the company work's field the game offers, checking when the work starts and every 10 minutes ([packages/ingame](../ingame/README.md#work)); the brain chooses the company and field, not the title. The outcome's `promotion` (the latest, `{ company, from, to, gameTimeMs }`, read only if well-formed) is shown on the dashboard's Work row ("…: converged via ui; promoted from IT Intern to IT Analyst"), stored as a `workPromotion` event and logged ("the Agent took a promotion at Carmichael Security: IT Intern to IT Analyst"), once: the outcome carries it snapshot after snapshot, so one already stored, after a restart of the brain too, isn't again.

## The faction state

M4 phase A slice A4b ([#197](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/197)): each joined faction's reputation and favour, the outstanding invitations and the queued augmentations, in each slow snapshot's `factions`, as the Agent reads them ([packages/ingame](../ingame/README.md#the-faction-state)). For the faction and augmentation choices ([A5](#factions-and-their-work), A6): `factionStateOf(slow)` in core gives the state only when the reading is well formed and was read; the desired-state controller keeps the latest slow snapshot whole, so it has them.
