- **Unread is loud** (`src/faction-state.ts`), but a single miss isn't a failure: a slow snapshot whose reading has no state, with its problem, or whose `factions` isn't well formed (a bug), is stored as a `factionStateUnread` event (`{ backend, problem }`) and logged ("the faction state couldn't be read (ui): …"), once per change of problem, and `factionStateRead` once it's read again; after a restart of the brain, the last of those stored says where it was. The dashboard raises `factionStateUnread`, and the run report fails, only once 3 slow snapshots in a row are unread, as an intent is stuck only after its tries: a dialog flood or the owner acting mid-read misses one. What isn't unread: a reading skipped (no sidebar on the page, the BitVerse say), one read with the page not put back as found ("read, but the page wasn't put back as found", shown on the dashboard and counted in the report), and an older Agent's slow snapshot, which has no reading (a redeploy's old Agent sends one): "not read by this Agent", information only.
- **The simulator suite** (`test/faction-state.test.ts`): a fresh start with CyberSec joined at 1,000 reputation, Sector-12's invitation ($20m in Sector-12), three augmentations queued (NeuroFlux Governor twice) and BitWire installed, working for CyberSec, 3 virtual minutes: through `sim`, every slow snapshot has CyberSec's reputation rising, its favour, the invitation and the queue, as do the dashboard's status and the run report, which passes; through `singularity` (BN1, SF4.3), the same, from the first slow snapshot; through `ui` on the simulator's page, which renders no sidebar, skipped each time, not a problem, PASS; and through `ui` with its worker deleted from home, unread every time, one event, then the alert and the report's FAIL, never an empty state.

## Safe mode

M3's twelfth slice ([#160](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/160), architecture §5.4): with the brain gone, the Agent keeps converging the hack loop and the work on the last desired state, never its intents, until its `ttlMs` passes ([packages/ingame](../ingame/README.md#safe-mode)).
- **The brain's part:** every state it sends carries `ttlMs`, `SAFE_MODE_TTL_MS` (12 hours, `src/desired-state.ts`), counted from when the Agent was last connected to it. Nothing in a state turns harmful with time alone (a reset, which does, drops it at once), and the loops and the work carry on after it anyway: it bounds how long a stopped brain's choices outlive it, and covers a night or a working day with the brain stopped or crashed for good. While the brain holds, the held state carries it too: it starts nothing new.
- **The dashboard:** nothing new. With the brain down there is no dashboard; back, it sends a fresh state, and the dialogs the Agent handled meanwhile arrive in its first fast snapshot.
- **The chaos suite** (`test/safe-mode.test.ts`), in an 8 GB fresh start in the simulator, the brain stopped with `stop()` (`test/helpers/brain-in-simulation.ts`: its connections closed, and every new one closed as it's made):
  - **The brain down for an hour, with a reload in the middle:** the file holds the hack loop and the work (faction work for Sector-12), never the queued join of CyberSec; a loop killed and the work switched by the owner are put back within a convergence, before and after the reload; the join is never tried while the brain is down (checked every 10 minutes of the Agent's log); the reloaded Agent's clock is the same 12 hours from the disconnect; the brain back tries the join again, with the loops and the work as they were.
  - **Past `ttlMs`** (5 minutes, set directly): a loop killed within it is put back; past it, the state is dropped, the file emptied, and a loop killed stays killed.
  - **A reset with no brain** (a new BitNode, `boot.js` standing in for the restart path): the kept state is dropped, never converged on: no loops, no work.
  - **Deployed again while the brain is down** (a new deployment written by hand, after the owner switched the work): the new deployment's Agent carries on from the file, its own loops in place of the old, the work put back.
  - **A brain that accepts the connection and never answers:** while the Agent waits for its welcome, safe mode goes on, and a killed loop is put back.

## Supervision

The brain keeps the game itself running, for unattended play ([#94](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/94); architecture §10.2, R9). It's process management only: nothing touches the game's code or its save.

- **Only an E0 brain on Windows supervises** (Steam's game). A brain for a source build (E2), or one on another system, never launches or kills anything, and its dashboard shows supervision "off". Tests run the brain as E2 for that reason.
- **Setting up the game? Pause supervision on the dashboard first.** An E0 brain on Windows launches Bitburner within 30 s of starting. It kills and relaunches it if no Agent reports for about 5 minutes. That's always the case before the game's settings are made, before the first `run agent/boot.js`, and on a new save. Resume once the dashboard shows the Agent connected.
- **Every 30 s, unless paused or given up**, it looks, and waits are counted in checks, not by the clock. So the clock stepping (time sync, waking from sleep) can't make a healthy game look silent, or hide a frozen one:
  - **Not running** → launch it through Steam (`cmd /c start "" steam://rungameid/1812820`), as a player does. Cause: `notRunning`.
    - So starting the brain starts the game: after a reboot, `npm run brain` brings both back.
    - **After a launch it waits 10 checks (5 minutes) before doing anything else,** so Steam can start, log in or update the game. If the game still isn't running then, it launches again.
  - **Running, but no new heartbeat for 10 checks in a row** (the fast snapshot, every 2 s; about 5 minutes) → kill it (`taskkill /IM bitburner.exe /T /F`) and launch it. Cause: `silent`.
    - This covers a frozen page, and an Agent that died while the game runs: a reload brings it back through autoexec.
    - A heartbeat counts as new by being a new snapshot, not by its time.
    - At most the progress since the last autosave (60 s by default) is lost.
    - The heartbeat comes even when home is full and no worker can run, so a full home never looks silent.
    - If taskkill reports a failure but the game is gone anyway, it still launches.
  - **Grace:** silence counts only after 10 checks (5 minutes) since the brain started or supervision was resumed. After a launch, the 10 checks start once the launch's wait is over, so the earliest kill is 10 minutes after the launch (a brain restart or a resume in between starts a plain 10-check grace instead). That gives Steam time to start the game, and the Agent time to boot.
  - **Running** is what `tasklist /FI "IMAGENAME eq bitburner.exe" /FO CSV /NH` lists, matched by image name. Its "no tasks" message is in the system's language, so it isn't read. Any `bitburner.exe` counts, so don't run another copy of the game (a non-Steam download, say) alongside.
  - The three programs are run by their full path in System32, never through a shell's parsing.
  - The switch is read again just before anything is killed or launched, so a pause is never late.
- **At most 3 launches in any hour,** the first included. A 4th would mean a crash loop, so the supervisor gives up instead, which raises the dashboard's `supervisionGaveUp` alert.
  - It stays given up, across restarts of the brain, until you resume supervision on the dashboard. Resuming after a give-up starts the count afresh.
  - The count comes from the store, so a brain that keeps restarting can't get round it.
- **A check that fails** (a command that errors or times out after 30 s, or a switch setting it didn't write) is recorded once, when a run of failures starts. Ten failed checks in a row (5 minutes) give up, with the same alert (its text says which cause, with the last failure: #127), so a broken Steam link doesn't fail silently for ever.
- **Paused** (the dashboard's switch): nothing is launched or killed, so you can close the game on purpose, or set it up.
- **Recorded:**
  - each launch is a `relaunch` event, with its cause;
  - a give-up is a `supervisionGaveUp` event, with the cause and the launches in the last hour, or with the failure that kept happening;
  - the start of a run of failed checks is a `supervisionFailed` event, with the failure and the cause it was acting on.
- **Stopping the brain** stops the checks first, so the game isn't relaunched while the brain shuts down.
- **Config:** `steamAppId` (1812820, verified on the reference rig) and `gameExecutable` (`bitburner.exe`).
- **Checked on the reference rig** by M2's owner's check (game closed, Agent killed, paused), recorded on #94. Since owner decision 45 there's no manual check: a live run's relaunches, failed checks and give-ups show in [the run report](#the-run-report).

## The gap report

The evidence for M2's exit criterion, "live snapshots on Steam without gaps" (architecture §13). Since M3, a release's live run is read back with [the run report](#the-run-report) instead ([releasing](../../docs/releasing.md) step 2), whose health section has the same gap check:

```
npm run gaps -- --since 2026-10-27T00:00:00Z --until 2026-10-28T00:00:00Z
```

- **What it lists:** every interval between consecutive fast snapshots (the heartbeat, every 2 s) longer than the threshold, including from the window's start to the first snapshot and from the last one to its end. It also shows the count, the first and last snapshots, and the longest interval.
- **The page heap** over the same window, from the fast snapshots' `pageHeapUsedBytes`: the first and last values, the lowest and highest, and how many snapshots reported one, or "not reported" (E2 has none). Growth would show here first (architecture R10). It's for reading: it doesn't change the exit code.
- **What it proves:** that the Agent was alive. A fast snapshot counts even when its tiers are degraded (`game: null`, [degraded snapshots](../ingame/README.md#snapshots)); whether data arrived shows in each snapshot's `degradedTiers`.
- **Options:**
  - `--threshold-seconds`, default 6: three cadences. Exactly the threshold isn't a gap.
  - Times need a `Z` or an offset (`2026-10-27T00:00:00Z`): without one, JavaScript would read them as local time.
  - A window that ends before it starts (or a `--since` in the future) checks nothing, so it's an error (exit 2), never "no gaps".
  - It opens the store read-only, so it's safe while the brain runs.
  - `--environment`, default `E0`.
  - `--until`, default now.
- **Exit codes:** 0 with no gaps, 1 with gaps, 2 when it couldn't run.

## The run report

What a run did and what went wrong, from the store alone (#185, owner decision 45): the "analyse" step of build, test, run, analyse, in place of a manual smoke test. What each section says, and what a FAIL means, is in [docs/setup.md](../../docs/setup.md#after-a-run-the-run-report).

```
npm run report -- [--data-dir <dir>] [--since <ISO time>] [--until <ISO time>] [--environment E0] [--verbose]
```

- **Summaries by default, `--verbose` for the detail** (#245; `runReport`'s `verbose`): run #3's 12.4 h report was 3,237 lines, about 2,700 of them section 3's one intent, cloud server, augmentation, faction work choice or dialog each, and section 2's inferred target. #245's first round brought it to 846 lines and 85,219 characters, still over a GitHub comment's 65,536 (the release posts it as one, [releasing](../../docs/releasing.md)): the invitations log, the work's changes and the ui backdoors' routine second tries were most of the rest. Now each is summarised a line a cycle, so a run's default report grows with its cycles, not with how busy each was (about 1,100 characters a cycle, 2b's block most of it; the coordinator checks run #3's figure under 60,000); `--verbose` lists each line as before. The summaries:
  - **intents** (`intentLines`): counts by action (the id's name before its arguments) and outcome, the most first ("backdoor: 51 done (36 tried again while still at it)"), then each one not done, done with a problem, or tried again for something besides its worker still at it, in its `--verbose` line. A try that only found its worker still at it ("installing the backdoor on CSEC: about 40 s left", `ingame/src/workers/backdoor-standing.ts:36`; "its worker is still at it"; "its last worker is still running") is routine: a ui backdoor's first try starts it and returns. One tried again with nothing reported is listed, as why isn't known. An install or a destroy not done whose reset followed (#286) is done, counted "1 by the reset that followed, with no done record" and in its `--verbose` line "done: the reset followed, 0 tries; the reset seen by …". The reset kills the worker before it reports: an install's try counts only once its worker returns (`ingame/src/agent/intent-runner.ts:48-51`), which it never does, hence its 0 tries; and a destroy's worker never reports done (`ingame/src/page/destroy-page.ts:110`). Its reset is the one section 4 sees for an install (`AugmentationTimeline.resetSeenBetween`: a slow snapshot with another `lastAugReset`; a destroy's counts too, as it resets the cycle) and section 2 for a destroy (`BitNodeCycles.bitNodeSeenBetween`: another BitNode), after the intent's first record, within `RESET_FOLLOWED_WITHIN_MS` (5 minutes) of its last, and before the same id's next episode. A reset before it, or later, isn't its, nor is the window's first slow snapshot (nothing before it to change from; on either side, #286 review): an install or a destroy first seen before it is not done, "before the window's first slow snapshot: not judged", in the words section 4 uses for an install sent then (`BEFORE_FIRST_SLOW`). The next episode can't start before the reset's slow snapshot is in: the brain sends a reset intent only once the connected Agent's slow snapshot has shown the last reset (`#resetTimeAwaited`, `desired-state.ts:1103-1107`), so a reset is never put down to an install given again by the next Agent;
  - **cloud servers** (`cloudServerLines`): the fleet a line a cycle, its count and RAM before the cycle's first change and after its last, so a change of the fleet's total at most once a cycle ("BN1.1 1, … to …: none → 20 servers, 640 GB (20 bought, 20 upgraded, $35,200,000)"), then the total;
  - **work** (`workLines`): its changes a line a cycle, counted by state ("15 changes: not converged ×7, converged (ui) ×7, not converged (ui)"), and each change with a problem listed ("not converged (ui): a dialog the Agent leaves for the owner is open"). A new work wanted reads "not converged" with no problem until its first try (`ingame/src/agent/work.ts:44`), which is routine;
  - **dialogs** (`dialogLines`): counts by kind, an alert by its first 4 words (`ALERT_FIRST_WORDS`, so 3 Source-File alerts aren't lost among 105 messages), and each left blocking the page (section 4's failures);
  - **backdoors** (`backdoorSummaryLines`): a line a hacker faction's server however many resets: backdoored at the window's start or not, in how many cycles it was backdoored and how long after each cycle's start (the least and the most, and where; `BitNodeCycles.cycleStartAt`), not backdoored since a last reset, and its faction's invitations (run #3's lines were about 2,000 characters each, every reset in full);
  - **factions joined**: each faction's first join counted by its cycle ("11 factions, each first in: at the window's start: 2, BN1.1 1: 9");
  - **section 2's lists** (`describeListChange`): a first → last list of 100 characters or more (the installed augmentations, the purchased servers, the factions, …) as its counts and the names added and gone, six of each at most ("5 → 17 (+ …, and 6 more)", "25 (unchanged)"); shorter, in full;
  - **faction work** (`factionLines`): the choices a line a cycle, counted by work and faction, and the stops by reason;
  - **invitations** (`invitationLines` with a `cycleOf`): a line a cycle, the city group chosen, the travels by city, the holds, and the factions that invited; a hold with no invitation ("NOT INVITED …") or a phase this brain doesn't store is listed under it;
  - **augmentations** (`AugmentationTimeline.summaryLines`): a line a cycle, the set bought with NeuroFlux Governor's levels as one count and their price, the installs chosen and why, the donations by faction ("2 donations: $1,000,000,000 to CyberSec, $2,000,000,000 to NiteSec": the reason chosen can name another faction), and the installs sent; a donation not shown, or an install with no reset, is listed under its cycle all the same. The progress events and the installs seen are left out: 2b says how each cycle went and what it installed (the multipliers gained and the Agent's time away only with `--verbose`);
  - **the hack loop's target:** the batcher's targets from its health in the fast snapshots, each stretch of one, ended by another target, a reset, or no look naming it for more than the heartbeat's 6 s (the same target after one is another stretch); with no batcher health (an older store, or the hack loop alone), the inference, as with `--verbose`. Neither crosses a reset (`resetBetween`): 2b's estimate of the cycle's start on the brain's clock, or a `lastAugReset` between two snapshots' own game times, which no clock offset can move (an install or a destroy puts every server's money back, which run #3's report listed as all ~50 servers at each install).

  The cycles are 2b's (`BitNodeCycles.cycleLabelAt`: "BN1.1 2"; "before BN1.1 1" before the first; "the window" with none seen). **The checks don't change:** sections 4 to 7, every failure and warning line and the verdict are the same with `--verbose` or without (a test asserts it).
- **The window:** `--since` defaults to the newest `brainStarted` event, `--until` to now. A window with the brain's stop at the BitNode's end in it, and no brain start after, ends at the run's last record (#308): the stop, or the close's `gameClosed`, and whatever the shutdown stored after; the first line after the heading says so. Times need a `Z` or an offset, as for the gap report. The newest `brainStarted` includes the brain runner's own restart after a crash, so a run spanning a restart needs `--since`; section 1 notes a `brainStarted` in the 24 h before the window.
- **Over a thinned window** (#283, [thinning](#thinning)): section 1 notes how many fast and medium snapshots in the window are thinned, and up to when. The time checks read every snapshot, so the heartbeat's gaps, clock steps, page stalls, delivery lags (the stubs keep the send stamps, #309), the page heap and the run's end are exact; everything else reads the whole snapshots, one a minute there. So its times there are within a minute, what came and went within one isn't seen (an intent done, a change of work, a dialog left blocking for seconds), and two checks that need readings closer than a minute aren't judged there: a backdoor overdue (it needs medium snapshots within 30 s of each other) and a batcher stuck on a poor target (looks within 6 s). The batcher not running for 3 fast snapshots in a row counts whole ones there, a minute apart. A gap's reload reads the install or destroy the Agent held from the stubs too. The batcher's target goes on across the thinned snapshots, so a stretch isn't cut a minute at a time. A clean run's report over thinned data has the same sections and verdict, and its failures still fail: tests show each, and the differences line by line.
- **What it reads** (nothing new is recorded): the fast, medium, slow and static snapshots and the events in the window (section 5 recomputes the hacking ceiling from them, #196), through `iterateSnapshotsInWindow` and `eventsInWindow`, and the hack loop's and the Augmentations switches (#222). The snapshots are folded one row at a time (a day's are hundreds of MB once parsed), and every read is in one read transaction (`readTransaction`). It opens the store read-only, so it's safe while the brain runs.
- **The checks** (`src/run-report.ts`), each a line under FAIL (or, for a warning, under WARN, #241):
  - **gaps** over 6 s in the fast snapshots (`findGaps`, as `npm run gaps`), from the first fast snapshot to the run's last record (its newest fast snapshot or event): the silence before the Agent first reports, and after the run stops, is shown, not counted. A window with no fast snapshot at all fails. Each gap names the restart events recorded in it (`brainStarted`, `relaunch`, `agentDisconnected`, `agentConnected`, deploys, supervision, `restartFellBack`) and an `installAugmentations` or `destroyBitNode` intent the Agent held at the last fast snapshot before it: a restart is still a FAIL, but a self-explaining one. Except a reload (#240): a gap that follows a `bitNodeDestroySent` or `augmentationInstallSent` event (the brain's or yours) recorded from 10 s before its start (`RELOAD_LEAD_MS`: run #3's destroys went out 4.4 s and 4.7 s before theirs), or a `deployed` one from 1 s before (the old Agent's snapshot in flight; a deploy earlier than that, the gap is a new Agent's death), to a fast snapshot's period and 1 s (3 s, `RELOAD_LAG_MS`) into it, or one at whose start the Agent held an `installAugmentations` or `destroyBitNode` intent (a round later than the lead), is shown, not counted: "gap: … (expected: the game reloading after a destroy sent at …)", "the Agent restarting after a deploy at …", or "the game reloading after the destroyBitNode(2) the Agent held". It must be the reload alone: no `brainStarted`, `relaunch` or `supervisionFailed` in it, an `agentConnected` in it, at most `RELOAD_GAP_BOUND_MS` (1 minute) long, and ending within the heartbeat's threshold (6 s) of the Agent's last connect in it. The game kills every script on an install or a destroy, and the next Agent starts once its Terminal is back (BitNode 6's cinematic adds about 15 s); run #3's were 6.1 s (a deploy), 9.5 s and 13.1 s (destroys). Otherwise it fails, naming the reload and why: "(not expected: …, but a restart in it too: relaunch at …)", "but no Agent connected in it", "but 62 s long, over the 60 s allowed", or "but silent for 10.0 s after the Agent connected at …". One event explains one gap: a second gap after the same destroy is a new Agent's own. A deploy later in a gap doesn't explain it: a reload of the game's own connects RFA again, which may deploy. The events in the 10 s before the window are read for a gap at its start.
  - **clock steps and page stalls** (#241, `src/clock-incidents.ts`), in section 4, from each tier's snapshots' two clocks, the game's time and the brain's receipt. A **clock step**: one clock's interval between two snapshots off the other's by more than `CLOCK_STEP_MIN_MS` (0.5 s), the clock that moved being the one further from a whole number of rounds (a retried medium snapshot is rounds late on both), and that clock, judged on its own intervals, long (or short, stepping back) that once: never short by more than `CLOCK_STEP_STEADY_MS` (0.25 s) the interval before or after, and before a step back never long (a snapshot received late is long and then short), so the two clocks taking a step up in adjacent intervals are both seen (#249's review); in the same interval both are long alike, which reads as a page stall over 2 s and as a round run long under it; the game's clock and the brain's moving alike within `CLOCK_STEP_PAIR_WITHIN_MS` (5 minutes) are one step, each process taking it up at its own moment (V8 and Chromium resync their wall clocks now and then). A **page stall**: two snapshots further apart on both clocks than due, the medium by more than `PAGE_STALL_MEDIUM_MARGIN_MS` (2 s, one round; not over a fast snapshot saying the medium tier was retried), the fast more than `PAGE_STALL_FAST_INTERVAL_MS` (4 s) apart; less any clock's moves seen within the interval; stalls within `PAGE_STALL_SAME_INCIDENT_WITHIN_MS` (a minute) of each other are one, and a medium interval over fast stalls adds nothing to them. "Cause unknown": our own scripts could stall the page too. Neither over a restart event, nor without a game time, nor from or to a fast snapshot whose worker failed (stamped at its round's start, sent at its end). Each tier is read in the store's order, by receipt: the brain's clock stepping back 2 s or more reorders the fast snapshots, so only the medium tier sees a step back of 2 to 10 s. `TOO_MANY_INCIDENTS` (3) or more within `TOO_MANY_INCIDENTS_WITHIN_MS` (an hour) fail (`clock steps, page stalls or delivery lags`), once a stretch (owner decision 55). Run #3 (2026-11-05) had one clock step: Windows stepped the PC's clock +2,678 ms, and the game's process took it up at about 17:11:46, the brain's at about 17:12:08 (#241's correction); no interval of BN1.2 was long on both clocks.
  - **delivery lags** (#309, `src/clock-incidents.ts`), in section 4 with the clock steps and page stalls: a medium or fast snapshot received more than `DELIVERY_LAG_MIN_MS` (0.2 s) after its stamp, the page's `Date.now()` as its worker reported, on the same PC as the brain's receipt: one clock, so it's lag, not a step (about 25 ms otherwise). A lag opens at the first snapshot late and drains at the next fast snapshot on time (the medium one, sent after the fast in its round, drains first and doesn't count), unless one is late again within `DELIVERY_LAG_SAME_WITHIN_MS` (10 s); a restart cuts it. One a clock step's moves fall in is the step's (the brain's clock taking a step before the game's makes snapshots look late until the game's does), and the brain's clock long as a lag starts and short as it drains is the lag's, never a step. A **backlog** (late in more than one round) is an incident: `delivery lag up to 0.6 s from … to … (not a clock step): 62 snapshots received late, drained in 1 min 44 s; …`, with whose it was from the fast snapshots' `sentAtMs` (the Agent's share, its worker's report to its send stamp, and the rest its encoding's, the socket's and the brain's, the stamp being taken before it's encoded; unknown before #309, "no send stamps", or with no fast snapshot late in it, "no fast snapshot late in it to tell"). One already late at the window's first snapshot says so, its drain time "at least". One never drained (the window's end, a restart), its snapshots late by the same amount throughout (within 0.25 s) and by more than `CLOCK_STEP_MIN_MS` (0.5 s), reads "(or a clock step, the brain's alone: steady at about … s, never drained)": the brain's clock stepping on alone, the game's not seen to follow, looks the same; under 0.5 s it's a lag only, a step that small being none. Backlogs count towards the 3 in an hour, and explain a batcher's desyncs as a page stall does only where the send stamps show the page was behind them (the Agent's share over 0.2 s, `holdsThePage`). One they show was the socket's or the brain's, or whose side they can't tell, excuses nothing (P1 of #314's review: every such lag seen was the brain's, which can't move the page's monotonic clock), and the batcher's failure names the last such within a weaken before the stretch: "(a delivery lag of … was the socket's or the brain's, the page sending on time: no excuse, #309)", or "(…, its side unknown, no send stamps showing the page behind it: no excuse, #309)". A **brief** lag (a round's snapshots late once) is only counted, on a line of its own with the worst. The live run of 2026-11-08 (BN1.4) had six backlogs of up to 0.7 s, read then as clock steps, each draining over 1 min 40 s to 3 min, and 69 brief lags: the dashboard's status sorted every fast snapshot in the 440 MB store for the newest (`ORDER BY id DESC` over the tier's rows, 0.65 s, the brain's event loop held meanwhile), polled every 2 s while its tab was open (and, it seems, once a minute on the minute with the tab hidden, as Chrome throttles a hidden tab's timers), with the supervisor's check doing the same every 30 s (0.16 s). The Agent's rounds run a few ms over 2 s, so its snapshots slid into the hold's start and through it, 5 to 7 ms a round: up to 0.65 s late at once, then less each round. The newest-of-a-tier reads now use `snapshots_by_tier_and_id` (#309: the status in 15 ms on a copy of that store, from 640 to 720 ms; the index built in 0.3 s at its first open), and the brain logs a status slower than `SLOW_DASHBOARD_STATUS_MS` (0.2 s) and its event loop held over 0.2 s, a line a minute ([the brain's holds](#the-dashboard)). The run's clarkinc desyncs, inside the 19:36:20 backlog, stay a failure: the lag was the brain's, and their cause is #315.
  - **income stalls:** each stretch of `INCOME_STALLED_AFTER_MS` (10 minutes, `dashboard/alerts.ts`, the same as #184's `incomeStalled` alert) or more, after money first rose, in which the medium snapshots' money didn't rise. A drop (a purchase) isn't a rise. A stretch still going at the last snapshot says so, and one over a heartbeat gap names the gap. While the hack loop's switch (`hackLoop.enabled`, as it is now: the store keeps no history of it) is off, stalls are shown, not counted.
  - **stuck intents:** an intent whose latest outcome in the window is not done after `INTENT_STUCK_AFTER_TRIES` (5) tries or more, as the dashboard's `intentStuck`. Intents are tracked per episode, as the Agent starts a new intent: the same id seen not done after it was done, with fewer tries, back after leaving the list (`ingame/src/agent/intent-runner.ts:7`), or from a new Agent (another session, as after a reset) was given again, and is listed again. So each `installAugmentations()` is its own episode, though the id is the same (#286 review: the exit run's 11 installs had merged into one). An install or a destroy its reset followed is done (#286), so never stuck.
  - **backdoors overdue** (#195): a stretch of `BACKDOOR_OVERDUE_AFTER_MS` (5 minutes, `dashboard/alerts.ts`, the same as the `backdoorOverdue` alert) or more in which the medium snapshots showed one of the hacker factions' servers rooted, at its level (from the newest static snapshot before it) and not backdoored. The brain asks for it with the next medium snapshot, and it takes seconds, so this is the brain's rule or the backdoor failing. One still due at the last medium snapshot says so. A gap in the medium snapshots (over 30 s) ends a stretch: the gap is a failure of its own. With no static snapshot in the window it can't judge any, and says so (give `--since` an Agent's start).
  - **backdoors asked for again** (#195): a `backdoor` intent on one of them whose episode starts while the medium snapshots show that server backdoored. Not one the window's first fast snapshot already holds (asked for before the window), nor one asked for after a reset took the backdoor away, nor one that follows a not-done episode of the same id: an Agent that reconnects, or a reload, forgets its intents, and the brain gives it the same one again, from no tries. The store can't say whose a backdoor was, so the line says it was the brain's rule or the owner's queue.
  - **augmentations not queued** (#210): each `augmentationNotQueued` event, a purchase the Agent reported done that 2 faction state reads since didn't show queued, as the dashboard's alert.
  - **the path to the World Daemon stalled** (#215): each `worldDaemonStalled` event, as the dashboard's alert; with step `daedalusCount` (#228), "Daedalus count stuck": an install while short that added none to the count, passing over one that counts within the money left after the set, or A6's own install with no facts from its commit (#238). An install that added none with nothing that counts passed over is section 3's information, not a failure. A `nextBitNode` stall (#308; only in a store from before #311: the brain no longer emits it) is expected when a stop at the BitNode's end for nothing more to gain follows it in its BitNode, however long after: the stall comes as w0r1d_d43m0n shows, the stop once it's ready. Only a stop in the stall's own BitNode counts. With no such stop (the stop at the BitNode's end off, or the stop didn't fire: the report can't tell, as the switch is a setting, not an event), the brain waits on the owner: once w0r1d_d43m0n has been ready (rooted at the level the newest static snapshot by then gives, in the medium snapshots, unbroken to its BitNode's last) 5 minutes, it's an owner prompt, a warning (WARN); before that, a line. With no static snapshot giving the requirement, the line says readiness can't be told.
  - **invitations not shown** (#228): each `invitationTravel` event of phase `notInvited`, a hold in a city with the money and no invitation for 5 minutes.
  - **installs reported done with no reset** (#213 re-review): each `augmentationInstallNotReset` event, with the faction state's queue then.
  - **donation failed** (#225): each `augmentationDonationFailed` event, a donation reported done that 2 faction state reads since didn't show in its faction's reputation, as the dashboard's alert.
  - **donations unused** (#225): a stretch of 30 minutes online (`DONATIONS_UNUSED_ONLINE_MS`, the slow snapshots' gaps left out) in one cycle in which every slow snapshot shows an augmentation within reach by a donation (a joined faction with donations unlocked sells it; the latest medium snapshot's money covers its price and the donation for its missing reputation), while the brain's progress in force counts none by donation and it buys, donates and chooses to install nothing. Shown, not counted, with the Augmentations switch off now.
  - **install overdue** (#222): a cycle 16 h online (`INSTALL_OVERDUE_ONLINE_MS`, the larger of the window's slow snapshots folded through the rule's `advanceOnlineClock` and the brain's own clock in its `augmentationProgress` events) with no `augmentationInstallChosen` in it, naming its last counts. Shown, not counted, with the Augmentations switch off now, or while The Red Pill's way held the rule at the cycle's last progress.
  - **installs not seen done** (#210): each `augmentationInstallSent` event (the brain's install, or the owner's, as it went out) with no slow snapshot showing another `lastAugReset` within `INSTALL_NOT_RESTARTED_AFTER_MS` (5 minutes, `dashboard/alerts.ts`). One sent less than 5 minutes before the window's end isn't judged, nor one sent before the window's first slow snapshot (nothing to tell another `lastAugReset` from: "install sent at …, before the window's first slow snapshot: not judged", #286 review), nor one called off meanwhile (withdrawn), nor one reported done with no reset (the failure above). A disconnect with no reconnect within 60 s fails on its own, too.
  - **blocking dialogs:** each stretch a `dialogs.blocking` text was shown, in a row, with when it was first and last seen.
  - **disconnects:** an `agentDisconnected` with no `agentConnected` within 60 s. A connect in the same millisecond counts (a replaced session). Not one under 60 s before the window ends (there's no telling yet), nor one that's the run's last record (the run ended there). The line names what was recorded meanwhile, as for gaps, and a destroy or install sent (the brain's or yours), or a deploy, in the 10 s before it (`RELOAD_LEAD_MS`, #240): "; just before it, a destroy sent at …".
  - **cloud server purchases done with a problem** (#194): a `buyServer` or `upgradeServer` the Agent reported done with a problem (bought under another name), as the dashboard's `cloudServerProblem`.
  - **faction work stalled** (#203): a stretch of `FACTION_WORK_STALLED_AFTER_MS` (10 minutes, the same as the `factionWorkStalled` alert) or more, with the brain's choice in force (its latest `factionWorkChosen` event, not ended by a `factionWorkStopped` or a `brainStarted`), in which its faction's reputation rose under half the choice's rate over each 10-minute window of slow snapshots, from the start of the first such window. A gap of over 3 minutes in them (`SLOW_SNAPSHOT_GAP_MS`) starts the readings again, as it's a failure of its own. One still under it at the last slow snapshot says so.
  - **the faction state unread** (#197): each stretch of 3 or more slow snapshots in a row (`FACTION_STATE_UNREAD_AFTER`) whose faction state couldn't be read, whatever each one's reason, with when, how many, the backend and the latest reason. A shorter stretch is listed, "not counted". Skipped readings, ones read with the page not put back, and an older Agent's slow snapshots (no reading) are counted on a line of their own, never a failure; a skipped one doesn't end a stretch.
  - **the batcher not running** (#211): each stretch of 3 fast snapshots in a row or more (`BATCHER_NOT_RUNNING_AFTER`) whose deploy report says the batcher, wanted, isn't running, with the reason.
  - **the batcher out of step** (#206): any desync (out of order, or the target outside its bounds at a hack), late or lost operation in its health over the window, a stretch at a time (#241), each run's own, the incident's span the batcher's (`batcherSpanOf`: the game's clock's move, for a step; #249's review): from its first look out of step to its first look batching with nothing risen, after a desync's recovery (`recovering` seen: since #315 in place, batching on with its hacks held until the target is back); or, with late or lost alone, once the parts in flight at its incident have landed or been found lost (a rise until a weaken and `LANDING_LOST_AFTER_MS` after the incident's end is the stretch's, and moves its batching again later; a move's `preparing` is no recovery; such a rise more than 10 minutes after the incident, once it was batching again, is out of step again). Anything out of step in the 10 minutes after is the same stretch's, and later, another's. **A warning instead of a failure** (owner decision 55): a stretch whose first look lies from a page stall's start, or a delivery lag's the Agent's send stamps show the page was behind (#309; one they show was the socket's or the brain's, or can't tell, excuses nothing, and the failure names the last such within a weaken before: "(a delivery lag of … was the socket's or the brain's, the page sending on time: no excuse, #309)" or "(…, its side unknown, no send stamps showing the page behind it: no excuse, #309)"), or a clock step's (for a batcher before #251, its health without `clock`: one since times its batches on the page's monotonic clock, which a step doesn't move, so a step explains none of its stretches, and they fail as put down to none, saying so) to `INCIDENT_ATTRIBUTION_WEAKENS` (1) weaken times on its target after its end (the weaken at its minimum security, for the player in the newest medium snapshot by then, from the newest static snapshot's figures: `WeakenTimes`; unknown, it's never guessed), an incident explaining only the first such stretch, with the batcher batching again within `BATCHER_INCIDENT_RECOVERY_MS` (10 minutes) of the incident's end and nothing out of step for 10 minutes after (`batcher out of step after a clock step: …`); or, its run's looks ending before either can be seen (the window's end, a restart, a new run), `… recovery not yet confirmed: …`. Otherwise a failure: `batcher out of step after a clock step, not recovered: …`, saying why; and the stretches put down to none in one line, `batcher out of step: …`, with their totals and the first's time and reason, each listed under it. **Its cause, when it's a grow shortfall** (#315): a stretch whose run's looks after a grow found the target below full within `GROW_SHORTFALL_REACH_MS` (5 minutes) before its first look out of step adds "its cause: a grow shortfall, the money below full after N grows from …, the least X% of …'s maximum, each hack taking more than its grow put back, so the batches drifted out of the bounds (#315)", only for a desync (out of bounds or out of order); put down to a clock step, page stall or delivery lag too, it says "a grow shortfall before it too, which may be its cause instead". The rise counts only looks while the run batched that target, and the least is given only where it fell within the reach, on that target (#315's review). The desyncs' line adds the recoveries in place that ran past their time (`recoveriesTimedOut`, a weaken and 10 batches: something kept the target down), each followed by a preparation.
  - **the batcher idle on a move** (#236): from the last hack on the target before to the first on the new one, past `BATCHER_MOVE_IDLE_FAILS_AFTER_MS` (10 minutes), or a move still under way that long at a run's last snapshot, with the targets and the times.
  - **the batcher stuck on a poor target** (#275): a cycle with 2 hours in all on targets the best rooted one would earn 100 times more than, on the hack loop's hosts' RAM, one failure with its longest stretch; elsewhere, a stretch of an hour or more a warning (`stuckTargetLines`, `src/batcher-report.ts`; under 10 minutes off poor targets joins a stretch; each target's income from `hackingCeiling`'s `incomes`, a medium snapshot at a time in `HackingFold`'s `targetIncomes`), with the targets, the cycle, the range of ratios and the best at the largest, and the hacking levels.
  - **the gang** (#325, section 7, in BN2 only): no gang formed with a gang faction joined for 5 minutes (`GANG_FORM_WITHIN_MS`: the gate's 20 s and backup, then a try every 30 s); a recruit the respect allows not made for a minute (`GANG_RECRUIT_WITHIN_MS`: under 30 s twice over); the wanted penalty's share above its floor over the rule's bound (1/N), off course, for 23 min 6 s (`GANG_WANTED_OVER_BOUND_MS`, core's `GANG_WANTED_RECOVERY_MS`): the rule's own condition for more members on justice, a member off justice and the wanted level, with the members' tasks of the reading, not back at the bound within that time (`gangWantedRecovers`). With every member on justice it's all the game allows, however long the decay takes; and the outcome, whatever the forecast: the share over the bound with a member off justice for 34 min 40 s (`GANG_WANTED_OUTCOME_MS`, the recovery time and half again for the forecast's error), so a forecast wrong in a way the rule can't see shows; the gang unread in BN2 for 5 minutes (`GANG_UNREAD_WITHIN_MS`); (G2) an ascension the rule makes (core's `gangAscensionToMake`, the brain's own measure on the same reading) not made for a minute (`GANG_ASCEND_WITHIN_MS`, as a recruit's); and (G3) equipment the rule buys (core's `planGangEquipment` on the same reading, the members' forecasts worked out there, with the money above the reading's `keepMoney`, the holds the worker was given) not bought for a minute (`GANG_EQUIP_WITHIN_MS`); and (G4) territory warfare engaged while the rule's measure (core's `gangWarfareMeasure`, with the members on Territory Warfare as read) says the clashes lose, or off while it says they pay, for a minute (`GANG_WARFARE_WITHIN_MS`, as a recruit's); and its silent stalls (#354, `src/gang-stalls.ts`), on the rule's plan at each reading as the brain has it (its justice members kept as the brain keeps them, from the plan at the reading before, those on justice at the window's first; and bonus time): one of the worker's problems in every reading for a minute (`GANG_PROBLEM_WITHIN_MS`: the worker runs every 10 s and at once on a changed state, so that's six runs refused alike), a member of the rule's off its task for a minute (`GANG_TASK_WITHIN_MS`: its task neither the plan's at the reading before, which the worker had to set since, nor this reading's, so a plan that moves a member back and forth, the worker a reading behind, isn't one; an owner's longer-named member is the owner's), and the respect flat while the plan has members working for a minute (`GANG_RESPECT_FLAT_WITHIN_MS`): risen by less than one of the game's 2 s processes' worth of the plan's tasks (core's `gangRespectPerCycle`, `formulas.ts:15-31`, × 10 cycles, `Gang.ts:99-142`) between readings two processes apart or more, a fall (an ascension's or a death's) not judged. A reading with no gang, or a gap in the readings over 30 s (`MEDIUM_SNAPSHOT_GAP_MS`: a thinned store, a degraded medium tier), ends each stretch, so one reading after a gap is never one. Respect is the gang's faction's only reputation (`Gang.ts:144-155`), so a flat one stalls The Red Pill's route. Since G5 (#334) it names the gang's faction's reputation and favour at the window's last slow snapshot, its reputation by hour (the first and last slow snapshot of each, #335's review: the route's bottleneck), the augmentations bought there (its `augmentationBought` events), and The Red Pill's route (installed, queued, bought, or its reputation and estimate at the cycle's rate, core's `gangRedPillEtaMs`; section 3's path fails the report if it's reached and not installed, or not rising while A6 holds for it). Each stretch with its times and figures. The section also gives when the gang formed and, by hour, its members, respect and rate, money a second, the penalty and its worst share above the floor against the bound then (with the members then), the task mix at the hour's end, the ascensions, each with its largest multiplier gain and the respect it had earned (an ascension is a member's points rising between readings), the equipment bought (an item new in a member's lists, a rootkit again after an ascension too; its cost the discounted one at the reading before), how many, for how much and which, the territory and power at the hour's start and end, the time engaged and the deaths (G4; clashes won and lost aren't read, the game counts neither), and the worker's problems as first seen. **BN2's route** ([#357](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/357)'s first slice): the objective at 12 the brain chose (its `gangObjectiveChosen` events, and the day before the window's last), against core's choice recomputed from the snapshots (`gangObjectiveChoice` on the reading and the slow snapshot of the time, worked out again as either changes; the section's ascension, equipment and warfare checks read that objective too). It fails an objective at 12 without its estimates; one differing from core's for a minute (`GANG_OBJECTIVE_DIFFERS_WITHIN_MS`); none from the brain at 12 members for 10 minutes online, added up across the BitNode (`GANG_OBJECTIVE_NEVER_MS`, each interval between two readings that both show it, up to 3 minutes, as #361's level-only time); and (1)'s, an install chosen in BN2 before the gang (the last gang reading by then none formed) with avmnite-02h's level read by a static snapshot, without the planner's estimates (its `timing`'s `route` "gang forming" and a finite install-now estimate).
  - **batches short of RAM** (#232): a batch with an operation the hosts' RAM couldn't hold (its hack lost), not while sharing on the batches' hosts, with the count and when the first was seen. Each short is listed above it with what held the RAM (#246).
  - **batches left home idle** (#340): with home among the batcher's hosts, 10 minutes or more batching with over half of what home may give the batches (its RAM beyond what it keeps) free, while the plan sent was held to the RAM at every look (the config's `ramBound`, #340's review) or batches were trimmed or short of RAM meanwhile: the batches wanted RAM home had, home listed among the hosts but the plan or the placement leaving it out untrimmed. A plan that leaves home out of the hosts altogether, or keeps too much of it, can't show here (no `home`, or both sides take it off): that's the next failure.
  - **home missing from the batches** (#340's re-review): 10 minutes or more batching with home left out of the batcher's hosts (its health's `home` null, a batcher since #340; an older one says nothing and isn't judged) while home's RAM in the fast snapshot is above its reserve then (what it keeps, with the Singularity worker of the slow snapshot by then, and the batcher's budget), nothing asks the batcher to share (the placeholder may keep home sharing then), and `batcher.home` and the batcher's switch are on as the store has them now. A gap in the fast snapshots or an install starts a stretch afresh. A `home` with a figure not a number of 0 or more isn't read. Idle alone isn't one: a plan bounded by its target's money, not the RAM (core's `batchIncomeMostPerSecond`), leaves home idle that no batch wants. Above it, section 6's home line: what home may give, beyond what it keeps, and what the batches held there on average and at most while batching (none while home was never a host).
  - **batch orders dropped** (#246's verification, #253): an order of a batch that found its batch gone, its hack out before it, so the hack landed without that grow or weaken, with the count and when the first was seen. Since #253 the hack is always its batch's last order out, so one is a bug.
  - **share starved batches**, **share idle** (#224) and **share left home idle** (#242): batches short of RAM while sharing on the batches' hosts (#226's review, #230); asked to share while batching, the room free, and no share threads for 10 minutes; asked, and over half of what home may share free for 5 minutes, with home beside the batcher's hosts (among them, since #340, it batches first and shares as they do, and `batches left home idle` judges it) ([sharing spare RAM](#sharing-spare-ram)).
  - `deployFailed`, `versionDrift` and `supervisionGaveUp` events, and `messageRefused` (#194's review): a brain message the Agent refused as not valid, which leaves it on the last one it took; and `restartFellBack` (#212): a restart after a reset that couldn't type `run /agent/boot.js`, and fell back to a reload or gave up.
  - **hacking income above what all the rooted RAM could earn** (#196), section 5: what hacking earned over the window, more than the hacking ceiling on every rooted server's RAM plus what the game's servers' money fell by ([the hacking ceiling](#the-hacking-ceiling)).
- **Cloud servers** (#194): section 2 gives the hack loop's hosts' RAM, first snapshot → last in each BitNode (`hackLoopHosts`, "12 GB on 2 servers → 76 GB on 3 servers"); section 3 the fleet a line a cycle (#245), or with `--verbose` each cloud server bought or upgraded, when, its RAM and its price (BN1's; unknown in another BitNode), and the total. A cloud server is one a slow snapshot in the window lists (`purchasedServers`); bought is first seen after the window's first medium snapshot, upgraded is seen with more RAM than the one before.
- **Values the snapshots don't have**, or have malformed, read as "unknown".
- **Split at each destroy** (#244, `src/cycle-report.ts`'s `BitNodeCycles`, fed by the same pass over the store: the slow snapshots first, whose `lastNodeReset` and `lastAugReset` say where each BitNode and cycle starts (`ResetDetection`, which [the campaign report](#the-campaign-report) folds them through too), then the medium and fast ones, each put in the one its time falls in). A reset's time is the game's, moved by the brain's receipt less the game's time for its first slow snapshot (taken as the brain's where they're over a day apart, or none), and never after that snapshot nor before the previous one's last. Section 2 opens with a **BitNodes** line: each BitNode the window entered ("BN1.2": its number and level, one more than its Source-File), its start ("before the window" if so), and destroyed after how long (the next one seen), or not destroyed, with how long by the window's last record ("destroy sent at …, the next BitNode not seen" if so). Then its progress, first snapshot → last, a BitNode at a time ("BN1.1, first snapshot → last:"), so a destroy no longer shows as "money: $294,042,984,413,384,600 → $1,262". Snapshots from before the first BitNode's start (an earlier BitNode with no slow snapshot of it in the window) are a block of their own ("before BN1.2's start …"), never mixed into it or its first cycle. Each BitNode's **milestones**, with the time since its start:
  - the first faction joined (the medium snapshots); "before the window" for a BitNode begun before it with an install before it too (its first look's `lastAugReset` isn't its `lastNodeReset`): an install leaves every faction, so the next join isn't the first;
  - each faction's donations unlocked (its favour in the faction state reaching core's `favorToDonate`), dated at the install that raised the favour (the cycle's start), not at the join after it;
  - Daedalus's count reached (`ownedAugs` at `daedalusAugmentationsRequired`);
  - Daedalus joined; "before the window; again +…" where the BitNode began before the window and Daedalus showed favour when first joined in it (it was joined in a cycle before);
  - The Red Pill bought (an `augmentationBought` event, or the queue or `ownedAugs` showing it, whichever is first);
  - destroyed (the next BitNode's start, and the `bitNodeDestroySent` before it, or "no destroy sent seen").

  Otherwise "before the window" for one already so at the BitNode's first look when it began before the window, and "not in the window" for one not reached.
- **The augmentation cycles** (#244), section 2b. First a **summary**, one line a cycle (the issue's table): the cycle ("BN1.1 2"; a BitNode's name is unique in the window, "BN1.4 #2" for a label seen again, as after a replay at the cap, #311, so two BitNodes' cycles never merge), its start, its length, how it ended, Daedalus's count before → after, and the answer that held the most of its time ("reputation 92%"; "—" where none was judged). Then a block per cycle, a BitNode at a time, from its install's `lastAugReset` to the next reset. The block's first line: when it started, how long it lasted (or "so far"), its time online (the slow snapshots folded through the rule's `advanceOnlineClock`, or the brain's own clock in its `augmentationProgress` events, the larger), and how it ended: "an install (count reached)" (the reason of the cycle's last `augmentationInstallChosen` not followed by an `augmentationCycleCalledOff`), "(yours)" (an `augmentationInstallSent` from your queue), "(none chosen in the window)", "a destroy", or "not ended in the window". Then:
  - **installed:** what the next cycle's `ownedAugs` gained (NeuroFlux Governor a level at a time) and Daedalus's count before → after, of the BitNode's required; "none, the BitNode destroyed", or "not yet".
  - **held back by:** shares of the time online judged, "reputation (92% of 50 min online judged), count (8%)". Judged at the cycle's first slow snapshot, every `HELD_BACK_JUDGED_EVERY_MS` (5 minutes) after, and at each slow snapshot where "factions" starts or ends (so it's to the minute), until its install was chosen (the first not called off; one chosen in the day before the window counts, so a window opening while the set is bought doesn't judge the buying). Each judgement stands for the time online to the next. Where the brain's `augmentationProgress` in force says the count isn't what it waits on, its own state: **The Red Pill's way** (`held`: the rule held for Daedalus, its reputation or donation, The Red Pill or the destroy) or **count reached** (a countdown running, or `countHold`, the install held for Daedalus's count). Otherwise `heldBackOf`, with the latest medium snapshot's money: the augmentations worth installing (core's `isWorthInstalling`: any while short of Daedalus's count, only those with a hacking value once it's reached) that a joined faction sells and nobody owns or has queued are counted, the cheapest first with their prerequisites had (passes until none is added; `purchaseSequenceCost` in the game's buy order; the donations one a faction, to the most reputation bought there), three ways: as things are (reputation reached, or a donation's money where the faction's donations are unlocked); with the reputation reached at the factions whose donations are locked (at one unlocked, reputation is money's, so its donation is still paid); and with the money unlimited (how many can be had at all, their prerequisites among them). **reputation** if only the second counts more, **money** if only the third, **reputation and money** if both, **count** if neither (everything is counted, or can't be had for a prerequisite sold only elsewhere: it waits on the bar), and **factions** if none is left at the joined factions (none joined yet, say). NeuroFlux Governor isn't counted: its levels are endless, so more money always buys one more and every cycle would read "money"; the bar with it falls as the bar does. It's a count, not the brain's set (chosen by hacking value), so an estimate, from what the store already has; nothing new is recorded. "not judged" without a faction state (an older Agent) or money; "nothing" for an install chosen at once; "not judged yet" for a cycle going on with no time online between its slow snapshots.
  - **faction work:** each faction the brain worked (`FactionWorkTimeline.readings`; a brain start ends a choice), the reputation it gained between slow snapshots no more than 3 minutes apart, a donation's left out, and that a second; one with a single reading is left out.
  - **favour:** of the joined factions taking donations whose donations aren't unlocked, the one whose favour after the install is most, before → after (the next cycle's, as first seen there, or core's `favorAfterInstall`, "(predicted)"); with all unlocked, "donations unlocked"; at a destroy, "lost with the destroy".
  - **share bonus** (the fast snapshots' `shareBonusOf`, 1 with no share, averaged) and **most money unspent** (the medium snapshots' most, until its install was chosen: the set's buying spends it then); **hacking** first → last, and most; **the batcher's targets**, in order. "not recorded" for a field an older Agent didn't send.
- **The faction state** (#197), in Progress, each BitNode's: each faction's reputation and favour at its first and last reading in the window ("CyberSec: reputation 100 → 1,235, favour 0 (unchanged)"), each invitation with when it was first seen, and the augmentations queued, first reading → last ("none → NeuroFlux Governor ×2, BitWire"); "never read" where none was.
- **Factions and their work** (#203), in section 3: the factions the medium snapshots showed joined, and when ("at the window's start" for those joined before it); the city factions' invitations passed over (seen, not joined then, and not of the city group the brain had chosen), with when each was first seen; the city group the brain chose each cycle, with every group's value, and its travels, holds and invitations (#228, `invitationLines`); the brain's faction work, counted a line a cycle (#245), or with `--verbose` each choice and why ("for Daedalus's count" too), and its ends, in order; and the reputation each faction gained between slow snapshots while the brain's work was on it. Those events aren't listed in section 1.
- **The hack loop's target** is inferred, and says so: the game's servers (not home or purchased ones) whose money or security changed between consecutive medium snapshots, in stretches. Only scripts change them, so the owner's own would show too.
- **The World Daemon** (#215), in section 3 (`src/world-daemon-report.ts`): the path as the window's last snapshots show it, the dashboard's words (Daedalus's requirements, The Red Pill's reputation and ETA, w0r1d_d43m0n's root and hacking requirement with the ETA at the window's experience rate), and each destroy sent, into its BitNode (#321), and ", a replay (the campaign's order has nothing playable left to gain (Source-File 1 at 3, Source-File 2 at 3, Source-File 4 at 3, Source-File 5 at 3), but the run)" with nothing playable left in the campaign's order to gain (#311; #311's own words, "Source-File 1 is at 3, nothing to gain but the run", in a store from before #321); and (#238) each install short of the count that added none, with nothing that counts passed over, as information (`daedalusCountNeuroFluxOnly`). In section 4, each `worldDaemonStalled` event is a failure, but, in a store from before #311 (the brain no longer emits it), the `nextBitNode` stall (#303, #308, [stopping at the BitNode's end](#stopping-at-the-bitnodes-end)): expected when a stop at the BitNode's end for nothing more to gain follows it in its BitNode, else the owner's prompt, a warning once w0r1d_d43m0n has been ready 5 minutes (section 4's list, above). Section 1 ends with the stop, if any, as the run's expected end; a silence after it is no gap, and a close not confirmed is a warning.
- **The augmentations** (#210), in section 3 (`src/augmentation-report.ts`), a line a cycle by default (#245); with `--verbose`, in time order: where the count rule stood each time it changed (#222: "progress: 5 of 8 new augmentations affordable (7 of 12 with NeuroFlux), 1.2 h online this cycle; the bar falls to 7 in 48 min", with the countdown or a hold), each install the brain chose and why (its set and its money, the time online this cycle, and the count reached, a quick install, nothing left or The Red Pill), each purchase (its price and the queue's length), each install sent (the brain's or the owner's, and what was queued), each cycle called off, and each install the slow snapshots show (another `lastAugReset`): what `ownedAugs` gained, NeuroFlux Governor's levels counted, the hacking multipliers gained from core's data, and how long the Agent was away around it. The rule's events are left out of section 1's timeline.
- **The backdoors** (#195), in section 3: for each of the hacker factions' servers, whether it was backdoored at the window's start, when the medium snapshots first showed it backdoored (or not, after a reset), and its faction's invitation: a `gameDialog` event of kind `factionInvitation` naming the faction, or "no invitation from … seen" for one backdoored in the window. A joined faction, or invitations suppressed in the game's options (`Faction/FactionHelpers.tsx:30`), show no dialog.
- **Exit codes:** 0 on PASS or WARN (#241), 1 on FAIL, 2 when it couldn't run (no store, no brain start without `--since`, a window that ends before it starts, or one with nothing recorded).
- **Tests:** `test/run-report.test.ts` builds a store and checks each section, each failure and the command's exit codes and default window, and run #3's clock step (#241: the game's clock and the brain's each +2,678 ms, 20 s apart; 1,615 desyncs; batching again 95 s later) as WARN, the same desyncs with the clocks steady as FAIL, and 3 incidents in an hour as a failure; and (#245) an hour's busy run (375 intents, 10 of them ui backdoors' routine second tries, 50 cloud server changes, 26 changes of work, 62 augmentations bought, 120 dialogs, 30 faction work choices, 11 invitation events) summarised in each block within 200 lines and 14,500 characters (909 lines with `--verbose`), runs of 6 and 12 cycles at two busynesses with each block a line a cycle and the report within 10,000 characters and 1,200 a cycle, the verdict and every check line the same with `--verbose`, the inference across a reset (on either clock) and the batcher's target stretches across a reset or a gap, and section 5's hours held back by reputation, the count and money, or not judged, and the window's alone; and (#286) an install and a destroy shaped as the BN1.3 exit run's (no done record, the Agent away after each, a new Agent after) done by the reset that followed, also with the reset seen exactly 5 minutes after, and an install by a destroy's reset; the exit run's 11 installs, 11 done; two installs, or two destroys, only the first followed by a reset, the second not done (an install at 5 tries stuck); an install the same Agent gives again after it left the list, or a new Agent holds, another episode, a reset after the second not the first's; a 5-try destroy so not stuck; and each not done with no reset after it, the reset before it, over 5 minutes after it, (a destroy) only another `lastAugReset`, or seen before the window's first slow snapshot ("not judged", as section 4 says of an install sent then); and an ordinary intent split only where the Agent's tries start again (a gap in the fast snapshots at 3 then 5 tries one episode, stuck once; a snapshot without it then 0 to 5 tries two, the second stuck); `test/cycle-report.test.ts` checks `heldBackBetween` on an hour across a destroy, factions, reputation and money, a tie, the brain's own state with no money, and each not judged, and `cycleLabelAt`; it writes each run's snapshots in one transaction (`writeTransaction`: one sync to disk, not one a row, which made it 100 s; now about 7 s); `test/cycle-report.test.ts` builds BN1.1's three cycles (held back by reputation, by money, then the bar and The Red Pill's way, an install chosen and called off), a destroy and BN1.2's first, and checks section 2 split at it, the milestones (re-reached after an install too), the summary and each cycle's block, `--since` and `--until` windows, an older store's "not recorded", the edges (an install chosen at once or before the window, a cycle with no time online yet, a countdown, factions to the minute, the work's gaps and a brain start, a destroy's lost favour, snapshots before the first BitNode), and `heldBackOf`'s judgements (donations one a faction, queued augmentations' prices, a prerequisite dearer than what needs it, one that can't be had) (#244); `test/clock-incidents.test.ts` checks finding clock steps and page stalls, delivery lags (#309: the 2026-11-08 run's shape, the brain held 0.65 s of every 2 s, a backlog drained in 3 min 20 s and no step; brief ones, merging, a restart, a step's own, the send stamps' side), too many in an hour, and a weaken's time; `test/telemetry-store.test.ts` that the newest-of-a-tier read sorts nothing, on an older store too, and the slow tier's read past a row is a seek on the same index, and `test/dashboard-server.test.ts` that a slow status is reported (#309); `test/batcher.test.ts` checks each stretch's verdict; `test/batcher-stuck-target.test.ts` checks the stuck target check's thresholds at their edges (100 times, an hour, 2 hours in a cycle, 10 minutes off poor targets), a cycle's poor time added up over stretches each under an hour, a dip, a move off and back or a move to another poor target joining a stretch, an install ending one, the time seen across a gap, an unknown or a stale income, the RAM growing across the line partway, the range of ratios, the hacking levels, and its place in section 6 (#275), and `test/run-report.test.ts` a store shaped like the BN1.3 run's cycles 6 and 7 (phantasy on 26.2 PB, an install, then n00dles at hacking 1,327 as the cloud servers grow from 32 GB to 1 TB and 16 TB each: a warning, then the cycle's failure from the cloud servers' arrival); `test/hacking-ceiling-simulation.test.ts` reads section 5 over 90 virtual minutes ([the ceiling suite](#the-ceiling-suite)); `test/run-report-simulation.test.ts` reads a 20-virtual-minute fresh start's store in the simulator (the hack loop on n00dles, TOR, two programs and home RAM bought) and gets PASS; [the backdoor suite](#the-backdoor-suite) and [the faction suite](#the-faction-suite) read theirs too.

## The campaign report

Clear time, cycles and installs per BitNode, per release, over the whole store: every run and restart in it (#290, architecture §7). It's the campaign's benchmark in place of community numbers, and it compares releases directly ("did v1.0.1 clear BN1.4 faster than v1.0.0?"). What it prints, for the owner, is in [docs/setup.md](../../docs/setup.md#the-campaign-report).

```
npm run campaign -- [--data-dir <dir>] [--environment E0]
```

- **Its own command**, not a flag of `npm run report` (#290's spec): the run report's window, `--verbose` and verdict don't apply to the whole store. It opens the store **read-only** (`TelemetryStore.openReadOnly`, as the run report's command does), so it's safe on the owner's store while the brain runs; a test runs it on a store file marked read-only and checks the file's bytes are unchanged. Exits 0 with the report, 2 when it couldn't run: no store, or no slow snapshot in the environment showing a BitNode (`resetInfo`).
- **What it reads: the slow snapshots and three kinds of event, never a medium or fast snapshot**: `brainStarted`, `augmentationInstallSent` (its `source`) and `bitNodeDestroySent` (`CAMPAIGN_EVENT_KINDS`, through `eventsInWindow`'s `kinds`), over all time, in one read transaction, the slow snapshots folded a row at a time. So however a store is thinned (#283) the report is the same, as long as the events and the slow snapshots are kept whole (the time online is built from the steps between slow snapshots). Of each slow snapshot only its `resetInfo` is read, taken out in the query (`iterateSlowResetInfo`'s `json_extract`, #285), about 525,000 a year at one a minute: a year's synthetic store reports in about 3.3 s, where parsing each slow snapshot whole took 5.2 s before the report had folded any. A slow snapshot with no `resetInfo` to read (or one that isn't an object) is left out, and counted in the header.
- **The campaign itself is `src/campaign-model.ts`** (#285): the fold over the slow snapshots (`CampaignFold`), the endings, and each BitNode's view (`campaignViews`), which [the dashboard's timeline](#the-dashboard) shows too, kept up to date as the page asks. The report prints it.
- **Where each BitNode and cycle starts is the run report's detection** (`ResetDetection` in `src/cycle-report.ts`, #244): another `lastNodeReset` is another BitNode, another `lastAugReset` another cycle, each start by the brain's clock. A label played again (BN1.4 after SF1.3, a new game, or a save loaded) is numbered: "BN1.4 #2".
- **How a BitNode ended**, from the next one's start (`endingOf`):
  - a start before the BitNode's last look (its last slow snapshot's game time) is **a save loaded**, older or newer: never a destroy, and the BitNode it starts has its start unseen (its wall clock and time online are "≥", and it's never a clear of its own);
  - the Source-Files are compared from the BitNode's last look to the next one's first (`sourceFileChange`), leaving out any a `sourceFileOverrides` on either side hides (`ownedSF` shows an overridden one at its override, `NetscriptFunctions.ts:1491-1495`; set in the BitNode's options, `BitNodeUtils.ts:54-62`, `BitNodeAdvancedOptions.tsx`);
  - its Source-File gained a level, and no other changed: **destroyed** (`RedPill.tsx:62-63`, `giveSourceFile`); more than one level, or another Source-File rose too: **destroyed, BitNodes unseen before** the next (BitNodes played that the store didn't see: its destroy's time unknown, so its wall clock and time online go to its last look, the step to the next start isn't counted, and it's never a clear of its own); another rose and its own didn't: **BitNodes unseen before** the next;
  - at its Source-File's cap (level 3, any BitNode but 12, `RedPill.tsx:28`: no level to gain) with a `bitNodeDestroySent`: **destroyed (at the cap)**. Only the last destroy sent in the BitNode counts, sent in its last cycle (after its last install, or its start) and no more than 5 minutes before its last look (`DESTROY_SENT_LEAD_MS`, the World Daemon's stall; #311 review): live, the slow snapshots go on while the worker types the backdoor, and a busy terminal's retry waits 30 s, so a replay's send can come a minute or more before the last look. One with the BitNode going on past 5 minutes didn't happen then;
  - its own Source-File overridden on either side (its level doesn't say): the destroy sent, on the same rule, **destroyed (its Source-File overridden; the destroy sent)**, or else **unknown**;
  - a Source-File lost: **a new game** (a new player's `lastNodeReset` is when it began, `PlayerObject.ts:168`); also with no Source-File to lose, since b1t_flum3 needs one (`Programs.ts:29-33`, `BitNodeUtils.ts:21-25`);
  - otherwise **b1t_flum3**, which enters a BitNode with no Source-File (`RedPill.tsx:62`'s `isFlume`; `Programs.ts:306-322`, `Singularity.ts:1148`), or at the cap a destroy the brain didn't send;
  - the newest BitNode: "destroy sent …, the next BitNode not seen", or "not yet".
- **Time online** is the bars' clock's rule (`advanceOnlineClock`, #222) over a whole BitNode, keyed by its `lastNodeReset` (in the clock's `lastAugReset` field), not one cycle: the game's time between slow snapshots no more than 3 minutes apart (`AUGMENTATION_SLOW_GAP_MS`), from the BitNode's start (its `lastNodeReset`) to its end (the next one's, after a reset), installs and all. **What it leaves out is shown** (the "left out" column, and each Compared entry): an offline gap, or the brain or the Agent away, each step over 3 minutes, and the step from the start to the first look where the store saw the start. The game goes on offline (`engine.tsx:264-271`), so a release away more could look faster online. The wall clock (the brain's, from the start to the next BitNode's) counts it all.
- **Cycles and installs:** each `lastAugReset` a BitNode's slow snapshots show is a cycle, each one that isn't its `lastNodeReset` an install, each counted once. Another cycle whose `lastAugReset` was seen already, or is before the last look, is **a save loaded** (older, or a newer one gone back to), listed, not an install; an install's is the game's time at the install (`PlayerObjectGeneralMethods.ts:126`), after the last look. One edge: the PC's clock set back before an install makes its `lastAugReset` earlier than the last look, so that install reads as a save loaded (and the clock's step back isn't time online). "≥" marks a lower bound: the cycles and installs of a BitNode whose first slow snapshot showed an install already.
- **By hand:** an install with no `augmentationInstallSent` from the brain's own rule (`source` "brain") in the cycle it ended: one from your queue (`source` "manual"), the game's Install button, or a Soft Reset (`ui/GameRoot.tsx:297-301`). Counted in the installs ("3 (1 by hand)"); a BitNode with one is never a clear of its own.
- **A release is the brain's**: the stored release (`<git describe>+<the Agent's deployment>`) before its "+" (`brainReleaseOf`), since the deployment changes with any rebuild. A brain run from an untagged or dirty checkout is its own release (`v1.0.0-4-gabc1234`, `…-dirty`): running from `main` splits a BitNode at every pull, so a clear to compare is a tagged release's. Each step of time online is the release of the slow snapshot that ends it, so the step across a change of release is the new one's (a BitNode's start, its first's; its end, its last's); an install is the release of the slow snapshot before it.
- **What it prints:** a header (the store's first and last record read, the brain starts, the releases); **a row per BitNode** (its start, how it ended, the wall clock, the time online and the time left out to its end, or so far, the cycles, the installs and those by hand, the augmentations installed at its last look, the brain starts in it, its releases), then each one's Source-Files as entered, time online by release, augmentations by name, and saves loaded; **a row per release and BitNode it played** (its time online there, the cycles it had a slow snapshot in, the installs it made, and whether it destroyed it: "a clear of its own" where it played the BitNode, the store seeing it from its start and first cycle to its destroy, with no save loaded in it ("yes, with a save loaded in it") and every install the brain's, with the clear time); and **compared**: each BitNode (number and level) two releases or more cleared on their own, the fastest online first, each with its time left out and the Source-Files it was entered with (the comparison is by label only: the other Source-Files and the BitNode's options aren't matched).
- **Another release only at the start** (`STARTER_ALLOWANCE_MS`, 5 minutes; owner decision 61): a release that played a BitNode only before the one that went on first did, with no install and at most 5 minutes online, leaves the other a clear of its own, its share printed ("a clear of its own (v0.2.0-53-g7c741cc's first 2 min 3 s)", in the Compared entry too). A brain upgraded just after a destroy is the case: the real store's BN1.3 (2 min 3 s of the release before, then 614978a's 12 h 34 min). Any more, and the BitNode is "played with … too".
- **Tests:** `test/campaign-report.test.ts` builds a campaign: BN1.1 begun two days before the store, across a brain restart onto another deployment and a 2-hour offline gap, with an install from your queue; BN1.2 played by two releases, the owner upgrading as an install reloads; BN1.3 and BN1.4 cleared by one; BN1.4 again by another (compared), each destroy at SF1's cap known by its `bitNodeDestroySent`; a third BN1.4 with a save loaded and a destroy sent; and the same campaign thinned (no fast snapshots, a medium one a minute), and the full store thinned by #283's own `thinStore` (its stubs), each of whose reports must be the same text. Then: a fresh save's BitNode from its start; an older BitNode's save loaded; saves within one BitNode and across BitNodes, back to an older one and then to the newer; a release changed mid-cycle; b1t_flum3 (with a destroy sent below the cap), a new game, a BitNode with no Source-File before it, BitNodes unseen (SF1 from none to 3, another Source-File rising), a destroy at the cap sent too early and none after, and BN12's uncapped Source-File; Source-File overrides on another Source-File and on the BitNode's own (with and without a destroy sent); a save loaded in a BitNode stopping a clear; another release at a BitNode's start, within and over the 5 minutes; the brain away across a destroy; installs by hand; a slow snapshot with no `resetInfo`; a BitNode begun before the store; and the command's exit codes and its read-only open.

## The brain against the fork

M4 phase B's exit criterion was the agent at least as far along as the legacy fork at each baseline checkpoint ([ADR 0011](../../docs/decisions/0011-checkpointed-simulator-comparisons.md)), until [owner decision 67](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md) (#337) retired that gate as the progress measure, the brain being ahead by multiples: progress is now the online hours to the destroy for each BitNode ([architecture §13](../../docs/architecture.md#13-milestones)), and this suite stays as a record. [Owner decision 65](../../docs/decisions/owner-decisions.md) says what the gate means (Q10, [#83](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/83)). `test/brain-at-checkpoints-simulation.test.ts` ([#319](https://github.com/UnderMyBed/bitburner-agent/issues/319)) measures the brain there:
- **The start:** exactly the baseline's, `baselineScenario()`'s fixture from `@bitburner-agent/legacy`: BN1 with SF1.1 and SF4.3, which the game makes BN1.2, home at 32 GB, on the same seeds and epoch.
- **The play:** the brain plays with its rules as on Steam (`useFreshStarts`, `withDesiredState`), but through the simulator's own effectors, not the Singularity workers SF4.3 would give it on Steam.
- **The runner** (`test/helpers/checkpoints.ts`, `runToCheckpoints`) reads the simulator's summary, the same one the baseline's checkpoints record, at 4, 8 and 12 virtual hours (`CHECKPOINTS_MS`). It uses the same clock: virtual time since the simulation started.
  - It polls every virtual minute, and a checkpoint takes the first poll at or past it, within one driver step (200 ms).
  - Each checkpoint also keeps the highest hacking level of every poll so far, its peak, for decision 65's gate.
  - Checkpoints that aren't in ascending order fail loudly, and so does a cap before the last checkpoint or a game that doesn't move on.
- **The end:** the first destroy, which is the first poll whose BitNode or Source-Files differ from the start's (the World Daemon rule destroys into BN1, so BN1.2 becomes BN1.3 with SF1.2). Otherwise it ends at a cap, 16 virtual hours by default.
