  - A checkpoint at or after the destroy's poll is recorded as destroyed, and counts as ahead.
  - The destroy's time is that poll's, up to a minute after the game left the BitVerse.
  - A destroy that changes neither the BitNode nor the Source-Files isn't seen: one at the Source-File's cap into the same BitNode, or b1t_flum3 into the same BitNode. Neither can happen from BN1.2 with SF1.1.
- **The output:**
  - rows in [baselines.md](../../docs/sim/baselines.md#the-brain-against-the-fork)'s columns;
  - then each seed against the fork's same seed under decision 65's gate (`compareWithFork`), with `FORK_AT_CHECKPOINTS`: the 5238ab6 baseline run's per-seed values on v26.13.0, from its JSON;
  - then each seed's destroy.

  The heap isn't measured, because the game runs in a child process. A seed whose run was stopped (`partial`) counts at the checkpoints it reached and is named at those it didn't, marked "stopped" in the comparison; speed and real time are over the runs that ended.
- **CI** runs, in about 40 s:
  - the runner's logic on a fake game: a destroy on a checkpoint's own poll, and an install's dip on a checkpoint, whose peak is kept;
  - the rows, the comparison and the knobs;
  - seed 1 from the baseline's start to 1 virtual hour, read at 20, 40 and 60 minutes. Each checkpoint must be the game's own summary, in BN1.2 with SF1.1 and SF4.3, with its peak as every poll's highest.
- **Decision 65's seeds:** seeds 1–2, while the lead is a multiple; more only if a gated measure comes within 25% of the fork's. #319 ran 1–2 to their destroys; 3 and 4 were stopped part-way when the owner stopped the long simulator runs, and their 4 h and 8 h readings are kept.
- **The full run** is a knobbed test rather than a CLI, because the harness is built on Vitest's hooks. It's heavy: on the reference rig, seeds 1 and 2 each took about 1 h 45 min of real time to their destroys (two at once, beside a third run). Most of that went on the hours when the cloud servers hold petabytes.
  - Run one seed a run, each under an external real-time kill. #319's ran under a 3-hour process-tree kill; Vitest's own 48 h test timeout kills nothing.
  - POSIX:
    ```
    BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS=1 BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_SEEDS=1 BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_OUT=<folder> npx vitest run packages/brain/test/brain-at-checkpoints-simulation.test.ts -t "the run, seed" --maxWorkers=1
    ```
  - PowerShell: set `$env:BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS = "1"`, `$env:BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_SEEDS = "1"` and `$env:BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_OUT = "<folder>"`, then run the same `npx vitest run …` line. They stay set for the session, so `Remove-Item Env:BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS*` afterwards.
  - `BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_SEEDS` takes seeds as the baseline's `--seeds` does (`a..b` or `a,b,c`, distinct). `BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_HOURS` sets the cap, 12 to 48 virtual hours. `BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS` set to anything but `1` runs nothing, and says so.
  - The folder is made if need be.
    - Each seed writes its progress lines to `seed-<n>.log` there.
    - Its result goes to `seed-<n>.json`, with the run's date, commit, rig and Node. The result is rewritten at each checkpoint and every 10 virtual minutes, marked `partial` until the run ends, so a run that's stopped keeps what it reached.
  - At the end, it prints the rows, the comparison and the destroys over every result in the folder, and writes them to `table.md` there.
  - `BRAIN_CHECKPOINTS_TABLE=<folder>` prints them again without running anything.
    - A result from before the peak was kept (#319's seeds 1–4) takes its peaks from its `seed-<n>.log` (`peaksFromLog`: the 10-minute progress lines and the checkpoint's own reading, a lower bound).
    - A result from before the run's metadata was kept takes today's date, commit, rig and Node, and the print names those seeds.

## The lockstep driver

`SimulatedGame` (`src/simulated-game.ts`) runs a brain against a simulation ([ADR 0012](../../docs/decisions/0012-simulator-network-in-lockstep.md)). Every brain-plus-simulator test uses it; the live brain never loads it.

```ts
const simulation = await Simulation.start({ …, network: { listenUrls: ["ws://127.0.0.1:12526"] } });
const game = await SimulatedGame.create({ simulation, stepMs: 100 });
const channel = new ControlChannel({ token, clock: game.clock });
game.listen("ws://127.0.0.1:12526", (connection) => channel.accept(connection));
await game.run(60_000);   // virtual ms
await game.runUntil(() => channel.connectedAgent() !== null, 10_000);   // or fail after 10 virtual s
```

- **Each step:**
  1. the brain's queued frames and closes go to the page;
  2. the simulation advances one step;
  3. what the page's sockets did comes back in order, each handed to the brain at its own virtual time, with the brain's timers fired in between;
  4. the brain's clock moves to the simulation's time.
- **Determinism:** the same seed and step size give the same run, however long the brain's work takes in real time. Tests check it with a deliberately slow brain.
  - **The brain's handlers must be synchronous.** A batch of frames is handed over without yielding, so the continuation of an `await` on real I/O would land in whichever step real time put it in. Awaiting only replies that come through the driver is fine, as the deployer does.
- **Closes:**
  - A connection the brain closes stops carrying frames at once, but is reported closed only once the close reaches the page (the next step), as a live socket reports it after the closing handshake.
  - A close by the page is reported at the virtual time it happened.
- **Fail fast:**
  - Steps can't overlap: await each one.
  - A reload can't happen mid-step.
  - A page socket to a URL nothing listens on fails the step, once the whole batch has been handled.
- **The brain's clock** takes delays as Node does: under 1 ms, or not a number, waits 1 ms, so a timer that re-arms itself can't spin.
- **Fix the step size per test:** the brain's replies reach the page at the start of the next step, so the step size is part of what makes a run.
- **Waiting (`runUntil`):** steps until a condition holds, checked before each step, and fails once more than the given virtual time has passed.
- **Faults (`deliveredCopies`):** a test can say how many copies of each frame from the page reach the brain: 0 drops it, 2 duplicates it, both copies back to back.
  - The function is called once per frame, in order, so a seeded one gives a reproducible run.
  - It isn't asked about frames on a connection the brain has already closed: the brain would discard them anyway.
  - An answer that isn't a whole number of copies fails the step.
- **Reloads (`replaceSimulation`):** for a save loaded into a new simulation; start it with the same `network.listenUrls`.
  - Every socket of the old page closes at once, as a reload closes them, and frames still queued for it are dropped.
  - The steps then drive the new simulation, and the brain stays as it was.
  - The brain's clock never goes back: if the save's time is a moment behind it, the clock waits for the reloaded game to catch up.

## The install suite

`test/install-restart.test.ts` (#164, architecture §5.7): installing augmentations and the restart after it, the Agent's side, in the simulator. The game starts with an augmentation queued (`diagnostics.queueAugmentation`, a harness edit) and the brain's desired state; the owner queues the install.

| Scenario | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Reconnecting to the reset game (`sim`) | One backup, then the install; `boot.js`, started 500 ms after the reset, starts the next Agent in a new session; its first slow snapshot shows the reset (the augmentation installed, `lastAugReset` later) and the brain takes the install off its queue with no second backup. A travel queued after the install waited for it, and is tried in the reset game (not done: the new start's money). The new session's medium snapshots show n00dles unrooted by the reset, then rooted again by the hack loop. No third session, and the same reset, 30 s on |
| Through Singularity (SF4.3) | The same through `singularity-install-augmentations.js`, which names `boot.js` for after the reset |
| Nothing queued | Through `sim` and `singularity`: done at the first try, with no reset (a slow snapshot after it shows the same `lastAugReset`), no disconnect and no alert |
| The brain down mid-install | As the old Agent dies, the brain goes (`stop()`: every connection refused) for 30 s; the next Agent, alone, drops the desired state kept on home for safe mode (#160), from before the reset, so nothing runs on the reset game's servers; a new brain with the same store has the queue and the note of the install sent (#175), and drops it on the next Agent's first slow snapshot: never sent again, installed once |
| A save-then-reload just after the install | Before `boot.js`'s 500 ms, the game reloads from a save taken then, as Options' "Force kill all active scripts" does: autoexec brings the Agent back, the brain sees the reset and takes the install off its queue, installed once |
| A reload of the last save from before the install | A plain reload loads the last save, from before the install (nothing saves on install). The next Agent's slow snapshot shows the same reset and the augmentation still queued, so the install stays queued: gated again, a new backup, installed. Three sessions, three backups (two installs' and the reloaded game's Remote File API connection's), installed once |

The `ui` backend's restart (the page timer, the terminal, save-and-reload) is E2's (#167, [`packages/e2`](../e2/README.md#what-the-tests-show)).

## The destroy suite

`test/destroy-restart.test.ts` (#175, architecture §5.7): destroying the BitNode and the restart after it, the Agent's side, in the simulator. The game is brought to The Red Pill's requirements by harness steps: The Red Pill queued (`diagnostics.queueAugmentation`) and installed by an owner's script through the simulator's own install, which links w0r1d_d43m0n; hacking exp for its level (`diagnostics.gainHackingExp`); the port openers bought (the simulator's own effectors) and run on it with NUKE.exe. The owner queues `destroyBitNode { nextBitNode: 2 }`.

| Scenario | What it shows |
|---|---|
| BN1 into BN2 (`sim`) | One backup, then the destroy: the terminal's backdoor on w0r1d_d43m0n, the BitVerse, the portal; `boot.js`, started 500 ms after entering BN2, starts the next Agent in a new session; its first slow snapshot shows BN2, SF1.1 and another `lastNodeReset`, and the brain takes the destroy off its queue with no second backup. One `bitnodeDestroyed`. A travel queued after the destroy waited for it, and is tried in BN2 (not done: the new start's money). No third session, and the same reset, 30 s on |
| Through Singularity (SF4.3, a 64 GB home) | The same through `singularity-destroy-bitnode.js`: SF1.1 raised to SF1.2 |
| The guard | Unrooted, or the level short: held, saying why, with no backup and nothing sent |
| The effector's own refusals | Sent past no gate, through `sim` and `singularity`: before The Red Pill, unrooted, the level short, each in the guard's words, nothing typed, still BN1 |
| The brain down mid-destroy | As the old Agent dies, the brain goes for 30 s; a new brain with the same store (the queue, and the note of the destroy sent) drops it on BN2's first slow snapshot: never sent to the next Agent, destroyed once |
| A save-then-reload just after the destroy | In BN2, before `boot.js`'s 500 ms, the game reloads from a save taken then: autoexec brings the Agent back, the brain takes the destroy off its queue, destroyed once |
| At the campaign order's caps (#311, #321) | The brain's own destroy, its World Daemon on and the stop at the BitNode's end set: BN1.4 (SF1.3, SF4.3 and SF5.3, nothing playable left in the order to gain; The Red Pill installed, w0r1d_d43m0n rooted at its level) destroyed into BN1 past the gate and a backup; the game gives no level (`RedPill.tsx:27-31`), so SF1 stays at 3 and `lastNodeReset` moves; `bitNodeDestroySent` with its `replay` and `skipped`, no `worldDaemonStalled`, the stop's "BN1.4 destroyed, BN1.4 seen again (a replay: …)", and the campaign report's "destroyed (at the cap)" then "BN1.4 #2" |
| Into BN6 | The portal's effect for BitNode 6, its Bladeburner cinematic left as its Continue does: the next Agent starts on the Terminal, in BN6 |
| A save that starts at the BitVerse | The owner's backdoor on w0r1d_d43m0n reaches the BitVerse; the game saved there loads there (no cinematic), and autoexec starts the Agent; the destroy goes out past its gate, and the effector goes straight to the portal: BN2, a third session, destroyed once |

The `ui` backend's destroy (the terminal, the BitVerse page, the restart) is E2's (#177, [`packages/e2`](../e2/README.md#what-the-tests-show)).

## The skeleton suite

`test/skeleton.test.ts` is M2's exit criterion in the simulator (environment E1): "connect, snapshot, reload, reconnect, Agent RAM in an 8 GB fresh start" ([architecture §13](../../docs/architecture.md#13-milestones)). It runs on every PR, in about 25 s in CI, against a budget of 60 s.

- **What runs:** the brain is wired like `main.ts` (`test/helpers/brain-in-simulation.ts`): its control channel, deployer, store and save backups, in `main.ts`'s order (the hack loop aside: it has [its own suite](#the-hack-loop)), with the deployer and the backups sharing the game's RFA connection. The game deploys through its own Remote File API client, and the built Agent and workers play their part. Everything is on the lockstep driver at a fixed 100 ms step. `main.ts`'s own wiring, on real sockets, is tested by `brain-process.test.ts`.
- **The start:** a new game with 8 GB of home RAM, the tutorial skipped, and settings naming the brain's Remote File API and running `agent/boot.js` on autoexec. The brain deploys; the one-time `run agent/boot.js` brings the Agent up.

| Scenario | What it shows |
|---|---|
| 8 GB fresh start | For 10 virtual minutes every tier arrives in the right number, each its period apart. Home never holds more than the Agent and one worker. The store holds every snapshot exactly once, numbered with none missed, and the gap report finds no gap. A save backup is taken beside the deploy, on the same connection |
| Reload | The game's save, loaded into a new simulation, brings the Agent back through autoexec alone: the Agent is a temporary script, so the save doesn't hold it. It's a new session, from the same deployment, and it sends a full snapshot. The reloaded game's RFA client reconnects, nothing is deployed again, and a new backup is taken |
| Reconnect | The brain drops the connection. The same Agent session comes back after its first backoff (1 s) with a full snapshot, its numbering carrying on, and there's no gap |
| The Agent stopped for 30 s | The gap report finds exactly that one gap |
| Faults on the wire | For 5 virtual minutes, snapshot frames are dropped (30%) and duplicated (15%) at random, seeded, and three heartbeats in a row are always dropped. Every snapshot that got through is stored once, and each records how many of its tier were dropped since the one stored before it. The gap report finds the gaps the drops made and no others |
| Determinism | The faulty run, a minute long, run twice with the same seed and step, stores identical rows and events, timestamps included |

- **Home's RAM:** a snapshot worker starts and exits within one timer, so checking between steps would never see it. The suite reads the simulator's `homePeak` instead, which is taken as RAM in use and the script count change. Home must have run exactly two scripts at most (the Agent and a worker, which also shows a worker ran), and used at most the RAM of the Agent plus the largest worker, by the game's own figures.
- **Faults are on snapshot frames from the page only.** Over a WebSocket a frame can't be lost or repeated while the connection stays up, so the faults are there to try the store and the gap report, which only snapshots reach. A lost hello is the hello timeout's case, which the control channel's own tests cover, as they cover a duplicate that arrives late.
- **What it doesn't model:** a reload here loads a save taken that moment; a real one loads the last save, which may be older. The old page's sockets also close before the new Agent says hello. E2's skeleton covers that, with the game's real autosave ([`packages/e2`](../e2/README.md#the-skeleton-on-e2)).

## Layout

| Path | What |
|---|---|
| `src/start.ts`, `src/restart-on-crash.ts` | `npm run brain`: runs `main.ts` and restarts it after a crash |
| `src/main.ts` | The brain's process |
| `src/config.ts` | The data directory and `config.json` |
| `src/telemetry-store.ts` | The store, and its thinning's SQL (#283) |
| `src/store-thinning.ts`, `src/thin-cli.ts` | Thinning the store's old data (#283): by hand (`npm run thin`, a dry run and a backup first), and at the brain's start when `config.json` opts in |
| `src/brain-lock.ts` | The brain's lock file, and whether a brain runs in a data directory (#283's review) |
| `src/data-backup.ts`, `src/data-backup-worker.ts` | The data directory's rotating backup (#284): when, what, the rotation, and the store's copy and check in a worker thread |
| `src/record-channel-events.ts` | The control channel's, the deployer's and the save backups' events into the store, the game's dialogs (#143), promotions (#155) and the faction state unread (#197) the Agent reports included; and `brainChannel`, the control channel with its numbering seeded from the store (#119) |
| `src/faction-state.ts` | The slow snapshot's faction state problem, and its events' kinds (#197) |
| `src/in-game-build.ts` | Reads the in-game build (checked against its manifest) and the pinned definitions |
| `src/gap-report.ts`, `src/gaps-cli.ts` | The gap report and its command (`npm run gaps`); the times, durations and counts both reports print (`describeCounts`, `plural`, #245) |
| `src/run-report.ts`, `src/report-cli.ts` | The run report and its command (`npm run report`, #185; summaries by default, `--verbose` for each line, #245) |
| `src/campaign-report.ts`, `src/campaign-cli.ts` | The campaign report and its command (`npm run campaign`, #290): clear time, cycles and installs per BitNode, per release, over the whole store |
| `src/campaign-model.ts` | The campaign as the store shows it (#285): the fold over the slow snapshots (`CampaignFold`), each BitNode's ending and view (`campaignViews`), and each cycle's time online, install and Daedalus's count at its start; each BitNode's stretches to the destroy and the prior (`destroyStretchesOf`, `destroyPriorOf`, #360); the campaign report's and the dashboard's timeline's |
| `src/destroy-estimate.ts`, `src/destroy-estimate-report.ts` | The time to the destroy (#360): the rule that makes and records the estimate at each moment (`DestroyEstimateRule`, its note `destroyEstimate.last`, its seed from the store `readDestroySeed`); the campaign report's section (the hindcast `DestroyHindcast`, the rows against the outcome) and the run report's check |
| `src/batcher-report.ts` | The run report's section 6, the batcher (#205): its health hour by hour, its moves, its failures, its stretches out of step, each put down to a page stall, a delivery lag the page was behind (#309), or (for a batcher before #251) a clock step, or not (#241), and a batcher stuck on a poor target (#275) |
| `src/brain-holds.ts` | The brain's event loop held (#309): slow dashboard statuses and Node's event-loop delay, a line a minute when anything was held |
| `src/clock-incidents.ts` | Clock steps and page stalls in the snapshots' two clocks, delivery lags on the one (#309), a weaken's time for the batcher's desyncs, and the run report's too-many-in-an-hour failure (#241) |
| `src/simulated-game.ts` | The lockstep driver |
| `src/save-backups.ts` | The save backups |
| `src/dashboard/` | The dashboard: `server.ts` (HTTP), `status.ts` (what it shows), `alerts.ts` (the rules), `timeline.ts` (the timeline of BitNodes and installs, kept up to date, #285), `page.html` (the page) |
| `src/supervision.ts` | The supervision switch, in the store's settings |
| `src/hack-loop.ts` | The hack loop's switch and rules (target and hosts, the move to the next target, #184, and each host's hack threads, #187) |
| `src/hacking-ceiling.ts` | The hacking ceiling over the snapshots, what hacking earned, the dashboard's rows and the run report's section 5 (#196); each target's batcher income on the hosts' RAM, a medium snapshot at a time, for section 6's stuck target check (#275) |
| `src/income-watch.ts` | The money's last rise over the medium snapshots, for `incomeStalled`, and the brain's log lines as a stall starts and ends (#184) |
| `src/programs.ts` | Buying the hacking programs: the switch, the next item, the prices and vendors |
| `src/desired-state.ts` | The controller that sends the hack loop, the intents (purchases, backdoors, joins, the manual queue) and the work (the owner's, or the brain's faction work) as one desired state |
| `src/work.ts` | The work the owner sets (#151, #153, #156, #158): kept in the store, its focus, and the Agent's outcome read, its promotion (#155) included |
| `src/irreversible-gate.ts` | The gate before an irreversible intent (#147, #164, #175): its guard on medium snapshots (a reset's queue guard on every update too, #271), then a save backup |
| `src/manual-intents.ts` | The owner's manual intents queue, and the note of a reset's intent sent (#175), in the store |
| `src/home-ram.ts` | Upgrading home's RAM: the switch and the next upgrade (the price is core's since #260) |
| `src/augmentations.ts` | Augmentations and the reset (#210): the rule, its commit, its events and log lines, the reserve |
| `src/world-daemon.ts`, `src/world-daemon-report.ts` | The World Daemon (#215): the path, the destroy, the stalls, their events and log lines, and what the run report reads |
| `src/augmentation-report.ts` | What the run report reads of augmentations (#210): the installs the slow snapshots show, the rule's events (a line a cycle by default, each with `--verbose`, #245), the failures |
| `src/cycle-report.ts` | Where each BitNode and cycle starts (`ResetDetection`: the run report's and the campaign's, #290, #285), and the run report's BitNodes and augmentation cycles (#244): the window split at each destroy, each BitNode's milestones, section 2b's summary and blocks, and what held each cycle back (`heldBackOf`), and each hour in section 5 (#245) |
| `src/cloud-servers.ts` | Cloud servers (#194): the switch, BN1's price and BN4's (#321), the hack loop's income per GB, the next purchase |
| `src/backdoors.ts` | The backdoors for the hacker factions (#195): the rule, its log lines, and what the run report reads of them |
| `src/factions.ts` | Factions and their work (#203): the joins, the work's choice (core's `factionWorkChoice` since #260), its switch, the stall, the log lines and events, and what the run report reads of them |
| `src/share.ts`, `src/share-report.ts` | Sharing spare RAM (#224): its switch, when the batcher is asked, the reserve, the bonus counted, the dashboard's view, and the run report's lines and failures |
| `src/gang.ts`, `src/gang-report.ts` | The gang in BN2 (#325): the rule's brain side (forming, recruits, ascensions and tasks by core's `planGang`, equipment by `planGangEquipment` within the money the controller leaves, G3; territory warfare's order and deaths, G4), the snapshot's reading checked, the gang faction left out of the factions joined and its shop handed to A6 and A8 apart (`gangShopOf`, G5), the dashboard's line, and the run report's section 7 |
| `src/switches.ts` | The dashboard's on/off switches, as settings in the store |
| `src/supervisor.ts`, `src/windows-game-process.ts` | The supervisor, and the game's process on Windows |
| `src/release.ts` | The brain's release, from `git describe` |
| `test/skeleton.test.ts` | The skeleton suite |
| `test/hack-loop-skeleton.test.ts` | The simulator suite for the hack loop and the intents |
| `test/hack-loop-income.test.ts` | The [income suite](#the-income-suite) (#184, #187) |
| `test/cloud-servers.test.ts`, `test/cloud-servers-income.test.ts` | Cloud servers' rule, and [its simulator suite](#cloud-servers) (#194) |
| `test/hacking-ceiling-simulation.test.ts` | The [ceiling suite](#the-ceiling-suite) (#196) |
| `test/hacking-ceiling-parity.test.ts` | The ceiling's formulas against the game's `ns.formulas.hacking` (#196) |
| `test/augmentations.test.ts`, `test/augmentations-simulation.test.ts` | Augmentations' rule (#210): its inputs, the count rule's clock, countdown and progress (#222), the commit, the phases, the purchases' check against the queue, the reserve and the events; and [the augmentation suite](#the-augmentation-suite) |
| `test/world-daemon.test.ts`, `test/world-daemon-simulation.test.ts` | The World Daemon (#215): the path, the destroy and its hold, the stalls; [its simulator suite](#the-world-daemon-suite) |
| `test/bn4-start-simulation.test.ts` | BN4 (#321): the brain from a BN4.1 start with SF1.3 for a virtual hour, through Singularity; [the BN4 suite](#the-bn4-suite) |
| `test/bn5-start-simulation.test.ts` | BN5 (#323): the brain from a BN5.1 start with SF1.3 and SF4.3 for a virtual hour, through Singularity; [the BN5 suite](#the-bn5-suite) |
| `test/bn5-path-simulation.test.ts` | BN5's path (#348): an install at its ×2 prices, and the destroy at its 4,500 into BN2.1, SF5.1 gained; [the BN5 suite](#the-bn5-suite) |
| `test/gang-start-simulation.test.ts` | BN2's gang, its real start (#353): avmnite-02h backdoored, NiteSec joined and the gang formed by the brain's own rules; [the gang suites](#the-gang-suites) |
| `test/bn2-end-simulation.test.ts` | BN2's end (#356): the destroy at its 15,000 into BN2.2, SF2.1 gained, the gang gone and formed again, the report across both; [the gang suites](#the-gang-suites) |
| `test/backdoors.test.ts`, `test/backdoors-simulation.test.ts` | The backdoors' rule and log, and the [backdoor suite](#the-backdoor-suite) (#195) |
| `test/factions.test.ts`, `test/factions-simulation.test.ts`, `test/faction-work-parity.test.ts` | The joins and the work's rule, log, events, stall and run report timeline; the [faction suite](#the-faction-suite); and core's rate against the engine's `ns.formulas.work.factionGains` (#203) |
| `test/income-watch.test.ts` | The money's last rise, and the log lines, through a real control channel (#184) |
| `test/safe-mode.test.ts` | The simulator chaos suite for safe mode (#160): the brain down for an hour with a reload, expiry, a reset, a new deployment |
| `test/effector-backends.test.ts` | The simulator suite for the Effectors' `sim` and `singularity` backends (#145), and for the `ui` workers that need no page (#159, #194): each action with money and without, and in BN1 with SF4.3. Faction work (#151) in Sector-12: with `sim`, started focused, unfocused, switched, and left alone with none wanted; with `singularity`, started focused, unfocused and switched; and, with `sim`, not converged where a joined faction (CyberSec) doesn't offer it. Company work (#153), a Waiter at Noodle Bar: with `sim`, after a travel to New Tokyo, focused then unfocused, and worked again from Sector-12 as the job is held; not converged from Sector-12 (not in its city), where the player doesn't qualify (Software at MegaCorp), or where the company offers only later jobs (Business at DeltaOne); with `singularity`, from Sector-12 (Singularity doesn't ask the city), started unfocused, then focused, and not qualified likewise. Promotions (#155), an IT Intern at Carmichael Security (`diagnostics.employWithReputation`, hacking 168): with `sim`, from Aevum, the start's check takes nothing without the reputation; given 7,000, nothing 8 minutes on, then the check 10 minutes after the first makes them IT Analyst, the work focused and not restarted (its cycles carry on), the outcome naming the promotion; with `singularity`, nothing at the start, then, qualified, a change of focus checks at once and `applyToCompany` promotes. A Systems Administrator there qualified for Head of Engineering (`gainCharismaExp`, 800,000 reputation) keeps the title through both, the work converged: a promotion stays in the field. Crime (#156): with `sim`, shoplifting focused, unfocused, then mugging, still the work 20 s on (a crime repeats); with `singularity`, unfocused, focused, then a switch to homicide. Classes (#158): with `sim`, Computer Science at Rothman University unfocused, then focused without starting it again, a gym in Aevum and Data Structures with $1,000 held back with their reasons, then Data Structures with the money, kept once the money is gone, then Iron Gym's strength training and Powerhouse Gym's, told apart by the location, as the page can't; with `singularity`, the same course, then Iron Gym's, then Powerhouse Gym's, held back under its $1.44m guard and then started, its focus still set with the money gone, and a gym in another city held back. Each focus change is shown not to start the class again (its game cycles keep counting), and the guard's problems are core's own words (`classStartProblem`). Buying augmentations (#149) from CyberSec, set up with reputation: through each backend, NeuroFlux a level at a time, nothing paid twice, and a level out of turn or no money not done. Joining Sector-12 (#147) through each, past the gate: sent only after two medium snapshots and a save backup; then, with `sim`, Chongqing held by the guard, and CyberSec (no enemies) sent at once but not done without an invitation. Upgrading one (#194), with `sim` and with `upgrade-server.js`: `pserv-0` from 8 GB to 64 GB at the difference in price, $3.08m; sent again, or for less, done with nothing paid; no such server, `n00dles`, and short of the money refused, with nothing paid. Buying a cloud server (#159), with `sim` and with its NS worker (`buy-server.js`, the `ui` backend, the simulator's effectors taken away), on a fresh save's 8 GB home: `pserv-0` bought once and, sent again at 8 or 64 GB, done with nothing paid; `n00dles` refused with no money spent; not enough money refused, and neither bought in the next medium snapshot. At the limit of 25 (the owner's servers, bought before the Agent starts), refused by `sim` and by the worker, which with SF4.3 is still the same worker, reported `ui`. Backdoors (#162), on nectar-net (the owner's NUKE.exe, `gainHackingExp(500)`): with `sim`, the first try says "installing … s left" and a program purchase queued with it "terminal busy"; it's not installed 15 s on, then all are done and the terminal is home; sent again, done at its first try with nothing typed. The refusals (no root, the level, home, a cloud server, no server), each with its reason and nothing typed. With `singularity` (SF4.3), installed through its worker, the terminal left home, and the same refusals; and on a 16 GB home, while its worker holds 8 GB, crime work uses the page rather than a Singularity worker that couldn't start, then Singularity once the backdoor is in |

| `test/destroy-restart.test.ts` | The [destroy suite](#the-destroy-suite) (#175) |
